# ASSURANCE-01 repository-wide audit

## Scope and evidence

This is the working investigation record for `ASSURANCE-01`. Its starting source is PR #302 at commit `42d02c5a38ecffaba59e58d5d56ba0ab858eda1f`. The audit covers the entire repository: product code, tests, fixtures, scripts, configuration, documentation checks, packaging, and CI. It is **incomplete**; the entries below are discoveries from successive source passes, not a conclusion that other areas are clear.

The [backlog matrix](backlog_matrix.md) owns the accepted outcome and task status. This document records evidence and questions. It authorizes no implementation, protection removal, coverage change, or evidence deletion. `QUAL-01` owns measured test cost; `HARNESS-01` owns simulated-science harness choices; `REPORT-ROSTER-01` owns the reporting roster decision.

All `path:line` references below refer to the pinned commit. **Source reviewed** means code or documentation was inspected, without execution in this audit. **Test characterized** means a committed test explicitly asserts the behavior; that test was not rerun for this record. **Inference** identifies a conclusion still needing a focused reproduction. No product or repository test suite, institutional Slurm run, scientific review, or biological validation was performed for this pass. Focused helper and tiny owner-path reproductions are identified in their entries; they do not execute a full Run or report transaction.

## Coverage ledger for the pinned tree

The pinned commit contains 575 tracked files. The counts below inventory every top-level surface; "sampled" means specific paths were inspected for the entries in this document, not that every file in a group has been cleared. A file-by-file disposition, distinct-fault map, and any proposal to retire protection remain open.

| Surface | Tracked files | This pass | Remaining review |
|---|---:|---|---|
| `src/emrys/` | 315 | Sampled stage, analysis, evidence, ingestion, reporting, contract, library, workflow, and coordinator boundaries | Trace every candidate's callers and complete owner-level contract and test comparison |
| `src/README.md` | 1 | Read the source-tree owner pointer and installed-command boundary | Recheck links and wording when the package layout changes |
| `tests/` | 186 | Sampled direct owners, fixtures, independent oracles, CI tests, and shared tooling | Map distinct failures, retained evidence, and measured cost before any test reduction |
| `docs/` | 35 | Routed all pinned paths by reader/authority role; sampled architecture, task, operator, design, and owner-contract claims | Reconcile every affected claim and documentation gate with its source authority |
| `configs/` | 11 | Compared profiles, live selector/template callers, and a specialist pairing example | Confirm remaining operator uses before any example retirement |
| `scripts/` | 6 | Traced all six to supported operator, Make, or CI routes; compared documentation, quality, sharding, and benchmark boundaries | Complete distinct-fault comparisons; no whole entrypoint is cleared for retirement |
| `.github/` | 3 | Mapped workflow triggers, selected job routes, source tests, and both evidence-scope READMEs | Effective hosted merge rules and observed outcomes remain separate; no CI is started under the current instruction |
| Other tracked paths | 18 | Mapped all 18 to tooling, governance/onboarding, licensing, or packaging/lock roles | Complete caller and fault comparisons before any root/support reduction |

The `tests/` inventory includes 100 files named `test_*` (88 Python, 10 shell, 2 R). This record has explicit `path:line` citations to 435 of 575 tracked pinned paths, including 139 of 186 under `tests/` (all 100 named test files) and 247 of 315 under `src/emrys/`. The other 68 source paths comprise 38 Markdown owner/resource guides and 30 docstring-only package `__init__.py` files; this is a citation inventory, not clearance of those files. The 140 uncited paths comprise those 68 source paths, 47 test-support paths, 16 documentation paths, and nine other paths. They still require the owner/caller decisions described below, even where they were inventoried or routed. These counts match unique pinned paths followed by line references in this document; they are not counts of test cases or source owners reviewed, executed, or cleared. The pinned tree also defines 22 named Make targets (`Makefile:17-23`; `scripts/make_quality.mk:73-239`), three local pre-commit hooks (`.pre-commit-config.yaml:1-21`), and one CI workflow with 13 job definitions (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:1-78`). A protection denominator cannot yet be stated: one file may contain several independent fault checks, and product guards and retained evidence sit outside the test tree.

The other 86 tracked `tests/` files comprise 49 owner READMEs, 17 Python support files, 11 JSON fixtures, five shell helpers, three TSV fixtures, and one digest golden. A caller pass identified A01-169/171 as bounded test-support candidates and A01-170 as two possible redundant golden self-tests; the remaining support files have no blanket clearance from this inventory.

The 49 test/fixture READMEs were read for owner routes and evidence limits; historical fixture-directory names are explicitly contextualized rather than evidence that the current schemas or fixtures are obsolete. The five shell helpers retain guarded R, selected synthetic Slurm setup, or operator validation routes; the Python support and JSON/TSV/digest data remain called by owner suites, Make, or CI. A01-94 adds a narrow shard-receipt metadata ceiling, without weakening its exact node-selection proof. The independent Step 09 CMH oracle derives expected counts separately from the R producer (`tests/analyses/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking/step_09_cmh_oracle.py:1-294`; `tests/analyses/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking/test_step_09_cmh_oracle.py:13-174`), so its corpus and tests are not interchangeable with producer-coupled synthetic builders. These are source/caller dispositions, not an executed test result or whole-file deletion authority.

The 18 root/other paths divide into six development-tooling files (`.coveragerc`, `.pre-commit-config.yaml`, `.shellcheckrc`, `Makefile`, `.gitignore`, `.env.example`), five governance/onboarding paths (`AGENTS.md`, `README.md`, `quickstart.md`, `Projects/README.md`, `Projects/.gitkeep`), four license/notice paths (`LICENSE`, `NOTICE`, `LICENSES/renv-MIT.txt`, `LICENSES/README.md`), and three package/lock paths (`pyproject.toml`, `setup.py`, `uv.lock`). The license texts and wheel metadata have distinct distribution roles (`tests/test_package_distribution.py:243-247`); A01-74 covers the stale settings example and A01-172 the directory marker. The 35 pinned `docs/` paths route through the architecture, design, operations, reference, task, and history indexes (`docs/README.md:6-29`; `docs/architecture/README.md:6-16`; `docs/design/DECISIONS.md:3-18`; `docs/operations/README.md:3-18`; `docs/tasks/README.md:3-20`; `docs/history/README.md:3-25`); their reader routes do not establish prose truth or current external use.

The 42 package `__init__.py` files include three substantial owner initializers already traced and 39 further paths: 33 docstring-only package markers (including the deliberately import-light coordinator marker, `src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/__init__.py:1-8`) and six executable metadata/export files. Non-namespace package discovery (`pyproject.toml:66-74`) makes marker deletion a packaging decision, not automatic compression. The root initializer supplies the installed version (`src/emrys/__init__.py:1-3`; `pyproject.toml:123-124`); logging, quality, and validation facades serve owner imports (`src/emrys/libraries/application_logging/__init__.py:3-43`; `src/emrys/libraries/quality/__init__.py:3-5`; `src/emrys/libraries/validation/__init__.py:3-60`); the scientific reporter facade is an entry point (`src/emrys/reporting/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking_report/__init__.py:3-5`; `pyproject.toml:45-46`). A01-180 covers the narrower orchestration/validation re-export question. The remaining package-local `renv/.gitignore` lists generated environment directories (`src/emrys/renv/.gitignore:1-8`), while root ignores overlap (`.gitignore:36-44`) and renv metadata explicitly manages local ignore rules (`src/emrys/renv/README.md:3-6`; `src/emrys/renv/settings.json:16-20`). This first trace does not clear the nested ignore file for retirement.

A separate reader-route pass checked all 46 previously uncited source Markdown guides for local targets, heading anchors, and documented grouped validation commands against the pinned CLI (`src/emrys/__main__.py:44-64`); it found no whole-guide orphan. A01-179 narrows the version-guide consolidation question without claiming that other prose is current or unnecessary. The 14 previously uncited orchestration JSON schemas are all registered in the closed 16-schema owner map and selected as wheel resources (`src/emrys/contracts/orchestration/api.py:22-73,208-249`; `tests/test_package_distribution.py:61-79`). A01-177/178 record one pre-entry admission gap and one repeated item definition within that active set; no schema file is cleared for deletion. The two GitHub READMEs explain the workflow's software-evidence ceiling and selected Slurm prerequisites (`.github/README.md:1-5`; `.github/workflows/README.md:1-21`); their prose and the workflow source do not establish a hosted result or Viking qualification.

### Owner-by-owner review ledger, first trace

These rows trace a supported route, a surviving defense, and its evidence ceiling at the pinned commit. They are **provisional source dispositions**, not file-by-file clearance, a test run, or authority to remove a check. Steps 02b and 03 are evidence owners in the Run's processing graph; they appear with the stage sequence so the worker-to-validator route stays visible. Finding IDs link to detailed exceptions elsewhere in this record.

| Processing owner | Route and surviving defense | Limit and disposition |
|---|---|---|
| Step 00a, STAR index | Materialization declares the reference/index inputs; the worker requires 15 index members, and the validator checks parameters and FASTA contig order (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/materialization.py:381-420`; `src/emrys/stages/star_index/step_00a_build_star_index.sh:66-96`; `src/emrys/stages/star_index/validator.py:113-189`; `tests/stages/star_index/test_validate_step_00a_star_index.py:153-214`). | A01-108 concerns optional missing-member provenance; A01-185 records fake-worker reference-path binding; A01-237 records the compound negative fixture's selective-check limit. Fake STAR tests do not prove native index usability; keep worker and validator boundaries. |
| Step 00b, GTF to BED12 | The converter normalizes records and names; the validator compares sorted BED rows with the GTF-derived view (`src/emrys/stages/gtf_to_bed12/converter.py:115-201,227-290`; `src/emrys/stages/gtf_to_bed12/validator.py:46-95`; `tests/stages/gtf_to_bed12/test_gtf_to_bed12.py:65-107,209-321`). | A01-23/42/78 identify shared normalization and supported-input gaps; A01-237 records the compound negative fixture's selective-check limit. No row in this first trace clears a product file, test, script, schema, configuration entry, or gate for retirement. Complete caller and fault comparisons remain required for every row; documentation, fixtures, scripts, configuration, packaging, and CI remain repository-wide review surfaces beyond these production-owner traces.

The reporting-start and verified-reporting schemas have similar shapes but protect different ledger transitions. The start record binds the origin and lock, while the verified record binds the start and semantic receipt (`src/emrys/contracts/schemas/orchestration/v1/reporting_start.schema.json:7-18,34-47`; `src/emrys/contracts/schemas/orchestration/v1/verified_reporting.schema.json:7-18,34-47`; `src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/reporting_boundary.py:734-755,949-1026`). Direct ledger cases exercise both phases (`tests/contracts/orchestration/test_reporting_ledger_contracts.py:21-61`). Their similar field scaffolding alone does not establish a shared policy or justify removing either record.

The remaining six orchestration schema leaves also have active roles. `common` supplies shared identifiers and the distinct general/success command shapes (`src/emrys/contracts/schemas/orchestration/v1/common.schema.json:114-155`); `project` admits both the built-in flat analysis and module form, and shares reference STAR settings (`src/emrys/contracts/schemas/orchestration/v1/project.schema.json:37-46,60-132`; `src/emrys/contracts/schemas/orchestration/v1/reference.schema.json:27-49`; `src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/normalization.py:596-638`). `resource-config` admits selected fragments before defaults merge and complete policy admission (`src/emrys/contracts/schemas/orchestration/v3/resource_config.schema.json:3-9`; `src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/execution_profile.py:399-415`). `task-start` binds owner entry and inputs, while `verified-task` binds a later Task Attempt reference (`src/emrys/contracts/schemas/orchestration/v1/task_start.schema.json:7-20,50-65`; `src/emrys/contracts/schemas/orchestration/v1/verified_task.schema.json:7-17`; `src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/task.py:2650-2665,2885-2920`). All six are registered and packaged (`src/emrys/contracts/orchestration/api.py:23-55`; `tests/test_package_distribution.py:61-79`). The two common command definitions share argv grammar but differ on permitted exits; that overlap alone does not establish a worthwhile reduction. No whole schema is cleared for retirement.

### Focused owner fault comparison, second pass

This pass compares tests by the failure they would detect, using committed test source without rerunning the suites. Similar successful output assertions are not interchangeable when one checks creation and another checks retained re-admission.

A01-196 adds a separate Step 06 direct-report/index parity question. A01-118 adds an uncovered post-check selector mutation; A01-191 finds two producer command expectations coupled to its own constants. A01-27/76/77 retain distinct Step 06 count/fraction limits; none proves allele or biological meaning. |

A01-209 isolates one repeated Python summary-failure case; A01-210 isolates R fixture cleanup, not scientific test retirement. A01-117 is a newly reproduced direct-validator interval absent from both; normal Run's stable-input recheck is another boundary. |

A01-130 limits the negative R fixture's cause attribution; A01-129's Task selector binding is a separate continuity issue. This is source and test characterization, not a fresh R run or evidence that every malformed VCF is caught. |

Retain the independent numeric oracles and transaction checks; neither establishes biological editing. |

Keep the ordinary failure and signal tests while adding startup cleanup; hosted jobs remain separately controlled. |

### Automated-gate crosswalk, source characterization

At the pinned commit, pull requests against any base, master pushes, and merge groups select nine ordinary CI job definitions; the userspace and Python 3.14 shard matrices expand those definitions to 14 runner jobs if every leg schedules. Four other definitions are manual or scheduled. Daily schedules select the Python 3.11 full suite and 130-pair synthetic Slurm profile; Sunday also selects the 100,000-pair profile. Manual dispatch offers eight Boolean lane selectors and rejects an empty selection (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:3-107,288-308,901-911,1023-1034,1064-1070,1099-1104,1275-1336`). This describes workflow selection, not a hosted result. The tracked pre-commit configuration defines Ruff check, Ruff format check, and ShellCheck hooks for matching changed files; their presence does not show that any developer has installed or run them (`.pre-commit-config.yaml:1-21`). They are distinct from the assembled Make and CI lanes (`scripts/make_quality.mk:211-239`). All 22 Make targets have a source-level route below; caller-complete fault comparisons remain open before any proposed retirement.

| CI job and pinned source | Selection | Defense and evidence ceiling | Related investigation |
|---|---|---|---|
| `manual-selection` (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:78-107`) | Manual dispatch | Rejects an empty lane selection; does not prove any selected lane passed | Check selector/required-check expectations |
| `workflow-lint` (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:109-162`) | Ordinary; selected dispatch | Checksum-verified Actionlint checks workflow syntax and embedded shell; does not execute jobs | Preserve workflow-lint fault coverage |
| `static-wheel` (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:164-186`) | Ordinary; selected dispatch | Runs static, documentation, and installed-wheel checks; not the full test suite | A01-03/06/07/08/17/35/38/68 |
| `shell-contracts` (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:188-208`) | Ordinary; selected dispatch | Runs listed direct shell owner contracts; no native-site or Slurm proof | A01-12/17/184/185/186; preserve worker fault cases while comparing fake inputs and duplicate parsing |
| `guarded-r` (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:210-286`) | Ordinary; selected dispatch | Restores locked R and runs guarded R/Step 08/09/projection fixtures on hosted Ubuntu; no Viking or biological proof | A01-21/22/34/187/189/204; retain real-R and independent numerical oracles while scoping Step 10 fixture and host-library provenance claims |
| `managed-runtime-userspace` (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:288-405`) | Ordinary; selected dispatch | Three distro/glibc legs install locked native/R tools and invoke versions; explicitly not Rocky's 4.18-kernel or Viking proof | A01-225/226/239; map startup-roster, executable-check, and repeated STAR claims separately |
| `managed-golden-path` (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:407-420,632-899`) | Ordinary; selected dispatch | Clean-clone synthetic Project, direct runtime/Doctor/Run/report path, borrower checks, and real-samtools containment; no Slurm or institution proof | A01-09/29/34; inspect retained artifacts |
| `python314-coverage-shards` (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:901-947`) | Ordinary; selected dispatch | Four behavioral shards upload coverage fragments and selection receipts; one shard cannot prove full inventory | A01-39/61/202; check collection cost |
| `python314-coverage` (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:948-991`) | Ordinary; selected dispatch | Requires complete, disjoint successful shard receipts, subprocess probe presence, and configured Python coverage policy; route line counts are not floors, and coverage is not an independent oracle | A01-39/61/124; retain receipt and global/owner floors |
| `python311-smoke` (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:993-1021`) | Ordinary; selected dispatch | Compiles source, smoke-tests the wheel, and invokes manifest validation on minimum Python; not the full 3.11 suite | Map version-specific faults |
| `python311-full-shards` (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:1023-1062`) | Schedule; selected dispatch | Four 3.11 behavioral shards upload selection receipts; each is partial alone | A01-39; check selected-inventory and receipt parity |
| `python311-full` (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:1064-1097`) | Schedule; selected dispatch | Requires complete/disjoint 3.11 behavioral receipts and every shard to pass; the collector excludes the wheel and shard self-test files, and this job has no 3.11 coverage floor | A01-39; preserve the declared minimum-version scope |
| `synthetic-e2e` (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:1099-1104,1272-1417`) | 130 each schedule, 100000 Sunday; either selected on dispatch | Real tools and disposable single-node Slurm exercise selected synthetic profiles and require their evidence uploads; 130 compares selected direct/Slurm Results and recovery, while 100000 runs the Slurm path without that comparison (`tests/tools/real_synthetic_e2e.py:1626-1630,1762-1780,2221-2252`); no CSU Viking, scientific review, or biological proof | A01-22/128/181/183; preserve distinct profile claims and report admission without promoting report parity |

No job is cleared by this source crosswalk. Each selection and outcome still needs exact-hosted-commit verification, and each suggested reduction needs a surviving defense at the same trust and evidence boundary. Workflow definitions also do not establish which statuses currently block a merge: an earlier hosted-rules inspection is recorded separately in `docs/tasks/polish-campaign.md:737-755` and needs a fresh effective-rules check before any current merge-gate claim or change.

### Local Make and hook source map

All 22 named Make targets have been mapped to their recipes and searched for in-repository callers. A target listed together with another below is still a distinct recipe or alias; these groups are not a claim of equivalent fault coverage. The test-owned Make inventory pins their names and recipe expansions (`tests/test_public_cli_contracts.py:182-205,877-923`).

| Targets | Current route and distinct boundary | Remaining decision |
|---|---|---|
| `test` (`Makefile:19-20`) | Direct full Pytest convenience; no in-repository caller identified | External developer use is unknown |
| `documentation-check`, `lint`, `validation-static` (`scripts/make_quality.mk:73-75,211-219,228-236`) | Static CI invokes the assembled target, which includes document checks, Ruff, ShellCheck, Vulture, source direction, Bash syntax, compilation, manifest, and shard-plan tests | A01-03/06/07/17/35; do not equate each subcheck |
| `shell-test`, `validation-shell-contracts`, `smoke` (`scripts/make_quality.mk:25-50,76-81,207-209,225-226`) | Shell alias and CI contract target cover all 10 tracked `test_*.sh` files; standalone smoke only parses a 13-path Bash roster | A01-17; compare omitted source files with all-file ShellCheck |
| `validation-wheel-smoke`, `report-test`, `validate` (`scripts/make_quality.mk:83-85,199-205,221-223`) | Installed distribution test, five-file reporting subset, and standalone sample-manifest command have different scopes | A01-36/38/68; operator use of subsets is open |
| `real-r-test`, `r-restore`, `r-check`, `local-real-r-test`, `validation-guarded-r` (`scripts/make_quality.mk:87-124,195-197`) | Direct wrappers can skip; guarded lane selects an existing R environment; restore is an explicit operator mutation | A01-85; preserve real-R computation proof |
| `python-coverage-shard`, `python-coverage-finalize`, `python-coverage-measure`, `python-coverage-enforce`, `python-coverage-check`, `python-coverage-baseline-update` (`scripts/make_quality.mk:126-193`) | Collection, receipt/coverage combination, policy, and explicit baseline mutation are separate phases | A01-39/61; retain complete/disjoint receipts and reviewed floors |
| `all-checks` (`scripts/make_quality.mk:238-244`; `tests/tools/run_validation.py:154-216`) | Local assembly runs static preflight, then coverage, wheel, shell, and guarded-R lanes | A01-65/146; do not call a partial lane a complete gate, a launch failure controlled cancellation, or whole-log retention byte-bounded |

The pinned Ruff paths cover all 282 tracked Python/PYI files; `make lint` ShellChecks all 28 tracked shell files, whereas the optional pre-commit hooks check matching staged files (`scripts/make_quality.mk:7,228-236`; `.pre-commit-config.yaml:5-21`). Those timing and scope differences survive the shared tool names. The fixed Bash syntax roster omits the sourced production `argument_parsing.sh` and `file_checks.sh`; parsing a caller does not parse a sourced file. The all-file ShellCheck gate remains, so this is a standalone `smoke` scope limit, not a demonstrated ordinary-CI blind spot. No Make target or hook has been shown safe to remove.

A second source comparison of the Make recipes, CI job selection, hooks, and their tests found no additional whole-gate retirement candidate beyond the matrix entries. Staged-file lint, all-tracked-file lint, Bash syntax, installed-wheel smoke, behavioral shards, coverage finalization, guarded R, and synthetic Slurm exercise different inputs or evidence layers (`scripts/make_quality.mk:73-85,126-193,207-244`; `.github/workflows/ci.yml:164-420,901-1104`; `.pre-commit-config.yaml:1-21`). This is a source disposition, not a hosted result or a claim that every gate is complete.

## Discovery notes

### A01-01 — Step 07 selector validation false pass

**Supported behavior and failure.** Step 07's validation report should reconcile the declared region selector with the output transaction. `src/emrys/libraries/validation/mpileup.py:130-153` checks a region-file row for a known contig but does not establish its coordinate bounds. The owner emits this as `selector_reconciliation` in `src/emrys/stages/partitioned_cohort_mpileup/validator.py:138-150`. A retained test at `tests/stages/partitioned_cohort_mpileup/test_validate_step_07_mpileup_outputs.py:424-445` explicitly expects an all-pass report for a BED end beyond the FAI bound. The owner contract at `src/emrys/stages/partitioned_cohort_mpileup/CONTRACT.md:90-103` states a limited validator ceiling.

**Surviving defenses and next step.** Project admission and the producer check selectors before work; receipt and sample checks protect other properties. Those defenses do not establish that an existing Step 07 validation report proves selector bounds. Retain the characterization. Trace every consumer of this report, then decide the owner's required validation ceiling before proposing a fix.

**Caller trace.** Project admission and the producer reject out-of-FAI BED coordinates before normal Run work (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/onboarding.py:1631-1683`; `src/emrys/stages/partitioned_cohort_mpileup/producer.py:118-169`), and Run binds the selector as a stable input (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/materialization.py:742-755`; `src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/task.py:2788-2800`). Reporting's native Step 07 reconciliation checks receipt/VCF relationships but not selector bounds or equality of `selector_type` and `selector_value` across its two receipt rows (`src/emrys/reporting/_artifact_index/reconcile_native.py:187-269`). The owner validator compares both rows with the partition manifest (`src/emrys/stages/partitioned_cohort_mpileup/validator.py:124-150`), and Run requires its all-pass report (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/task.py:2788-2800`). Adapter tests mutate record counts and header definitions, not a cross-orientation selector mismatch (`tests/reporting/test_artifact_adapters.py:1004-1025,1538-1565`). These are standalone report/index ceilings, not demonstrated supported-Run false passes. The report's intended selector claim still needs an owner decision.

### A01-02 — Selector syntax differs across owners

Project admission uses `str.isdigit()` before integer conversion (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/onboarding.py:1645-1651`); the Step 07 producer requires ASCII `[0-9]+` (`src/emrys/stages/partitioned_cohort_mpileup/producer.py:131-136`). `src/emrys/contracts/SOURCE_TOPOLOGY.md:45` records this disparity. An admitted Project selector therefore may not be accepted by the worker. This is a source-level mismatch, not a reproduced Run failure. Decide the supported coordinate syntax, inspect Project-side tests and all selector readers, and preserve producer refusal until caller-complete parity is demonstrated.

**Focused syntax probe.** Arabic-Indic `١` passes `str.isdigit()` and `int` but fails the producer's ASCII regex; superscript `²` passes `isdigit()` then raises raw `ValueError` in the Project helper. The producer test includes a superscript refusal (`tests/stages/partitioned_cohort_mpileup/test_partitioned_cohort_mpileup_producer.py:371-419`), but no Project Unicode fixture was found. This was not a full Project or Run invocation. A caller-complete grammar decision must preserve re-admission at each trust boundary.

### A01-03 — CI whitespace check does not inspect committed changes

`scripts/make_quality.mk:211-219` runs `git diff --check` inside `validation-static`. The CI job first checks out the commit and runs that target (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:173-185`); the command inspects unstaged worktree changes rather than the committed PR range. The local command remains useful for local edits. The checkout at `.github/workflows/ci.yml:118-122` does not request full history. Specify an event-aware committed comparison range, fetch enough history, and verify the file types before changing the gate.

**Focused reproduction.** In a disposable Git repository, a clean checkout with one committed trailing-space line returned exit 0 from `git diff --check`, while `git show --check HEAD` returned exit 2 and identified the line. This isolates the Git command's blind spot; it is not a hosted CI run with a bad commit. Keep the useful local check and choose a committed comparison range with sufficient event-specific checkout history.

**CI event scope.** The workflow invokes the static target for pull requests, master pushes, merge groups, and selected manual dispatches (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:3-18,164-186`). Its shared checkout has no explicit history depth (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:118-122`). Workflow and Make tests protect trigger/target wiring but do not inject a bad committed line (`tests/test_ci_workflow.py:65-69,111-131`; `tests/test_validation_orchestrator.py:101-133`). The bare check also serves useful local invocation paths (`tests/tools/run_validation.py:154-164`). Define the checked result and comparison endpoints separately for PR, merge-group, push, and manual events; fetch or validate both endpoints and fail clearly when a required endpoint is absent. A net two-tree diff and every-intermediate-commit scan make different promises. The disposable Git reproduction supports the command-level failure; no hosted bad-commit run was performed.

### A01-04 — Validation inventory assertion misses source owners

`tests/contract_integration/validation_rosters/test_validation_check_rosters.py:84-95` says it checks the exact live validator inventory, but scans `scripts/validate_step_*.py`. The listed validators reside under `src/emrys`; the scan and its expected script subset are both empty at this commit. The test still confirms that its named paths exist and that its two lists agree. The gap is discovery of a *new* source-owned validator, not loss of every roster check. The Stage Map identifies 14 built-in stage, evidence, and analysis owners (`src/emrys/contracts/STAGE_MAP.md:17-34`), while the grouped CLI exposes 17 validation routes including three generic commands (`src/emrys/__main__.py:11-33,44-65`). Literal check-ID expectations cover Steps 00a–09 (`tests/contract_integration/validation_rosters/validation_roster_expectations.py:5-102`), and Step 10 has a direct one-row assertion (`tests/analyses/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking/scientific_context_projection/test_validator.py:80-99`). Profile-to-Stage Map and template checks are separate surviving defenses (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_profile.py:271-306`). Reconcile the owner identity, installed route, and emitted-report sets without adding another mutable product registry; preserve literal oracles independent of producer constants.

### A01-05 — Central roster mutation multiplication

The same test file parametrizes generic roster mutations over all listed steps (`:98-146`). Its shared report validator has no step-specific branches (`src/emrys/libraries/validation/report.py:83-111`). Reordering a one-check roster produces the same order, so that acceptance case cannot probe an ordering defect. Candidate reduction is representative one-check and multi-check helper cases, while retaining every owner's literal expected roster and actual emitted-report assertion. First map the distinct faults and measure cost under `QUAL-01`.

**Fault and cost map.** The parametrization expands statically to 56 literal-oracle cases, 42 shared-validator missing/extra/duplicate cases, and 14 shared-validator reorder acceptances; the one-check Step 10 literal reorder skips, leaving 111 executable cases (`tests/contract_integration/validation_rosters/test_validation_check_rosters.py:98-146`). All 42 shared-validator mutations change row count and stop at the length gate, before its separate ID-set comparison (`src/emrys/libraries/validation/report.py:97-106`). A direct library test already exercises an equal-count wrong ID (`tests/libraries/test_validation_report.py:161-168`). The Step 10 shared reorder acceptance is identical to its input. One missing, one extra, one duplicate, and one meaningful multi-check reorder case would retain those helper fault classes while reducing 56 parametrized helper variants to four, including removal of the vacuous Step 10 skip. That is up to 52 collected variants, not a measured time saving or a reason to trim the 56 literal owner-oracle cases. The shard baseline does not retain measured per-roster durations; unlisted nodes receive a 1.0-second scheduling estimate (`tests/baselines/python_test_durations.json:1-9`; `tests/tools/python_test_shards.py:150-155`). Treat the apparent 112-second allocation as an estimate, not observed cost. Any later reduction should target central helper permutations only after `QUAL-01` measurement, retaining literal ordered rosters, each owner's emitted-report assertion, Step 10's direct check, and the reporting boundary's distinct malformed-roster fixtures (`tests/reporting/test_artifact_adapters.py:559-604`).

### A01-06 — Canonical document names and headings

`scripts/documentation/validate_structure.py:14-34,199-206` requires 19 named documents and an H1 somewhere in each. These pages include durable scientist, operator, architecture, owner, and accepted-work homes assigned by the documentation authority map (`docs/design/decisions/repository-and-delivery.md:79-89`). A named-path check can detect removal of an authority page even if its inbound link is also removed; local-link admission checks a different stale-destination fault (`scripts/documentation/validate_structure.py:164-196`). `first_heading` (`:120-126`) accepts any nonempty H1 text, regardless of position. `tests/documentation/test_validate_structure.py:158-176` explicitly accepts a renamed Workflow title. Thus the gate protects page presence and minimum heading structure, not title identity or first-line placement. The test fixture mirrors the 19 names at `tests/documentation/test_validate_structure.py:17-37`, but its negative test (`:179-195`) exercises only Workflow, Runbook, and Troubleshooting, with no assertion that fixture and production rosters match. Retain named-path and link protection while deciding whether a stronger heading rule has maintenance value; no whole documentation-gate retirement is justified.

### A01-07 — Anchor parser and tilde fences

Local link targets and anchors are checked in `scripts/documentation/validate_structure.py:164-196`. Its anchor extractor (`:129-147`) toggles fenced-code state only for backticks, although CommonMark also permits tilde fences. A heading-looking line inside a tilde fence could therefore satisfy a link to a nonexistent heading. An isolated local call to `markdown_anchors` on a temporary file containing only a tilde fence and `# phantom` returned `phantom` as an anchor. This does not establish the full documentation check's behavior under every input.

The same extractor recognizes only unindented raw ATX lines and slugs raw source text (`scripts/documentation/validate_structure.py:129-147`). CommonMark setext headings, ATX headings indented one to three spaces, and rendered text from an inline-link heading are outside that model. A valid `#title` link could be rejected, while a source-derived non-rendered slug such as `#titleguidemd` could be accepted (`:164-196`). The direct link test covers a plain anchor and a missing anchor, not these heading forms (`tests/documentation/test_validate_structure.py:290-310`). Its valid `#runbook` anchor is placed beside two intentional link errors; the assertions require the intended errors but never rule out an extra false-rejection of that valid anchor (`:295-310`). The first run does separately exclude a diagnostic naming the valid `guide(1).md` target (`:306`), so that particular acceptance is partly protected. After the bad links are removed, a Mermaid fault still prevents the fixture from asserting a clean link result (`:312-323`). This is source inference about future syntax or regression, not a broken tracked link. Reuse one established Markdown parse/slug contract for anchors, and isolate a successful anchor/link fixture while retaining destination existence and containment checks.

Raw HTML `<a href>` and `<img src>` targets are also outside `markdown_destinations`, which selects only MarkdownIt `link_open` and `image` children (`scripts/documentation/validate_structure.py:150-160`). The documentation-checker guide calls its scope local links and anchors (`scripts/documentation/README.md:4-7`), but no pinned tracked raw-HTML link/image attribute was found. Decide whether such HTML is prohibited or included before expanding the gate; preserve ordinary Markdown link checks. The tilde-fence probe remains the only executed case in this entry.

### A01-08 — Mermaid gate ceiling

`scripts/documentation/validate_structure.py:235-251` verifies a standalone Mermaid declaration and rejects Markdown fences in Mermaid source. It does not parse the whole diagram. `scripts/documentation/README.md:3-18` describes this narrow role. No removal or expansion follows merely from the narrow scope. Determine whether a distinct diagram failure merits an established parser or renderer, and account for its maintenance cost.

### A01-09 — Hosted resource fixture independence

`tests/test_ci_workflow.py:411-479` executes the workflow's fixture-generating text and admits the result through production profile and resource-policy code. `tests/tools/real_synthetic_e2e.py:463-476` starts from packaged policy defaults; `tests/test_real_synthetic_e2e.py:202-220` compares much of its result with those same defaults. These are useful wiring and admission checks but not independent oracles for every default value. Inventory the literal owner-policy tests and the exact fixture claims before replacing any assertion. Hosted CI does not establish Viking utilization or performance.

### A01-10 — Reporting adapter fixture independence

`tests/reporting/fixtures/artifact_adapters_v1/build_fixture.py` derives parts of expected headers and row counts from the production adapter registry. `tests/reporting/test_artifact_adapters.py:236-250` compares inventory rows with the template the builder also reads, while retaining a literal count of 74 and registry checks. The comparison can catch builder drift but cannot independently establish that the template itself is correct. Map literal headers, row identities, and independent contract goldens before changing this fixture.

**Surviving independent checks.** The contract golden suite pins selected public schema pointers, ordered header constants, canonical JSON/TSV and report-receipt bytes, plus both report HTML view hashes; it mutates schemas, headers, serializers, and report input to show those oracles reject drift (`tests/contract_integration/independent_contract_goldens/test_independent_contract_goldens.py:95-175,178-277`). These checks are independent of the adapter fixture template, but they do not enumerate all 74 adapter inventory rows. Compare the exact overlapping headers and rows before proposing any fixture or test reduction; retain the goldens' distinct contract and rendering faults.

The fixture has a separate exact duplication: its Step 10 setup assigns the same reference DICT path twice and writes identical bytes twice to that path (`tests/reporting/fixtures/artifact_adapters_v1/build_fixture.py:910-911,920-927`). The first assignment and write suffice for the later transaction (`:929-974`); the second pair is a test-only reduction candidate that does not retire an oracle.

### A01-11 — Reporting roster identity defect

`tests/reporting/test_artifact_adapters.py:585-604` characterizes the generic artifact index marking a Step 00a validation-report entry `complete` after its checks are reordered or one unique ID is replaced. The generic reader and inspector enforce header, row shape, unique safe IDs, step/scope/status, and failure status, but not exact identity and order (`src/emrys/reporting/_artifact_index/_text_tabular.py:41-58,83-89`; `src/emrys/reporting/_artifact_index/inspection.py:151-199`). `src/emrys/reporting/_artifact_index/README.md:62-69` directs that these tests remain until the defect is resolved. Run inspection rehashes bound task reports and requires all-pass (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/task.py:2330-2348`); reporting requires successful complete Results and binds processing-source bytes (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/reporting_operation.py:149-158,185-239`). The built-in science reporter additionally requires exact ordered Step 09 and Step 10 checks (`src/emrys/reporting/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking_report/computational.py:104-113,150-185`; `src/emrys/reporting/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking_report/scientific_context.py:211-228`). Thus the direct builder fixture does not demonstrate a supported full-Run false completion or a uniform gap in every report view. The admitted collaborator artifact descriptor does not declare a check-ID roster (`src/emrys/analyses/__init__.py:59-68,245-274,340-359`); `REPORT-ROSTER-01` must settle its source of truth and external-provider contract before implementation or test retirement. Retain the generic shape and transaction defenses and both roster characterizations.

### A01-17 — Make recipes and static checks

`tests/test_public_cli_contracts.py:876-923` inventories Make targets and dry-runs their command expansions against a large fixture. Exact operator mutation commands merit protection; an internal recipe snapshot may be more restrictive than its supported behavior. `scripts/make_quality.mk:207-233` runs a fixed shell syntax roster and all-tracked-file ShellCheck in different targets; `smoke` exposes the syntax check separately. `report-test` and `smoke` have no identified in-repository caller, which does not establish that operators do not use them. Decide each target's supported use and failure before retiring a snapshot or gate. Also inspect the unused `SHFMT_BIN` setting without adding another formatter by default.

**Roster and configuration detail.** `SHELL_CONTRACT_PATHS` includes all 10 tracked `test_*.sh` files by that naming convention (`scripts/make_quality.mk:40-50`); that roster says nothing about whether each test invokes every fake-tool fault. `SHELL_SYNTAX_PATHS` names 13 paths and omits the sourced production `src/emrys/libraries/argument_parsing.sh` and `file_checks.sh` (`scripts/make_quality.mk:25-39`). `smoke` therefore is a selected syntax check, while `lint` separately passes every tracked shell file to ShellCheck (`scripts/make_quality.mk:207-236`). The standalone syntax target has a narrower promise; normal static CI includes `lint`, so the omitted paths are not an established CI lint bypass. The unused `PYTHON_BIN` Make default (`Makefile:2`) has no recipe reference; the separate shell test reads an environment variable with its own `python3` fallback (`tests/shell/test_local_r_environment.sh:83-84`). Its presence is pinned by a Make snapshot (`tests/test_public_cli_contracts.py:208,877-889`). Assess external override use before a one-line configuration reduction.

**Literal test overlap.** `tests/test_validation_orchestrator.py:93-95,101-133` and `tests/test_python_coverage_baseline.py:262-288` both assert `.coveragerc` parallel mode and Make/compileall/shard-distribution literals. The public Make contract test also checks all 22 target expansions (`tests/test_public_cli_contracts.py:876-923`), while the shard-owner tests exercise actual distribution selection (`tests/test_python_test_shards.py:78-94`). These are candidate duplicate literal assertions, not a basis for retiring exact Make behavior or selected shard tests. Compare which single-token mutation each catches before trimming source snapshots.

**Eight-target test reduction.** The substring loop for eight quality targets in `tests/test_validation_orchestrator.py:97-111` adds no traced command or availability fault beyond the exact 22-target `.PHONY` inventory and per-target dry-run expansion oracle (`tests/test_public_cli_contracts.py:182-205,877-922`; `tests/fixtures/public_cli_contracts/make_target_expansions.json:2-9,93-152,277-284,300-355`). A removed target whose name remains in a comment can pass the substring loop but fails the Make inventory or expansion. The loop's extra constraint is physical placement in `scripts/make_quality.mk`; no supported operator failure from moving an otherwise equivalent recipe was traced. This is a bounded test-code reduction candidate, not authority to change Make. Retain the runner-to-target mapping (`tests/test_validation_orchestrator.py:36-57`), dependency-source assertions (`:60-91`), actual Make fault tests (`:143-209`), and the exact public command golden. Other literals at `:112-133` substantially overlap that golden, but `demo-report:` absence has a separate non-phony-target fault to assess before removal.

**Shared shell-syntax case product.** The syntax test cross-products `smoke` and `validation-static` with success plus two malformed-script positions (`tests/test_validation_orchestrator.py:171-209`). Both recipes invoke the same `STATIC_SHELL_CHECKS` loop (`scripts/make_quality.mk:207-226`); the middle-script failure under `validation-static` adds no different syntax-loop fault after the `smoke` case. Retain `validation-static` success and its final-script failure to prove the target reaches the shared loop, plus all three `smoke` positions. This is a one-collected-case proposal, not measured cost or authority to remove either Make gate.

**Future shell-test inventory.** `SHELL_CONTRACT_PATHS` currently names all ten tracked `test_*.sh` files, and the shell-contract target executes only that fixed roster (`scripts/make_quality.mk:40-50,76-79`). Hosted shell CI and the local validation runner select that target (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:202-208`; `tests/tools/run_validation.py:196-207`). The public Make golden snapshots the current expansion but does not derive the tracked shell-test inventory (`tests/test_public_cli_contracts.py:876-923`). A newly added shell test absent from the roster would still be ShellChecked by `lint`, but would not be executed through this shell-contract lane; Python shard collection is separate (`tests/tools/python_test_shards.py:93-98`). Add an inventory-oracle or explicit exemption rule if new shell tests are meant to join this lane automatically. This is a future-addition test gap, not a missing pinned shell test or a whole-gate retirement case.

### A01-18 — Dependency source and artifact checks

`tests/test_validation_orchestrator.py:60-90` pins source dependency declarations and group wiring. `tests/test_package_distribution.py:192-222` checks dependencies in built wheel metadata. These partly overlap but inspect different representations: the former can detect source-list drift, the latter packaging drift. Compare the exact failures caught by source literals, lockfile checks, and wheel inspection before proposing selective consolidation; retain installed-artifact protection.

**Marker and interpreter-range faults.** The wheel test drops environment markers from each `Requires-Dist` before its exact dependency map comparison (`tests/test_package_distribution.py:179-189,218-222`). A source declaration such as a Linux-only Snakemake dependency could therefore pass that wheel comparison and a Linux installed probe while omitting the dependency on another platform. The source test's exact unmarked dependency set (`tests/test_validation_orchestrator.py:60-76`) has a distinct cross-environment fault and should not be retired as duplicate wheel coverage. Separately, the package advertises Python `>=3.11` (`pyproject.toml:12`), but wheel metadata inspection does not assert `Requires-Python`; the wheel smoke's temporary installer independently declares `>=3.11` (`tests/test_package_distribution.py:192-247,264-269`). Current CI exercises 3.11 and 3.14 (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:70-75,993-1021`), not every interpreter allowed by a future changed declaration. A source/lock-coordinated widening could evade those runtime lanes. Pin the wheel's advertised interpreter floor if it is a supported contract. These are regression-oracle limits; the pinned source and metadata are not shown wrong.

### A01-19 — Manual Step 05 operator check

`tests/data_checks/validate_step05_outputs.sh` performs site-specific checks of existing BAM/BAI outputs, can query Slurm, writes a selected status TSV, and probes output-directory writability. `tests/data_checks/README.md:1-7` explicitly retains it and limits its evidence claim. Its `PASS` branch depends on the preexisting BAM/BAI, quickcheck, header substrings, and scratch markers, but not the recorded Slurm `job_state`; a failed historical job with a structurally passing prior pair can still yield `status=PASS` and exit zero (`tests/data_checks/validate_step05_outputs.sh:149-183,204-221`). That is an existing-output status, not evidence that the selected job succeeded or produced those files. No in-repository caller was found; that is insufficient to call an operator script obsolete. Establish operator use and retained outputs, and keep the status's source claim narrow. Do not run it as part of a local source audit.

### A01-20 — Step 07 VCF semantics are outside the report ceiling

`tests/stages/partitioned_cohort_mpileup/test_validate_step_07_mpileup_outputs.py:448-462` writes malformed REF, symbolic ALT, and broken FORMAT/sample data, then expects every validation row to pass. `src/emrys/stages/partitioned_cohort_mpileup/CONTRACT.md:90-96,115-119` limits the validator to shape and counts. This is another retained characterization of a passing report's meaning, separate from A01-01's selector bounds. Trace report consumers and decide whether a semantic admission check or clearer wording is required; do not delete the test as redundant.

**Later defenses.** Reporting's native reconciliation requires INFO/FORMAT header definitions (`src/emrys/reporting/_artifact_index/reconcile_native.py:212-220`), and Step 08 R parses declared definitions and lexical counts (`src/emrys/stages/cohort_candidate_preprocessing/_step_08_vcf_processing.R:42-97`). These protect some malformed shapes without proving every REF/ALT or genotype meaningful. No complete report or supported Run was reproduced with the standalone Step 07 fixture.

### A01-21 — Step 08 candidate identity and order need a separate oracle

A test replaces candidate IDs with arbitrary unique strings and reverses rows while expecting all-pass (`tests/stages/cohort_candidate_preprocessing/test_validate_step_08_preprocessing_outputs.py:376-390`). The owner contract (`src/emrys/stages/cohort_candidate_preprocessing/CONTRACT.md:121-129,143-146`) says the Python report checks internal structure, not reconstructed IDs or deterministic order. Guarded real-R tests assert literal candidate order and worker-count byte determinism (`tests/stages/cohort_candidate_preprocessing/test_step_08_vcf_preprocessing.R:619-635,873-885`). Retain both evidence layers; a passing Python report alone does not prove the producer's scientific reconstruction.

**Downstream trace.** Reporting's Step 08 native reconciliation checks source bindings, sample order, partition/orientation counts, and summary arithmetic, but does not reconstruct candidate IDs or row order (`src/emrys/reporting/_artifact_index/reconcile_native.py:271-435`). Its TSV adapter records header and row-count evidence for Step 08 sites (`src/emrys/reporting/_artifact_index/_text_tabular.py:19-111`). The Step 09 owner compares its carried rows with the Step 08 rows it reads (`src/emrys/contracts/scientific_evidence/step09.py:804-832`), while reporting's Step 09 projection binds Step 08 source path/hash and sample order (`src/emrys/reporting/_artifact_index/reconcile_step09.py:37-72`). These downstream consistency checks do not independently establish Step 08 candidate ID/order derivation. No full report falsification fixture was run; retain guarded real-R literal and determinism oracles.

**Standalone coordinate ceiling.** The shared Step 08 sites helper parses genomic `position` as nonnegative (`src/emrys/contracts/scientific_evidence/step08.py:520-522`), and the owner report checks positive positions only when `--step07-root` is supplied (`src/emrys/stages/cohort_candidate_preprocessing/validator.py:189-195`). A tiny coherent sites/receipt helper fixture accepted `position=0`; no full owner report was run for that fixture. The runner does supply `--step07-root` (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/materialization.py:798-810`), so this does not establish a normal Run false pass. Direct owner tests characterize arbitrary IDs and row order, not zero position (`tests/stages/cohort_candidate_preprocessing/test_validate_step_08_preprocessing_outputs.py:376-390`). Decide whether standalone inspection promises VCF-valid coordinate bounds or only carried transaction structure before changing the optional-context boundary.

**Carried VCF-field ceiling.** The Step 08 sites header carries source QUAL, FILTER, and INFO alternate depth, and the R worker fills them from its VCF reader (`src/emrys/stages/cohort_candidate_preprocessing/step_08_vcf_preprocessing.R:19-25`; `src/emrys/stages/cohort_candidate_preprocessing/_step_08_vcf_processing.R:227-230`; `src/emrys/stages/cohort_candidate_preprocessing/CONTRACT.md:53-57`). The shared Python sites validator does not inspect the three values after header admission (`src/emrys/contracts/scientific_evidence/step08.py:483-584`). Its direct negative fixtures exercise position, flags, and sample counts/frequencies, while guarded R asserts some INFO depth values but no independent QUAL/FILTER source comparison (`tests/contracts/scientific_evidence/test_step08.py:501-520,620-655`; `tests/stages/cohort_candidate_preprocessing/test_step_08_vcf_preprocessing.R:648-668`). Step 09 carries this metadata and checks agreement with the Step 08 table, not the VCF's original values (`src/emrys/contracts/scientific_evidence/step09.py:438-486,804-832`); reporting reconciles source hashes, header IDs, sample order, and counts but not those cells (`src/emrys/reporting/_artifact_index/reconcile_native.py:187-435`). Thus a standalone coherent table can carry lexically malformed QUAL or negative INFO depth without a site-helper refusal. The owner contract limits the Python check to basic/table-internal fields (`src/emrys/stages/cohort_candidate_preprocessing/CONTRACT.md:92-99,121-129`), so decide whether stronger direct admission is promised before adding those oracles. This source trace is not evidence that normal R emits such values.

### A01-22 — Step 09 validation does not recompute CMH statistics

`tests/analyses/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking/test_validate_step_09_cmh_outputs.py:282-335` fabricates CMH statistics, p-values, adjusted values, and odds ratios while the report passes. `src/emrys/analyses/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking/CONTRACT.md:105-113` states this ceiling. A production-independent Python oracle and guarded R corpus cover numerical behavior (`tests/analyses/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking/test_step_09_cmh_oracle.py:108-178`; `tests/analyses/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking/test_step_09_cmh_editing_site_calling.R:222-313`). Preserve the characterization and independent oracles, and avoid promoting report status to scientific proof.

**Corpus-maintenance ceiling.** The Step 09 boundary-coverage test unions free-text `requirement_tags` from the corpus rather than deriving categories from the input count vectors or expected results (`tests/analyses/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking/test_step_09_cmh_oracle.py:126-146`). Tags could drift onto ordinary rows while that breadth assertion still passes. The committed corpus currently contains the named difficult cases, and per-case independent Python and guarded R checks compare numeric outputs (`tests/analyses/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking/step_09_cmh_oracle.tsv:2-13`; `tests/analyses/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking/test_step_09_cmh_oracle.py:149-178`; `tests/analyses/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking/test_step_09_cmh_editing_site_calling.R:239-313`). This is a future fixture-maintenance weakness, not evidence that today's numerical oracle is vacuous. Preserve the corpus and independent calculations if deriving coverage from values later.

**Synthetic end-to-end ceiling.** The generated fixture declares expected control/treatment allele frequencies, a positive difference, and common odds ratio (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/synthetic_fixture.py:627-640`), but the real-tool E2E Step 09 oracle reads only candidate ID and call status, checks three/all and one/significant rows, and never compares those numeric expectations (`tests/tools/real_synthetic_e2e.py:872-910`). It accepts either `significant_up` or `significant_down` for the selected candidate despite that declared positive direction (`:900-904`). Its successful result would not independently detect an internally consistent wrong effect size or direction in that hosted journey. The numerical corpus and guarded R tests above protect different inputs and computations; Run receipts protect byte identity.

In particular, the guarded R test executes the committed CMH implementation against every literal corpus row and checks its integrated fixture's ordering, statuses, summary, directional cases, and global BH values (`tests/analyses/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking/test_step_09_cmh_editing_site_calling.R:157-313,586-678,806-955`). A01-22 concerns arbitrary externally supplied, internally consistent values at direct report admission, not absence of a normal native-computation oracle. Decide whether the synthetic E2E promises the fixture's literal effects before adding a justified numeric assertion and requiring the expected `significant_up` direction. A01-183 separately concerns status vocabulary and cross-file agreement in this direct driver oracle. No hosted run or full-Run false pass was reproduced in this audit.

`src/emrys/stages/gtf_to_bed12/validator.py:48-59` calls the converter's `normalize_gtf` before comparing expected and observed BED12 lines. On zero exit, the producer retains nonempty native `samtools quickcheck -v` output (`src/emrys/evidence/canonical_bam_qc/step_02b_bam_qc.sh:52-62`), while the validator accepts only the synthetic empty-success PASS line (`src/emrys/evidence/canonical_bam_qc/validator.py:47-53`). `src/emrys/stages/canonical_bam/CONTRACT.md:137-147,182-186` records that the validator permits a zero-record BAM and omits producer-required `LB` and `PL:ILLUMINA` fields. Its nominal fake-`samtools` fixture supplies only `ID` and `SM` yet requires all report rows to pass (`tests/stages/canonical_bam/test_validate_step_02_canonical_bam.py:41-60,147-173`); the worker fixture separately requires the full producer RG fields (`tests/stages/canonical_bam/test_step_02_sort_index_bam.sh:192-205`). `tests/evidence/reference_provenance/test_reference_provenance.py:466-518` injects publication and restoration failures and characterizes surviving backups without a lock or recovery marker. The producer test permits an internally inconsistent flag subcount to be emitted (`tests/stages/mechanical_orientation/test_mechanical_orientation_producer.py:248-255`); the validator test catches count arithmetic (`tests/stages/mechanical_orientation/test_validate_step_06_orientation_outputs.py:113-127`). ### A01-28 — All-pass is not an exact-roster oracle

`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/all_pass.py:64-111` requires a nonempty report, unique passing rows, matching step/scope, and a bound hash, but accepts arbitrary check-ID membership. `tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_all_pass.py:46-112` deliberately uses invented IDs. Exact owner rosters have a separate publication check (`src/emrys/libraries/validation/report.py:83-111`) and literal owner expectations. ### A01-29 — Real workflow engine with test-owned science

`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/fixtures/workflow.py:1,20-47` uses the real Snakefile/profile but substitutes no-science commands and builds a production-derived adapter registry. `tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_workflow.py:256-397,474-668` exercises scheduling, reuse, and recovery; the fixture README (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/fixtures/README.md:1-3`) limits its claim. Retain these orchestration fault checks. They do not prove scientific computation or institutional execution. Separately, `tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_profile.py:27-201,271-306` pins 13 task owners and 70 artifact templates as literal expectations and should not be retired as a duplicate of this generated fixture.

The real-engine test calls Snakemake directly with a local fixture profile and a fixture metadata directory (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_workflow.py:68-111`). Production constructs a package profile, Run-root working directory, and admitted cores/resources in its launch arguments (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/lifecycle.py:565-630`); separate lifecycle tests characterize that builder (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_lifecycle.py:1645-1674`). These are complementary source-level protections, not one exact production-argument Snakemake execution. Do not infer Run-root metadata or resume behavior from the direct engine fixture alone.

The full 35-job success/reuse fixtures pass no `--cores` or `--resources` override to their launcher, so they run under the packaged profile's one-core default (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_workflow.py:68-111,474-480,640-652`; `src/emrys/workflow/profiles/local/profile.v9+.yaml:1-7`). Production always supplies admitted cores and resource limits (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/lifecycle.py:605-624`). The real-engine fixtures establish DAG and reuse behavior with serial test-owned tasks; they do not establish concurrent production resource scheduling. Preserve those engine fixtures and the separate launch-argument tests.

### A01-30 — Scheduler fixture case multiplication

The current submission writer emits v4 requests (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/control.py:1175-1194`), while retained-request admission recognizes v1–v4 and observation intentionally requires complete v2–v4 request-specific streams (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/slurm_submission.py:55-59,95-105,219-245`). Older-version fixtures therefore exercise a supported retained-diagnostic path; a newer writer alone does not make them obsolete. The reduction question is repeated case combinations, not wholesale legacy-reader removal.

`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_slurm_submission.py:1482-1609` expands three request versions, three cluster arrangements, and four states into 36 cases around shared observation logic. The request observer distinguishes unnamed v2 from named v3/v4, while the scheduler observer branches on cluster selection, live versus accounting response, terminality, and usage (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/slurm_submission.py:219-245`; `src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/scheduler_observation.py:94-299,351-425`). A provisional six-case subset could retain those branch classes: v2 local PENDING/COMPLETED, v3 explicit local RUNNING and local CANCELLED, and v4 remote RUNNING/COMPLETED. Compare fault detection and measured cost before any reduction; preserve identity-drift and usage fault tests at `test_slurm_submission.py:1611-1741`.

A separate request-specific-stream roundtrip repeats three commands over v2/v3/v4, nine cases (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_slurm_submission.py:881-935`). Stream and scheduler-name policy vary by schema, while command-specific admission only distinguishes `run` from a request with `requested_run` (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/slurm_submission.py:122-165,171-195`). The separate resume/report test preserves their exact requested Run (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_slurm_submission.py:863-878`). A three-version `run` roundtrip is therefore a narrower reduction candidate. The current nine-case test changes `command` and `requested_run` but leaves `emrys_argv` as `run` (`:82-94,887-891`); it does not prove command/argv coherence. Keep the negative stream/name cases at `:937-976`.

The invalid-request observation test also multiplies three retained-record defects by `include_resources=False/True` (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_slurm_submission.py:979-1001`). `observe_submission_request` returns unknown before reading that option for these records (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/slurm_submission.py:219-245`), and the test forbids a scheduler call. Keeping all three record defects once each would reduce six variants to three without losing their refusal oracle; valid resource observation remains covered by the later scheduler cases. This is the narrower source-supported reduction candidate. The 36-case scheduler cross-product and nine-case request roundtrip still need branch-interaction and measured-cost comparison before changing them.

### A01-31 — Scheduler response cap is after capture

`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/scheduler_observation.py:54-68,208-218` captures subprocess stdout before rejecting replies over 64 KiB. The coordinator contract (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/CONTRACT.md:487-496`) acknowledges that capture memory is not strictly bounded. Oversized-byte tests (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_slurm_submission.py:1922-1956`) protect admission of a reply, not process-memory bounds. Retain that defense and do not report it as a bounded capture proof.

The submission transport has a separate live/retained bound: ordinary `submit` reads all retained `sbatch.stdout` into memory, and waited mode reads an unbounded first line and remainder before constructing diagnostic text (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/slurm_submission.py:1005-1059`). `_submitted_response` accepts a canonical job ID plus any nonempty cluster string on its sole line (`:1095-1111`), whereas the later request reader caps `sbatch.stdout` at 4,096 bytes and marks an overlong response's job ID unconfirmed (`:577-600`). A malformed one-line `ID;cluster` response with a very long cluster name could therefore be announced as submitted (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/control.py:1206-1225`) yet lose exact retained job observation. Existing ordinary-response tests use small bytes (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_slurm_submission.py:1369-1479`); no realistic Slurm client is shown to emit the malformed value, and no fake-client or cluster submission was executed for this note. Raw transcripts survive, and the duplicate-submission guard treats unknown prior observation conservatively (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/control.py:1113-1164`). Compare accepted live grammar, retained reader bounds, and transcript memory before changing either boundary; do not infer a lost Run or automatic duplicate submission.

### A01-32 — Prepared-finalization continuity has a stated limit

`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_lifecycle.py:1875-1919,2194-2256` tests distinct-inode equal-byte substitution and preview/confirmation behavior. The owner contract (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/CONTRACT.md:1021-1027`) says inode recycling under the same UID remains indistinguishable. Retain exact-byte, device/inode, and recovery tests; their passing status cannot establish continuity through recycled-inode substitution.

`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/CONTRACT.md:414-427` keeps v1-v3 submission contexts readable for historical diagnosis, with their original identity limits. `tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_task.py:2509-2552` exercises the internal Task module that `src/emrys/workflow/Snakefile:280` actually invokes. These tests protect distinct supported diagnostic and execution paths. Neither is obsolete merely because its name says historical or private.

There is a current-schema fixture ceiling within this retained coverage. The submission-inspection owner fixture defaults to v2, and its explicit schema case covers v2/v3 but not v4 (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_submission_inspection.py:88-103,225-250`). The production writer selects v4, and the inspector admits v2/v3/v4 (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/slurm_submission.py:55-59,72-90`; `src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/_submission_inspection.py:417-433`). Consider making the general association fixture v4 while keeping explicit historical cases; verify its downstream expectations before adoption.

### A01-34 — Runtime checks establish different evidence levels

`src/emrys/evidence/runtime_availability/README.md:3-38,106-134` separates selected tool probes, empty-Snakemake startup, Slurm compute qualification, and Run success. Real local startup coverage (`tests/evidence/runtime_availability/test_runtime_availability.py:241-300`) differs from synthetic Doctor scenarios (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_doctor.py:2553-2610`). The six real Snakemake-startup cases include four different login-name fallbacks under a patched user database (`tests/evidence/runtime_availability/test_runtime_availability.py:238-300`). They exercise the same EMRYS startup path through different environment names; only measured cost and a supported-site decision could justify a representative subset. Likewise, the Doctor Slurm qualification cases cover scheduler, profile, runtime, finalization, and identity phases rather than one repeated check (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_doctor.py:2550-2579`). Keep these protections until each fault is mapped. The installed runtime policy also has an independent current-roster guard: discovery tests require 26 derived checks and a selected STAR path (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_onboarding.py:2885-2930`), so a single missing packaged policy row is not an unguarded false-ready scenario. A01-199 separately tests the same-count probe-semantics ceiling; the currently packaged policy still selects the bcftools version probe.

**Reference fixture ceiling.** The provenance owner fixture uses a five-byte stand-in for STAR `Genome` and expects passing summary and contig agreement (`tests/evidence/reference_provenance/test_reference_provenance.py:38-50,228-256`). That protects listed file observation and name/length reconciliation, not functional STAR index usability or derivation from the FASTA. Retain the hash and contig checks; a real native or cluster route supplies a different evidence level.

### A01-35 — Stage-map gate checks owner presence, not full identity

`scripts/documentation/validate_structure.py:211-232` counts 14 slug rows matching a raw-line pattern anywhere in `src/emrys/contracts/STAGE_MAP.md` and requires adjacent README, CONTRACT, and test directories. It does not parse the identity table's machine-key or alias columns; matching rows in a fenced example could satisfy the count. A `tests.is_dir()` check establishes a directory, not test content or fault coverage. It also follows a symlinked test directory outside the checkout, as the owner-document `is_file()` checks do; A01-175 holds the corresponding source-location policy question. Wrapping all identity rows in a fenced code block could therefore leave both checks green while removing the rendered identity table. The profile comparison still protects the processing key/slug/alias values against different faults. This is a source inference, not a reproduced documentation-gate failure. Preserve owner-presence and profile-parity checks, then compare the remaining rendered-table obligation with existing machine-key contract tests before altering the parser.

### A01-36 — Reporting target's selected tests need a promise

`scripts/make_quality.mk:199-205` runs five reporting test files via `report-test` but omits `tests/reporting/test_artifact_adapters.py`. That module tests active artifact-index status and malformed or mismatched report rosters (`tests/reporting/test_artifact_adapters.py:507-604`). The reporting test guide includes artifact indexing in its owner scope (`tests/reporting/README.md:1-9,11-23`). The Python shard planner collects the omitted module in the ordinary full-suite route (`tests/tools/python_test_shards.py:20-23,93-127`; `.github/workflows/ci.yml:901-946`). A developer can therefore pass the focused reporting target without running artifact-index faults; this is a local-target scope gap, not a claim that ordinary PR CI omits those tests. Include the module in the target or explicitly document a narrower subset after deciding the target promise. Preserve full-suite shard coverage and the adapter fault tests.

`pyproject.toml:31,59-61` declares `snakemake==9.25.1` as a runtime dependency and the sole workflow-group dependency. The repository's current `uv` commands still install project dependencies when selecting the workflow group; local help inspection supports this reading, without running a sync. ### A01-38 — Installed-wheel smoke does not prove Git-build provenance

`tests/test_package_distribution.py:138-176` builds from a staged source copy without `.git` and later expects a report receipt with `git_commit` equal to `unavailable` (`:590-600`). The test meaningfully protects built-wheel resources and installed commands. It does not prove commit provenance for a wheel built inside a Git checkout; that needs separate evidence if claimed. Retain the installed-artifact checks.

The helper named `install_locked_wheel` reads the source `uv.lock` for dependency name/version constraints, creates a fresh temporary installer lock, and runs offline locked sync against that new lock (`tests/test_package_distribution.py:250-303`). Its installed probe obtains versions, but the assertion checks dependency names and nonempty version strings rather than equality with source-lock versions or artifact hashes (`:311-359`). This is constrained-version installability and isolated installed-wheel evidence, not exact source-lock artifact identity. The ordinary static CI job separately uses source-project `uv sync --locked` (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:164-186`); retain that distinct dependency boundary and avoid calling the wheel fixture a source-lock replay.

The build writer embeds `python_lock_sha256` from `uv.lock`, and installed package admission checks only that it has the shape of a 64-character hex digest (`setup.py:38-45`; `src/emrys/libraries/source_authority.py:75-89`). The wheel test copies the reviewed lock into its build tree but compares the report's installed-package record only with another record observed from that same wheel (`tests/test_package_distribution.py:138-155,590-600`). A writer regression to a different well-shaped digest could pass this self-consistent receipt check. Compare the embedded value with an independently computed digest of the copied source lock if exact lock provenance is a required wheel claim. Separately, the wheel test checks public `emrys report --help` but invokes internal context/publication functions for actual installed rendering (`tests/test_package_distribution.py:385-409,486-541`). Source-tree tests cover the Run-facing report command (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_materialization.py:5905-6047`); installed evidence proves provider loading, rendering, and receipts rather than the complete public generation route. Preserve both distinct layers without calling either one a full installed Run.

### A01-39 — Shard timing policy is separate from completeness proof

`tests/tools/python_test_shards.py:139-144` fails planning when a duration estimate names a node ID absent from collection, although estimates schedule work rather than define correctness. Each of four shards recollects the full suite (`:253-256`), and receipt verification recollects it again (`:366-371`). The verifier proves exact complete/disjoint selection (`:299-357`), not that `estimated_seconds` is a measured duration. Measure collection cost and decide whether a stale scheduling estimate should block the suite under `QUAL-01`; preserve receipt proof.

The collector deliberately excludes the installed-wheel test and its own shard self-test (`tests/tools/python_test_shards.py:20-23,93-98`). Scheduled Python 3.11 shards and their verifier use that same selected inventory, while the 3.11 wheel smoke and static-wheel jobs do not run on schedules (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:164-169,993-998,1023-1097`). Thus the nightly job named "complete suite" proves the declared behavioral inventory, not every `test_*` file. A manual dispatch selecting only Python 3.14 has the same exclusion, since `static-wheel` is selected separately (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:32-34,164-186,901-991`). Ordinary PR lanes cover the installed-wheel boundary separately. State the selected scope when reporting a scheduled or manual behavioral-suite result; no hosted result was inspected here.

### A01-43 — Read-group report checks are narrower than record tags

The shared header parser checks one exact `@RG` with matching ID/SM (`src/emrys/libraries/alignments/bam.py:56-65`); Step 04 and Step 05 validators use it without recounting alignment tags (`src/emrys/stages/duplicate_marking/validator.py:60-67,96-100`; `src/emrys/stages/split_n_cigar/validator.py:88-96,127-131`). ### A01-44 — Step 02 and Step 05 workers accept broader header text

`src/emrys/stages/split_n_cigar/step_05_split_n_cigar_reads.sh:84-93` uses grep and substring checks for coordinate sort and RG ID/SM, whereas `src/emrys/libraries/alignments/bam.py:56-65` requires exact tab-delimited tokens. For example, a sort token containing `SO:coordinate` only as part of a different field could pass the worker's header check and fail validation; other worker checks still apply. Native tests cover `SO:unknown` but not a field-token mutation (`tests/stages/split_n_cigar/test_step_05_split_n_cigar_reads.sh:238`).

The Step 02 canonical-BAM worker uses the same substring policy both to decide whether sorting and RG rewriting are needed and to validate the output (`src/emrys/stages/canonical_bam/step_02_sort_index_bam.sh:64-82,87-107,115-131`). A synthetic header `@HD\tVN:1.6\tSO:queryname\tXX:SO:coordinate` satisfies its `grep '^@HD.*SO:coordinate'`; if its remaining RG/count inputs and hard-link are valid, the worker can skip sorting, preserve the source bytes, and still pass its final substring check. Likewise, `@RG\tID:sample\tSM:other\tLB:sample\tPL:ILLUMINA\tZZ:SM:sample` satisfies the worker's `*SM:sample*` check although the actual `SM` token is different. The shared Python validator requires exact tab tokens and would reject these headers. Step 02's native test rejects `SO:unknown` without an extra matching field but has no such decoy-field case (`tests/stages/canonical_bam/test_step_02_sort_index_bam.sh:201-205`). These are source-inferred decision paths, not a native-BAM or full-Run reproduction. Preserve exact validator parsing, read-group record-count checks, and canonical fast-path behavior while deciding a caller-complete worker header policy and focused negative fixtures for both owners.
