Even renderability would not prove graph/data agreement.

### A01-50 — RSeQC reports are mechanical, not manifest policy

The Step 03 worker publishes nonempty RSeQC output; the validator checks three exact finite fractions and their sum (`src/emrys/evidence/rseqc_orientation/validator.py:59-76`). It receives no BAM, index, BED12, tool identity, or Attempt receipt, and no computational stage derives manifest strandedness from it (`src/emrys/evidence/rseqc_orientation/CONTRACT.md:18-29,72-107`). Producer and validator tests retain different malformed-output boundaries. Preserve those protections and the independent declared strandedness policy; a passing fraction report is not biological strand classification.

The shared fraction parser ignores other report lines (`src/emrys/libraries/evidence/qc.py:36-62`), and the owner contract explicitly excludes RSeQC's paired-end declaration from validation (`src/emrys/evidence/rseqc_orientation/CONTRACT.md:98-105`). A tiny direct owner fixture containing `This is SingleEnd Data` plus three valid paired-orientation fractions produced five passing rows. This fabricated contradiction clarifies the check's narrow meaning; it is not an observed native RSeQC output or a supported-Run failure. `report_structure` promises the required fraction labels, not input-type confirmation.

### A01-51 — Reporting duplicates QC lexical parsers with different results

`src/emrys/reporting/_artifact_index/inspection.py:259-294` parses flagstat and RSeQC separately from `src/emrys/libraries/evidence/qc.py:8-62`, used by their validators. The reporter rejects blank flagstat lines but overwrites duplicate total/mapped rows; the shared parser ignores blanks and reports duplicate rows. Reporter RSeQC accepts any matching fraction label, while the owner parser requires three exact labels and catches duplicates/nonfinite values. Metric projection and owner validation are different boundaries. Map artifact status and exact inputs before considering reuse of lexical parsing; do not silently unify pass/fail policy.

The divergence also runs the other way for numeric spelling. The owner parses finite `float()` values and checks three fractions plus their sum (`src/emrys/libraries/evidence/qc.py:36-61`; `src/emrys/evidence/rseqc_orientation/validator.py:59-76`). Reporting matches only plain unsigned decimals in `Fraction of reads` lines (`src/emrys/reporting/_artifact_index/inspection.py:278-294`). A tiny direct parser/regex probe with `1e-2`, `9.7e-1`, and `2e-2` returned owner values 0.01/0.97/0.02 with no errors and sum 1, while none of the three lines matched the reporting regex. With all three exponent forms the present artifact is classified failed for missing RSeQC metrics; with a mixed form, one matched decimal can let it remain complete while the exponent metrics are absent (`src/emrys/reporting/_artifact_index/inspection.py:190-213,278-294,347-385`). Both positive fixture families use plain decimals (`tests/evidence/rseqc_orientation/test_validate_step_03_rseqc_orientation.py:27-35,150-189`; `tests/reporting/fixtures/artifact_adapters_v1/build_fixture.py:825-830`). Decide whether every owner-valid finite numeric form must project complete reporting metrics, then add all-exponent and mixed-form adapter fixtures. Retain the owner's exact labels and sum check and the reporting source/transaction checks; this probe is not native RSeQC output or a full Run/report result.

### A01-52 — Shared reference parsers leak raw errors

`src/emrys/libraries/references/contigs.py:27-31,65-70` raises raw `IndexError` for a whitespace-only FASTA header and raw `ValueError` for a Unicode DICT length. Tests explicitly characterize both (`tests/libraries/test_reference_contigs.py:98-102,220-228`); focused local calls reproduced them. Project admission, Step 00c validation, and reference provenance catch their declared parser error but not both raw types (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/onboarding.py:789-796`; `src/emrys/stages/fasta_sidecars/validator.py:68-74`; `src/emrys/evidence/reference_provenance/_reference_contigs.py:105-123`). Provenance STAR-length parsing has a related `isdigit()`/`int()` path. Normalize malformed-input errors at the shared boundary or map all callers together, retaining valid contig/order semantics and controlled report behavior. The two direct tests currently expect the raw `IndexError` and `ValueError` (`tests/libraries/test_reference_contigs.py:98-102,220-228`); they characterize known diagnostic defects, not a supported promise to preserve those exception classes. An approved repair should replace their expectations with controlled `ReferenceContigError` or an owner-equivalent refusal, rather than deleting the malformed-input cases. Keep public Project, validator, and provenance diagnostics distinct from the direct helper oracle.

Step 00a's `parse_fasta` also calls that shared FASTA parser and translates only `ReferenceContigError` (`src/emrys/libraries/alignments/star.py:122-128`). Its report builder catches `ValueError`, not the whitespace-header `IndexError` (`src/emrys/stages/star_index/validator.py:116-129`), and the generic validation runtime does not catch it (`src/emrys/libraries/validation/runtime.py:63-72`). This is a source-composed public-validator diagnostic consequence of the reproduced helper fault; a full Step 00a CLI invocation with that header was not run.

### A01-53 — Step 07 receipt parsing can escape its exit contract

`src/emrys/libraries/validation/tsv.py:51-55` uses permissive `csv.DictReader` for the Step 07 receipt, then `src/emrys/stages/partitioned_cohort_mpileup/validator.py:125-169` directly indexes and assumes field values. The owner contract (`src/emrys/stages/partitioned_cohort_mpileup/CONTRACT.md:98-103`) records that some missing-field shapes can raise `KeyError` or `AttributeError` with traceback and exit 1 instead of controlled exit 2. This is source plus contract evidence, without a new runtime reproduction. Preserve exact receipt and check identities; evaluate the existing strict TSV reader only after its behavior is compared for every caller.

**Focused reader probe.** An exact receipt header followed by a row missing trailing `vcf_record_count` makes `csv.DictReader` return `None` for that field, which the validator calls `.isdigit()` on (`src/emrys/stages/partitioned_cohort_mpileup/validator.py:160-169`). The shared runtime does not catch `AttributeError` (`src/emrys/libraries/validation/runtime.py:14,63-72`). The producer emits complete rows and checks strict TSV (`src/emrys/stages/partitioned_cohort_mpileup/producer.py:347-381`); Run rejects a nonzero validator exit (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/task.py:2763-2769`). This is malformed standalone input and a diagnostic-exit defect, not a verified-Run bypass. The reader probe was not a full CLI execution.

### A01-54 — Public manifest pass is weaker than Run admission

The legacy public manifest validator lists `condition` as required but accepts a blank value, prints “Conditions: none,” and checks sample IDs only for nonempty uniqueness (`src/emrys/ingestion/sample_manifest_admission/validator.py:18-21,120-175`). Step 08 requires nonempty condition, replicate, and safe IDs (`src/emrys/contracts/scientific_evidence/step08.py:326-353`). Tiny temporary fixtures confirmed that blank condition and `S/1` pass the public helper but fail Step 08. The existing test named “empty required fields fail” covers sample ID and FASTQ fields, not condition (`tests/ingestion/sample_manifest_admission/test_validate_manifest.py:190-203`). Decide the public command's promised scope; preserve Step 08's stricter Run-boundary refusal and intentional optional-replicate distinction.

The optional `--check-files` branch tests only `Path.exists()` for each declared FASTQ path (`src/emrys/ingestion/sample_manifest_admission/validator.py:131-143`), so an existing directory can satisfy that check. Its public help promises path existence rather than regular-file or FASTQ-content validation (`:54-60`); direct fixtures use empty files and missing paths, not directories (`tests/ingestion/sample_manifest_admission/test_validate_manifest.py:100-121,228-264`). This is a scope decision, not a demonstrated violation of the current command promise. Guided Project admission separately hashes a regular file with identity checks (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/normalization.py:238-257,361-375`). Keep those stronger Project checks and decide whether the public helper should explicitly reject directories.

### A01-55 — Step 00c final pair check omits uniqueness

`src/emrys/stages/fasta_sidecars/step_00c_prepare_gatk_reference.sh:57-111,128-149` parses FAI and DICT rows, sorts their name/length pairs, and compares the multisets. It has no duplicate-name rejection, although `src/emrys/stages/fasta_sidecars/CONTRACT.md:41-45` promises unique names. The independent validator parses all three inputs and requires ordered FASTA agreement (`src/emrys/stages/fasta_sidecars/validator.py:68-100`). This is a source-level postprocessing gap, not proof that samtools or GATK emit duplicate sidecars in normal use. Retain the validator; decide whether a faulty-tool producer case should enforce the worker's uniqueness promise. Its unordered comparison is already disclosed in the contract.

The direct validator tests mutate a length and a malformed FAI, but do not swap two otherwise valid contigs (`tests/stages/fasta_sidecars/test_validate_step_00c_reference_sidecars.py:83-107`). The ordered validator code is still a surviving defense; the missing reorder fixture matters if the worker's multiset comparison is ever consolidated with it. Do not treat an untested mutation as an observed validator false pass.

Reporting has another order ceiling: its native FASTA/FAI/DICT adapters construct insertion-ordered contig dictionaries, then the private Step 00c reconciler compares the dictionaries with `==`, which ignores order (`src/emrys/reporting/_artifact_index/_text_genomic.py:80-173`; `src/emrys/reporting/_artifact_index/reconcile_native.py:118-137`). A two-contig FAI or DICT reorder with unchanged names and lengths can therefore pass that private comparison. The direct adapter mismatch test changes a FAI length, not a pure reorder (`tests/reporting/test_artifact_adapters.py:1356-1381`). The Step 00c owner still compares ordered lists, and Run requires its all-pass report (`src/emrys/stages/fasta_sidecars/validator.py:68-100`; `src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/task.py:2763-2807`); no supported-Run false completion is shown. Preserve that owner guard and decide whether the standalone artifact index claims ordered contig evidence before changing its parser.

### A01-56 — Duplicate Picard header can hide malformed data

`src/emrys/libraries/quality/picard.py:19-42` tests required header membership, then converts the header and row to a dictionary, so a later duplicate column overwrites the first. Step 04 uses that result for `duplication_metrics` (`src/emrys/stages/duplicate_marking/validator.py:68-70,102-107`). Preserve numeric bounds and native evidence checks. The reproduction did not execute Picard or a full Run.

**Producer boundary.** The Step 04 worker only requires nonempty metrics; interpretation belongs to the validator (`src/emrys/stages/duplicate_marking/CONTRACT.md:33-36,54-59`). Thus the duplicate-column probe reaches the owner report parser, but no native Picard or full Run was exercised. Keep native BAM checks and the normal Picard producer path while deciding whether unique metric headers are part of validation's promise.

### A01-57 — Validation stdout can precede a failed publication

The shared `--execute` path prints report bytes before rechecking input snapshots or publishing (`src/emrys/libraries/validation/runtime.py:27-49`). An isolated in-process fixture returned exit 2 and no report while stdout already contained a `pass` row after input mutation. Retain the authoritative exit, file, and hash-bound task evidence.

### A01-58 — BAM quickcheck report labels exceed the gate

`src/emrys/libraries/alignments/bam.py:32-44` treats `samtools quickcheck -v` as passing on exit 0 even with nonempty stderr, while Step 02, 04, and 05 report an expected value of "exit=0 with empty diagnostics" (`src/emrys/stages/canonical_bam/validator.py:99-104`; `src/emrys/stages/duplicate_marking/validator.py:84-89`; `src/emrys/stages/split_n_cigar/validator.py:115-120`). ### A01-59 — Profile nonroot pattern does not survive normalization

The v3-directory execution-profile schema's `absolute_nonroot_path` pattern accepts `/tmp/..` for `scratch_parent` and exact module init (`src/emrys/contracts/schemas/orchestration/v3/execution_profile.schema.json:17-20,37-40,120-121`). Source admission normalizes both with `os.path.abspath`, producing `/` (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/execution_profile.py:359-383`), and the placement document emits that normalized path (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/execution_profile.py:153-172`). The batch script independently rejects a root scratch parent before use and rejects `/` as a module-init file before sourcing it (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/slurm_submission.py:701-709,749-755`). This is an early-admission and diagnostic question, not evidence that a batch job sources the root. Decide whether profile admission itself must reject normalized root for both fields. ### A01-60 — Benchmark pass can mean process success only

The opt-in benchmark captures validator logs but sets trial `status=pass` from setup, producer, and validator exit codes plus optional artifact byte equality (`scripts/benchmark_stage_resources.py:461-510`). EMRYS validators can publish `status=fail` rows and return 0; Run separately invokes semantic all-pass (`src/emrys/libraries/validation/runtime.py:27-49`; `src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/task.py:2765-2807`). Source composition therefore implies a benchmark manifest using an EMRYS owner validator could count a failed report in a successful repetition and recommendation. Existing benchmark tests cover nonzero exits and a passing result, not zero-exit failed rows (`tests/test_benchmark_stage_resources.py:156-264`). A tiny synthetic benchmark fixture ran the actual trial and summary code with an in-memory stub for unavailable PyYAML: validator stdout contained a failed TSV row, yet the script exited 0, wrote `status=pass`, and recommended the value. No native stage or cluster run occurred. Keep the raw trial logs and advisory-only boundary; decide whether the helper needs exact owner-report semantics or should call its result process success. `docs/operations/RUNBOOK.md:770-774` currently calls it validation status.

**Protocol and retirement.** The benchmark manifest accepts arbitrary `validator_argv` and repetitions but no report path or semantic result field (`scripts/benchmark_stage_resources.py:31-40,88-97,170-200`). It never reads the captured validator output. The reproduced gap applies to manifests choosing an EMRYS owner validator; it is not automatic adoption or a claim about every validator protocol. If this helper remains in use, label process success honestly or use the existing owner all-pass gate. A new generic report parser needs separate justification because `SETUP-02` defers whole-helper retirement until campaign completion (`docs/tasks/backlog_matrix.md:173`).

The global and critical-owner nonregression checks remain (`tests/tools/python_coverage_baseline.py:407-424`). Decide who declares a new shared module and whether automation is needed before changing the floor or list. This is a gate-scope observation, not evidence of an uncovered new module.

`src/emrys/libraries/alignments/star.py:53-66` checks that each of three mapping percentages is syntactically valid and in 0..100; it does not reconcile their sum. `src/emrys/libraries/references/contigs.py:61-72` converts DICT `@SQ` fields to a dictionary, retaining the last value for duplicate tags. `tests/libraries/test_reference_contigs.py:29-46` explicitly characterizes `SN:old` followed by `SN:chr1` as `chr1`. ### A01-64 — Benchmark recommendation can omit failed repetitions

`scripts/benchmark_stage_resources.py:322-366` filters to passing trial rows before grouping resource values, then recommends by the passing subset's median. The exact summary helper, given one pass and one fail for the same case/value, wrote `successful_repetitions=1` and `recommended=yes`. The outer benchmark still exits nonzero when any trial fails (`scripts/benchmark_stage_resources.py:477-510`), and raw trial rows remain, so this is a misleading advisory summary rather than suppressed aggregate failure. `docs/tasks/backlog_matrix.md:173` and `docs/tasks/optimization_campaign.md:364-370` explicitly require rejecting a candidate with any failed repetition. Existing summary tests include an entirely failed value, not a mixed one (`tests/test_benchmark_stage_resources.py:267-310`). Preserve raw rows and exit behavior; condition recommendation on every planned repetition or label incomplete candidates.

**Recommendation decision.** Optional artifact-byte mismatch can also produce a failed row whose passing sibling still informs the summary (`scripts/benchmark_stage_resources.py:467-481`). The helper does not compare `successful_repetitions` with manifest `repetitions`. The campaign requires failed repetitions to remain visible and candidate adoption to consider all repetitions and review evidence (`docs/tasks/optimization_campaign.md:364-370,393-400`). Preserve raw rows and nonzero aggregate exit; suppress `recommended=yes` for any failed or missing planned repetition while this advisory helper remains supported.

### A01-65 — Launch exception can strand a local validation lane

The local `all-checks` target delegates to `tests/tools/run_validation.py` (`scripts/make_quality.mk:238-244`). `run_lanes` starts pending lanes until its concurrency limit; `start_lane` can raise while opening a log or spawning a process (`tests/tools/run_validation.py:269-314,391-394`). Its ordinary failure and signal paths terminate and reap process groups, but the outer `finally` restores handlers and only closes log handles (`:395-421,466-495`). A tiny two-lane fixture with a missing second executable raised `FileNotFoundError` while the first child wrote a marker afterward. A separate controlled probe injected `OSError` on the second launch, observed the first five-second child still running when `run_lanes` unwound, then killed and reaped it. The CLI catches only `ValidationError`, so a launch error also escapes its controlled summary (`:518-575`). Existing tests cover successful parallel lanes, peer failure, and SIGINT cleanup (`tests/test_validation_orchestrator.py:336-352,407-559`), not launch exceptions. Preserve ordinary cancellation and retained diagnostics; cancel and reap owned groups on internal launch failure. No full `make all-checks` or CI job was run.

### A01-73 — Saved settings can block diagnostic CLI flags

The public entry point loads a saved `.env` from the current directory or an ancestor before it parses any arguments (`src/emrys/__main__.py:349-373`). The loader rejects nonregular `.env` paths immediately and rejects malformed EMRYS-marked settings (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/onboarding.py:153-189`). Consequently a directory or symlink named `.env`, or a malformed saved EMRYS file, can return a settings error for `emrys --version` or `emrys --help` before either diagnostic flag is handled. The Runbook promises that `emrys --version` works from any directory without a Project or scientific runtime (`docs/operations/RUNBOOK.md:184-188`). Current version/help tests use empty temporary working directories (`tests/test_public_cli_contracts.py:713-735,782-800`); the unknown-setting refusal test protects the consuming-command boundary (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_onboarding.py:267-282`). This is a source-derived CLI availability mismatch, not a Run or scientific failure; a full installed CLI reproduction was unavailable in the local Python environment. Preserve strict saved-settings admission for commands that consume those defaults, and decide which global diagnostic flags must bypass it.

There is a separate availability ceiling before `main`: the module eagerly imports functional owners (`src/emrys/__main__.py:11-33`), then parses version/help only inside `main` (`:349-373`). A source-bound local `python3 -m emrys --version` stopped at an absent declared `jsonschema` dependency during import, before dispatch. This observes an incomplete local interpreter, not an installed-wheel failure; it shows why the current environment could not reproduce the saved-settings path through the full CLI. If version/help are intended for diagnosing a damaged environment, test that promise separately from the consuming-command dependency contract.

### A01-74 — Tracked settings example contradicts current setup

The root `.env.example` tells users to copy it to `.env` and fill Slurm and workspace variables (`.env.example:1-11`). The current public CLI loader only adopts a regular ancestor `.env` containing `EMRYS_ENV_VERSION=1` and closed keys; a regular copied example lacks that marker and is skipped (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/onboarding.py:107-108,153-190`). A pinned-tree search found no product reader for the sample's seven variable names. The supported `emrys setup --execute` path refuses to create its current settings when any `.env` already exists (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/onboarding.py:214-219`), a behavior protected by an existing test (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_onboarding.py:301-324`). Thus following the tracked sample can silently leave intended defaults unapplied and then block supported setup; it does not demonstrate a Run or execution-profile bypass. No full CLI sequence was run. Decide whether to retire this stale sample or replace its guidance with the current closed setup contract; preserve create-absent behavior and avoid deleting operator data.

### A01-75 — Numeric conversion escapes controlled validation

The shared Step 08 scientific-evidence parser accepts any-length ASCII decimal with a regex, then calls Python `int` (`src/emrys/contracts/scientific_evidence/step08.py:195-198`). Under the local default 4,300-digit conversion limit, a focused `step08.attempt` call with 5,000 digits raised uncaught `ValueError` instead of returning a failed check; its catch set omits that error (`src/emrys/contracts/scientific_evidence/step08.py:169-173`; `src/emrys/libraries/validation/tsv.py:206-213`). Step 08 and Step 09 validators use this parser for input/summary counts (`src/emrys/stages/cohort_candidate_preprocessing/validator.py:99-102,205-208`; `src/emrys/analyses/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking/validator.py:140-149,251-254`); Step 10 scientific context also calls it (`src/emrys/contracts/scientific_evidence/scientific_context.py:409-417`). The shared validation CLI runtime does not catch `ValueError` (`src/emrys/libraries/validation/runtime.py:14,63-72`). Reporting has a separate regex-then-`int` native-field reader (`src/emrys/reporting/_artifact_index/reconcile_native.py:33-39`), while its per-scope reconciliation catches only `ArtifactIndexError` (`src/emrys/reporting/_artifact_index/reconciliation.py:72-88`); that reporting consequence is source inference because its full import was unavailable locally. Ordinary numeric tests cover valid and short invalid forms (`tests/contracts/scientific_evidence/test_step08.py:378-413`). This is a malformed-input diagnostic and scope-classification gap, not evidence that a supported Run accepts corrupt data. Preserve the distinct scientific-evidence and reporting trust boundaries while normalizing bounded decimal conversion.

The same conversion ceiling reaches two other owner inputs. Step 02b's flagstat parser matches any-length ASCII counts, then converts without a `ValueError` guard (`src/emrys/libraries/evidence/qc.py:8-33`); a focused 5,000-digit total raised raw `ValueError` before its validator could build a failed report (`src/emrys/evidence/canonical_bam_qc/validator.py:54-68`). Step 07 region parsing likewise converts an unbounded numeric match (`src/emrys/libraries/validation/mpileup.py:98-125`); a focused `selector_ok` call with `chr1:` followed by 5,000 digits escaped its `ValidationError` catch (`:130-141`). Project setup and Project admission also catch only that controlled error around the same parser (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/onboarding.py:1063-1067,1739-1746`). Existing helper tests cover short malformed selectors and ordinary flagstat duplicates (`tests/libraries/test_shared_domain_helpers.py:190-197,308-325`). These probes do not execute full owner commands or prove normal tool output can contain such values; they extend the malformed-input fault map without merging the distinct evidence and Project boundaries.

An adjacent reporting input has a different conversion failure: `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` is parsed as an integer with a controlled `ValueError`, but an integer outside the platform timestamp range can raise raw `OverflowError` at `datetime.fromtimestamp` (`src/emrys/reporting/_artifact_index/core.py:57-69`). A temporary standard-library probe with a 29-digit integer reproduced that exception; the artifact-index builder calls this helper at both start and finish (`src/emrys/reporting/_artifact_index/context.py:164,189`). Committed reporting fixtures set an ordinary fixed epoch (`tests/reporting/test_artifact_adapters.py:119-147,701`). This is a malformed environment diagnostic, not an artifact-index false pass or a claim about a normal Run. Preserve deterministic timestamps while defining the representable epoch range and controlled error type.

The orchestration contract JSON loader has the same diagnostic shape at a different authority boundary. It converts admitted bytes with `json.loads` but catches `UnicodeError` and `JSONDecodeError`, not the `ValueError` raised by Python's integer-string limit (`src/emrys/contracts/orchestration/api.py:124-138`). On local Python 3.14.5 with its default 4,300-digit limit, a standard-library parse of a JSON object containing a 5,000-digit integer raised raw `ValueError`. Canonical-record admission wraps only `ContractValidationError` (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/_inspection_admission.py:137-151`), so the controlled record-error path is not established for this malformed byte shape. Direct loader tests cover duplicate keys and non-object input, but no oversized numeric token (`tests/contracts/orchestration/test_orchestration_contracts.py:1266-1285`). This is a source and standard-library characterization, not an execution of the owner loader or a false Run admission; retain duplicate-key, canonical-byte, and schema defenses while defining the numeric error boundary.

The artifact-contract JSON loader has an independent copy of that error boundary. It catches `UnicodeError` and `JSONDecodeError` but not `ValueError` around `json.loads` (`src/emrys/contracts/artifacts/_artifact_contracts/schema.py:40-52`). An isolated extraction of the pinned loader functions admitted a ten-digit numeric field and raised raw `ValueError` for a 5,000-digit value. The public artifact-contract CLI catches only `ContractValidationError` when validating a named document (`src/emrys/contracts/artifacts/validator.py:47-61,93-114`); its ordinary malformed-input tests omit this numeric shape (`tests/contracts/artifacts/test_artifact_schema_contracts.py:200-234,239-259`). This is controlled-diagnostic failure for a malformed record, not false schema admission or a completed report transaction. Retain duplicate-key, nonstandard-constant, and exact inventory checks while correcting the artifact owner boundary.

Application-log Slurm correlation independently converts an unbounded ASCII decimal with `int` before classifying the identifier (`src/emrys/libraries/application_logging/helpers.py:206-235,297-309`). An oversized job ID would raise the same raw `ValueError` before any log file opens (`src/emrys/libraries/application_logging/handler.py:160-165`), although Run's immediate caller catches `ValueError` and reports a logging failure (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/control.py:1259-1283`). Direct helper tests cover ordinary IDs, leading zero, and incomplete array identity, not an oversized decimal (`tests/libraries/application_logging/test_helpers.py:311-328`). This is a source-inferred helper error-type gap, not a lost Run receipt or evidence that Slurm emits such IDs; keep the early no-storage refusal and caller-level failure handling.

Storage qualification has the same malformed-JSON-number diagnostic gap at its own evidence boundary. `_json_object` catches decode and syntax errors, but Python's 4,300-digit integer limit raises `ValueError` for a 5,000-digit JSON integer (`src/emrys/evidence/storage_inventory/qualification.py:330-337`). A direct pinned-owner helper probe reproduced that raw exception; the public storage command catches only `StorageQualificationError` (`src/emrys/evidence/storage_inventory/qualification.py:881-913`). This is lost controlled diagnosis for malformed receipt data, not a false qualification pass. Preserve direct and two-phase receipt identities and their separate fault tests while admitting numeric conversion errors as malformed input.

### A01-76 — Step 06 counts reader misses row-width faults

The Step 06 counts reader uses permissive `csv.DictReader` through `read_tsv`, checks the exact header, then reads named cells without checking each row's width (`src/emrys/libraries/validation/tsv.py:51-55`; `src/emrys/libraries/alignments/orientation.py:74-98`). A tiny direct counts fixture with one extra trailing cell returned a typed row, and the owner report fixture produced five passing checks (`src/emrys/stages/mechanical_orientation/validator.py:70-129`). Removing only the trailing `assigned_fraction` cell makes `float(None)` raise uncaught `TypeError`, rather than a controlled malformed-input result. The producer writes an exact row (`src/emrys/stages/mechanical_orientation/producer.py:157-169`), and reporting's generic TSV reader rejects unequal row width (`src/emrys/reporting/_artifact_index/_text_tabular.py:31-59`). These surviving boundaries do not make the standalone owner report precise. No supported Run or full reporting transaction used the malformed fixture. Preserve the five check identities and require exact row width at the owner boundary.

### A01-77 — Valid Step 06 rounded fraction can fail validation

The Step 06 producer writes `assigned_records/input_records` to six decimal places (`src/emrys/stages/mechanical_orientation/producer.py:140-169`). The owner validator compares the parsed float to the unrounded ratio with a strict `<= 0.0000005` tolerance (`src/emrys/stages/mechanical_orientation/validator.py:103-114`). For valid counts `input=2,000,000` and `assigned=800,003`, the producer writes `0.400002`, while binary-float subtraction from `0.4000015` is `5.000000000143778e-7`; the assigned arithmetic check failed in a tiny direct validator fixture. Reporting uses `5.000001e-7` for the same producer format (`src/emrys/reporting/_artifact_index/reconcile_native.py:175-183`). Run requires semantic all-pass (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/task.py:2763-2807`), so the supported-Run impact is source-composed inference, not a completed Run reproduction. Retain exact count arithmetic; choose a decimal/rounding rule shared by producer, owner validator, and reporter.

### A01-78 — Distinct GTF IDs can collide in BED names

Step 00b groups exon rows by the original transcript ID but replaces whitespace with underscores when building BED names (`src/emrys/stages/gtf_to_bed12/converter.py:104-112,161-180,227-256`). A tiny two-exon source fixture with `transcript_id "tx A"` and `transcript_id "tx_A"`, both in one gene, produced duplicate `tx_A|g` names. The owner test establishes that whitespace IDs are supported input (`tests/stages/gtf_to_bed12/test_gtf_to_bed12.py:177-206`). Project admission normalizes the GTF and checks nonempty models, contigs, and bounds, but not normalized-name uniqueness (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/onboarding.py:1717-1736`). The Step 00b validator later has a separate unique-name check that would fail (`src/emrys/stages/gtf_to_bed12/validator.py:50-58,83-88`). No full Run was executed. Decide whether Project rejects the colliding input or normalization refuses it before publication; preserve the validator as an independent boundary.

The direct validator suite asserts the `unique_transcript_names` row in its passing roster but has no duplicate-name negative (`tests/stages/gtf_to_bed12/test_validate_step_00b_bed12.py:90-101,125-162`). That is an owner-test gap, not evidence that the validator accepts duplicate names. A small collision fixture would protect a later admission/normalization change without replacing the converter's literal output oracle.

### A01-79 — Runtime probe display limit does not bound capture

The runtime tool probe uses `subprocess.run` with `stdout=PIPE` and merged stderr, then slices the completed output to its detail limit (`src/emrys/evidence/runtime_availability/_probes.py:75-109`). A noisy executable can therefore fill memory during a timed probe before truncation. Doctor and lifecycle runtime admission call this probe owner (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/doctor.py:603-613`; `src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/lifecycle.py:1277-1300`). The existing timeout test verifies elapsed/detail behavior, not capture memory (`tests/evidence/runtime_availability/test_runtime_availability.py:610-658`). This is source inference; no flooding executable was run. Retain timeouts and version/namespace evidence, and assess a bounded capture mechanism under the current runtime owner rather than adding another probe path.

### A01-80 — Final storage receipt admits incomplete capacity fields

The storage owner defines a full root-snapshot field set, and its producer records capacity fields with root identity (`src/emrys/evidence/storage_inventory/qualification.py:41-55,389-403`). Retained two-phase final admission checks only seven cross-node stable identity fields in each root snapshot (`src/emrys/evidence/storage_inventory/qualification.py:405-411,856-870`). A focused in-memory call accepted matching identity with missing total/free capacity fields and a nonnumeric available-capacity field. Direct receipt admission separately requires the exact root field set (`src/emrys/evidence/storage_inventory/qualification.py:773-794`). Final admission still checks top-level shape, compute-receipt hash binding, identity, and ordered roots (`src/emrys/evidence/storage_inventory/qualification.py:817-866`); an already-bound Run projects the admitted receipt hash into its runtime identity (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/doctor.py:291-302`). The full local receipt probe below demonstrates final-admission acceptance, not Run acceptance. Define final evidence field presence and type separately from capacity-value equality, since free space legitimately changes.

The two-phase owner tests execute both phases on one temporary host by setting and unsetting `SLURM_JOB_ID` (`tests/evidence/storage_inventory/test_storage_inventory.py:360-382`). The code requires a nonempty job ID for compute and its absence for finalize, but its local test does not prove distinct worker/head hosts or cross-node visibility (`src/emrys/evidence/storage_inventory/qualification.py:518-547,640-659`). Preserve receipt, probe, hash, root, and UID/GID checks; the live institutional placement claim requires separate site evidence.

Final admission checks `compute_receipt.path` and `sha256`, compares `compute` with the bound compute receipt, and checks UID/GID in `head`, but does not close the `compute_receipt` or `head` key sets (`src/emrys/evidence/storage_inventory/qualification.py:837-855`). Extra fields therefore remain admissible at those nested locations; direct receipt `context` has exact key closure (`:759-771`). The retained final-receipt hash still binds the whole admitted file into Run identity. The full local receipt probe below covers capacity-field mutation; nested key closure and Run admission were not exercised.

The compute receipt reader requires exact `compute` keys, but does not check that `host` and `slurm_job_id` have the nonempty string values its producer writes (`src/emrys/evidence/storage_inventory/qualification.py:480-515,518-545`). The final receipt reader binds the complete compute bytes by hash but compares only UID/GID in both `compute` and `head` contexts; a missing final `head.host` is not rejected there (`:837-855`). The retained probe rechecks seven stable root-identity fields, not the complete recorded capacity snapshot (`:595-600,405-411`). This extends the evidence-shape question beyond capacity to site attribution; it does not show an unbound or forged Run, and the one-host test cannot establish institutional placement.

**Full local receipt probe.** In a disposable temporary directory, a source-bound two-phase fixture used a synthetic mount identity and a fake `SLURM_JOB_ID` to create compute and head receipts. After successful local finalization, removing `filesystem_total_bytes` and replacing `filesystem_available_bytes` with `"not-a-number"` in one final root still let `admit_final_qualification` return the mutated final receipt (`src/emrys/evidence/storage_inventory/qualification.py:817-870`). This upgrades the earlier in-memory snapshot observation to a complete retained-receipt admission example. It did not use Slurm, distinct hosts, a production filesystem, Doctor, or a Run; the mutation and its evidence were confined to the temporary fixture.

Both compute-receipt and direct-admission checks also accept any 64-character string for `source_sha256` and `visible_sha256`, without a lowercase-hex requirement (`src/emrys/evidence/storage_inventory/qualification.py:512-515,789-792`). The producer writes hexdigests (`:470-476`), while the compute mutation test uses only a short digest (`tests/evidence/storage_inventory/test_storage_inventory.py:628-668`). A 64-character non-hex mutation such as repeated `z` is therefore a specific missing lexical oracle. This is malformed-evidence admission scope, not a claim that retained probe bytes are independently rehashed or that a forged Run was demonstrated; preserve the exact receipt-byte binding and root-identity checks.

### A01-81 — PID existence is weaker than Run owner identity

After lock/Attempt binding and host checks, local Run inspection calls `process_is_alive` and labels a true result `local live owner` (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/inspection.py:298-353`). That helper uses only `os.kill(pid, 0)` (`src/emrys/libraries/process_environment.py:55-64`); the Attempt binds host and PID but no process-start identity (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/lifecycle.py:1509-1521`). If the original process dies and the host reuses its PID, inspection can label the unrelated process as the lock owner and set `running` (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/inspection.py:440-451`). Existing tests inject live/dead Boolean responses, not PID reuse (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_lifecycle.py:3575-3635`). This is source inference, not a reproduced live PID reuse. Structural lock admission, remote/dead refusal, and no automatic recovery remain defenses; the false label does not fabricate Results or authorize resumption. Define the needed identity evidence or state the observable liveness limit.

### A01-82 — Same-base Step 09 target passes Python contract

The Step 09 Python projection and result checks require `target_rna_change` to match `[ACGT]>[ACGT]` but do not require different bases (`src/emrys/contracts/scientific_evidence/step09.py:571-579,890-905`). Focused helper calls accepted `A>A` with otherwise consistent empty candidates and paired samples. Project creation and semantic policy admission, followed by the R producer, explicitly reject equal target bases (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/onboarding.py:896-902`; `src/emrys/contracts/orchestration/api.py:402-420`; `src/emrys/analyses/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking/step_09_cmh_editing_site_calling.R:25-32`). The scientific figure path separately requires its target in the admitted mutation spectrum (`src/emrys/reporting/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking_report/figures.py:574-585`). This is a standalone/malformed historical Python-contract gap; no supported Run or full report accepted `A>A` in this audit. Align the target grammar at each trust boundary while retaining producer refusal and numerical reconciliation.

### A01-83 — Scientific derivative files lack repository-wide ignore patterns

The tracked `.gitignore` ignores BAM/CRAM and FASTQ suffixes plus standard `results/`, `Projects/`, and raw-data directories, but has no VCF/BCF or adjacent BAM/CRAM index suffix rule (`.gitignore:1-26`). A read-only `git check-ignore --no-index -v` probe found no match for `data/test/prod.vcf`, `data/test/prod.vcf.gz`, `data/test/prod.bcf`, `data/test/prod.bam.bai`, or `data/test/prod.cram.crai`, nor their counterparts under `scratch/`; a BAM in scratch and VCFs under `results/` or `Projects/` were ignored. The root and agent rules prohibit committing production variant and alignment data (`README.md:103-105`; `AGENTS.md:65-66`), and none of these derivative files is tracked at the pinned commit. This is an accidental-staging defense gap outside standard output locations, not an observed data leak; ignore rules also do not replace review or prevent forced addition. Decide which derivative suffixes and intentionally tiny fixture locations a repository rule should cover.

### A01-84 — Isolated child tests do not prove package identity

The Matplotlib determinism test starts two `sys.executable -I -c` children from unrelated temporary directories, imports `emrys.reporting...figures`, and compares figure hashes and environment state without recording `figures.__file__` or another source identity (`tests/reporting/test_figures.py:198-279`). Isolated Python ignores the parent's `PYTHONPATH`; a local source-bound test can therefore fail if no package is installed or probe a stale installed package if one exists. A tiny isolated import from a foreign directory found no installed `emrys` in the current system Python; it was not the full figure test. Hosted CI first synchronizes the locked project environment (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:919-933`), and same-process figure tests still exercise their selected import, so this does not establish a hosted CI false pass. Require an explicit child package-path or revision assertion if the test promises cross-process behavior of the checked-out source, while preserving its distinct determinism and no-home-write checks.

The installed logging-delegate smoke similarly invokes `sys.executable -I -c`, skips when the child reports no installed `emrys`, and asserts event order and stream separation without recording the imported package's path or revision (`tests/libraries/application_logging/test_installed_smoke.py:13-87`). It can therefore skip locally or pass against an older installed package while the changed logging source remains untested by this case. The full public-CLI suite has a separate parent/child package-path comparison (`tests/test_public_cli_contracts.py:713-734`), and hosted full-suite shards first synchronize the locked project (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:919-933`). Those are surviving defenses, so this is a standalone/subset identity ceiling rather than a demonstrated complete-CI false pass. Retain the logging event-order and stream assertions and bind the child when claiming coverage of the checked-out revision.

The same isolated-child attribution question applies to Step 02b and Step 03 validator CLI tests: their helpers invoke `sys.executable -I -m emrys` from arbitrary working directories and compare owner reports, while their parent tests import checkout modules (`tests/evidence/canonical_bam_qc/test_validate_step_02b_bam_qc.py:12-13,73-92,141-175`; `tests/evidence/rseqc_orientation/test_validate_step_03_rseqc_orientation.py:12-13,65-84,150-183`). Every sampled direct Python stage-validator helper has the same isolated child pattern for Steps 00a, 00b, 00c, 01, 02, 04, 05, 06, 07, and 08 (`tests/stages/star_index/test_validate_step_00a_star_index.py:69-99`; `tests/stages/gtf_to_bed12/test_validate_step_00b_bed12.py:39-55`; `tests/stages/fasta_sidecars/test_validate_step_00c_reference_sidecars.py:56-75`; `tests/stages/star_alignment/test_validate_step_01_star_alignment.py:63-82`; `tests/stages/canonical_bam/test_validate_step_02_canonical_bam.py:79-98`; `tests/stages/duplicate_marking/test_validate_step_04_mark_duplicates.py:118-137`; `tests/stages/split_n_cigar/test_validate_step_05_split_ncigar.py:119-138`; `tests/stages/mechanical_orientation/test_validate_step_06_orientation_outputs.py:96-115`; `tests/stages/partitioned_cohort_mpileup/test_validate_step_07_mpileup_outputs.py:173-186`; `tests/stages/cohort_candidate_preprocessing/test_validate_step_08_preprocessing_outputs.py:212-225`). The Step 00b converter subprocess also uses an isolated installed command (`tests/stages/gtf_to_bed12/test_gtf_to_bed12.py:13-29`). The standalone manifest CLI suite uses the same pattern from the repository root (`tests/ingestion/sample_manifest_admission/test_validate_manifest.py:15-22`). In a subset/local run, exact report rows could come from another installed revision. Bind child package identity before attributing those subprocess assertions to the checkout; retain foreign-CWD, report, and command-line fault checks and the separate full-suite package-path defense. No child validator was run for this extension.

The artifact-contract CLI helper likewise invokes the isolated module from a foreign working directory but does not compare the child package path or revision with the checkout (`tests/contracts/artifacts/test_artifact_schema_contracts.py:140-155,197-245,771-809`). Its foreign-CWD and malformed-record checks remain useful. The same locked hosted setup is a separate defense; this extension identifies a local/subset source-attribution limit, without claiming a hosted false pass.

Reference-provenance command tests use the same isolated `sys.executable -I -m emrys` route and intentionally run from a foreign directory (`tests/evidence/reference_provenance/test_reference_provenance.py:13-29,146-166,196-263`). Their literal contig, status, no-write, and repeat assertions protect useful owner behavior; the parent also imports the checkout reconciler for a separate direct parser fixture (`tests/evidence/reference_provenance/test_reference_provenance.py:10-13,268-289`). Neither establishes that the isolated child loaded that checkout in a local subset run. Bind child identity when attributing the command results to the changed source, while retaining foreign-CWD and direct-owner checks. This is the existing A01-84 attribution ceiling, not a new reference-provenance semantic defect or a hosted CI result.

### A01-85 — Direct real-R target can pass with no R execution

`make real-r-test` calls three wrapper scripts without requiring an R executable first (`scripts/make_quality.mk:87-90`). Each wrapper prints `SKIP` and exits zero when the default `Rscript` is absent (`tests/stages/cohort_candidate_preprocessing/run_step_08_vcf_preprocessing_tests.sh:7-33`; `tests/analyses/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking/run_step_09_cmh_tests.sh:7-27`; `tests/analyses/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking/scientific_context_projection/run_scientific_context_projection_tests.sh:7-24`). Thus a successful direct target can represent zero real-R cases, even though the Make inventory classifies it as a local gate (`tests/test_public_cli_contracts.py:182-205`). This is a source characterization, not an observed hosted skip. CI restores R and invokes `validation-guarded-r`, whose `local-real-r-test` supplies an explicit `RSCRIPT_BIN_OVERRIDE` so missing R fails (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:233-286`; `scripts/make_quality.mk:112-124,195-197`). Decide whether the direct convenience target promises optional execution or a required pass; retain the hosted guarded computation check.

### A01-86 — Unused test fake-tool controls add no current coverage

Ten test-owned fake-tool controls have no selecting in-repository case: `FAKE_FAIL_TOOL=STAR` in Step 00a; `STAR_MUTATE_INDEX_FILE` in Step 01; `FAKE_RG_MODE=missing`, `FAKE_QUICKCHECK_FAIL_MATCH`, and `FAKE_TOTAL_RECORDS` in Step 02; `FAKE_JAVA_MODE=partial_failure` and `FAKE_SAMTOOLS_INDEX_EXIT` in Step 04; `FAKE_GATK_FAIL` and `FAKE_QUICKCHECK_FAIL` in Step 05; and `FAKE_R_MARKER` in Step 10 (`tests/stages/star_index/test_step_00a_build_star_index.py:56-58,94-97`; `tests/stages/star_alignment/test_step_01_star_align.sh:40-42`; `tests/stages/canonical_bam/test_step_02_sort_index_bam.sh:30-65,146-148,202-203`; `tests/stages/duplicate_marking/test_step_04_mark_duplicates.sh:71-85,126-130`; `tests/stages/split_n_cigar/test_step_05_split_n_cigar_reads.sh:94-97,155-161`; `tests/analyses/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking/scientific_context_projection/test_scientific_context_projection.sh:22-24`). A pinned-tree literal search found definitions but no selecting invocation. None currently protects a fault.

The Step 10 marker branch is the clearest retirement candidate: that same shell fixture records R arguments and asserts final-path identity plus five staged outputs (`tests/analyses/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking/scientific_context_projection/test_scientific_context_projection.sh:23-24,72-80`). Removing the unselected marker preserves its supported handoff checks and bad-receipt refusal. The other controls may represent distinct untested faults: Step 00a's incomplete-index case is not a native nonzero exit (`tests/stages/star_index/test_step_00a_build_star_index.py:183-197`); Step 05's worker GATK and quickcheck failures differ from its exercised bad sort/Java/BAI cases and the direct validator quickcheck report (`tests/stages/split_n_cigar/test_step_05_split_n_cigar_reads.sh:223-241`; `tests/stages/split_n_cigar/test_validate_step_05_split_ncigar.py:224-235`). Compare task staging/recovery and each worker contract before activating representative cases or removing the controls. Keep the shell-owner lane and existing assertions.

### A01-87 — Reference reconciliation buffers large declared files

The reference-provenance observer calls unbounded `read_bytes` for every listed artifact, then hashes the returned bytes (`src/emrys/evidence/reference_provenance/_reference_contigs.py:20-47`). The inventory includes a required STAR `Genome` member (`configs/reference_provenance.example.tsv:9`), while the owner test substitutes five bytes (`tests/evidence/reference_provenance/test_reference_provenance.py:38-50`). On `--execute`, reconciliation observes all items again before publication (`src/emrys/evidence/reference_provenance/reconciler.py:132-165`). `read_bytes` accumulates chunks and joins them without a size limit; an existing `sha256_with_identity` can stream a digest under the same no-follow identity checks (`src/emrys/libraries/validation/inputs.py:58-90,149-211`). Separately, FASTA, GTF, and BED12 contig readers load whole text files (`src/emrys/libraries/references/contigs.py:18-19`; `src/emrys/evidence/reference_provenance/_reference_contigs.py:61-70`). This is a source-based memory and repeated-I/O risk, not a measured large-reference failure. Preserve exact digests, sizes, empty-file refusal, path identity, second-observation drift checks, and contig semantics; a streaming hash alone would not bound every parser.

The Step 00a validator has an adjacent reference-scale ceiling: it parses the full FASTA and calls `stable_text` on the GTF (`src/emrys/stages/star_index/validator.py:116-129`). The FASTA helper loads the entire file and splits it into a line list (`src/emrys/libraries/alignments/star.py:122-128`; `src/emrys/libraries/validation/inputs.py:46-55`), whereas Project reference admission already passes a streaming file iterator to the same contig parser (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/onboarding.py:773-795`). Its direct tiny fixture uses a four-base FASTA and a small GTF (`tests/stages/star_index/test_step_00a_build_star_index.py:151-180`), so no reference-scale memory result is available. Keep ordered contig comparison, input snapshots, and GTF identity while assessing a bounded reader; do not infer an observed out-of-memory Run.

### A01-88 — Passing reference evidence can misstate its source path

Reference inventory admission accepts explicit paths and provenance values with repeated internal spaces (`src/emrys/evidence/reference_provenance/_reference_inventory.py:43-47,65-114`). The artifact TSV includes declared and resolved paths, but its writer applies the diagnostic `clean` helper to every field, collapsing whitespace (`src/emrys/evidence/reference_provenance/_reference_render.py:22-46`; `src/emrys/libraries/validation/report.py:32-33`). A complete tiny FASTA/FAI/DICT/GTF/BED12/STAR fixture ran `reconcile_from_args(..., execute=True)` in a temporary directory: it exited zero, published a passing summary and artifact TSV, and recorded `ref space/genome.fa` although the inspected `ref  space/genome.fa` existed and the emitted path did not. This is a direct small-owner reproduction, not a supported Run or institution test. The summary's profile SHA-256 still binds original inventory bytes; it does not make the human/path projection exact. Preserve TSV shape and diagnostic cleanup while retaining exact machine path and provenance fields, or reject values that the representation cannot preserve.

### A01-89 — Private HTML receipt reader leaks malformed-input exceptions

The receipt reader expects each TSV row to carry canonical JSON, but a missing final cell becomes `None` through `csv.DictReader`; `json.loads(None)` raises `TypeError` outside its `JSONDecodeError` handler (`src/emrys/reporting/_run_report/receipt.py:188-207`). A singleton `[]` or `null` JSON value reaches `validate_receipt` and raises `AttributeError` at `document.get` before schema validation (`:28-39`). Tiny standard-library probes confirmed these transitions; the full owner reader could not be imported in the available Python because `jsonschema` is absent. Existing direct tests cover a wrong header, zero rows, two disagreeing JSON cells, and invalid JSON, but not a singleton missing or nonobject value (`tests/reporting/test_report.py:559-595`). Direct private `validate_report_transaction` catches only `ReportRenderError` (`src/emrys/reporting/transaction_validation.py:520-550`); the outer `validate_receipt` transaction wrapper catches general exceptions and converts them to `ReportingTransactionError` (`:805-856`). This is an internal diagnostic/test gap, not a demonstrated public Run evidence bypass. Normalize malformed cells and JSON type at the reader while preserving exact receipt bytes and outer transaction refusal.

### A01-90 — Workflow rule-roster assertion filters unknown rules

The test named `test_profile_and_rule_rosters_are_exact_and_output_only_verified_state` invokes real Snakemake `--list-rules`, but retains only names already in its expected set before comparison (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_workflow.py:423-429`). An additional listed rule would be discarded, so this assertion would still pass. The DAG count/edge and processing-only comparisons also select known owner labels (`:261-268,386-394`); they establish the current known graph rather than absence of an extra disconnected rule. The pinned Snakefile currently declares 12 processing rules, a generic analysis rule, and the `cohort_slice` target (`src/emrys/workflow/Snakefile:394-446`); no unknown rule or supported-Run failure was observed. Preserve the real-engine DAG, reuse, and recovery fault tests, the literal profile/Stage Map/template checks (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_profile.py:27-126,271-322`), and Snakefile's independent fixed processing rule/scope fence (`src/emrys/workflow/Snakefile:149-178`). Make the complete rule-list assertion compare all parsed rules without creating another registry.

### A01-91 — Public manifest decoding and CSV errors escape controlled validation

The public manifest command checks file presence/type, then opens and iterates its TSV with the interpreter's default text encoding; it does not normalize decoding or read failures (`src/emrys/ingestion/sample_manifest_admission/validator.py:64-80,102-154`). `validate_from_args` catches only `ValidationError` (`src/emrys/ingestion/sample_manifest_admission/validator.py:178-190`). A tiny valid-header file containing byte `0xff` caused a source-bound `validate_from_args` call under the local UTF-8 locale to raise `UnicodeDecodeError` rather than print `Manifest validation failed` and return 1. A separate tiny 140,000-character TSV field exceeded `csv.DictReader`'s default 131,072-byte field limit and raised raw `_csv.Error` during iteration (`src/emrys/ingestion/sample_manifest_admission/validator.py:77-102`). Direct tests cover headers, values, duplicates, and missing FASTQ paths, but neither malformed-input shape (`tests/ingestion/sample_manifest_admission/test_validate_manifest.py:146-241`). The public CLI imports declared dependencies before this owner can run, so a full installed command was not reproduced locally. This is an error-reporting boundary, separate from A01-54's accepted-value gap; it does not show Run admission of bad data. Preserve the current schema, row, and optional file-existence checks while defining explicit decoding, CSV, and controlled read-error behavior.

### A01-92 — Top-level R cleanup hook leaves a diagnostic PDF

The explicit R environment check creates and verifies a temporary PDF, then registers `on.exit(unlink(pdf_path), add = TRUE)` at script top level (`scripts/check_r_environment.R:128-158`). A tiny temporary script executed by `Rscript --vanilla` created a file, registered the same top-level cleanup, exited zero, and left the file present; the surrounding temporary directory was then removed. Thus the intended PDF cleanup is ineffective even after a passing probe, and a later failure can leave the artifact as well. The script's Make route (`scripts/make_quality.mk:101-112`) and shell contract test check command wiring and guarded selection, not PDF removal (`tests/shell/test_local_r_environment.sh:225-294`). Hosted guarded R restores and executes real R (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:253-286`), but no retained cleanup assertion was identified. Retain the headless PDF signature/EOF proof and nonbootstrapping library checks; place cleanup in a function scope or remove the owned file explicitly.

### A01-93 — Artifact inventory admits a normalized-away dot segment

`validate_resolved_path` promises to reject both `.` and `..` path components, but constructs `Path(value)` before checking `path.parts` (`src/emrys/contracts/artifacts/_artifact_contracts/identity.py:43-66`). A standard-library probe shows `Path('a/./b').parts` becomes `('a', 'b')`, so the raw `.` is no longer visible. The public artifact-inventory TSV route calls this helper without an artifact JSON Schema check (`src/emrys/contracts/artifacts/_artifact_contracts/inventory.py:36-40,67-80,139-155`; `src/emrys/contracts/artifacts/validator.py:73-82,96-114`), and orchestration's projected inventory uses it too (`src/emrys/contracts/orchestration/artifact_inventory.py:153-171`). The artifact JSON path schema separately rejects dot segments (`src/emrys/contracts/schemas/artifacts/v1/common.schema.json:19-23`), and run-summary validation applies that schema before semantic reconciliation (`src/emrys/reporting/_run_summary/validation.py:13-44`). Inventory also rejects later canonical aliases (`src/emrys/contracts/artifacts/_artifact_contracts/inventory.py:144-155`). Existing tests cover `..` via schema, redundant separators, aliases, and templates, but not a `./` inventory row (`tests/contracts/artifacts/test_artifact_schema_contracts.py:303-313,518-556,588-663`). This is a source-derived lexical admission mismatch, not a demonstrated complete-Run bypass; align the raw-component rule across inventory and profile projection while retaining the downstream defenses.

### A01-94 — Shard duration estimates admit nonfinite values

The test-only shard planner reads its JSON baseline with Python's permissive decoder, then requires positive `int` or `float` values without checking finiteness (`tests/tools/python_test_shards.py:66-90`). A tiny temporary baseline with `default_seconds: NaN` loaded successfully; planning two fake node IDs produced `nan` estimates, the receipt serialized `"estimated_seconds": NaN`, and `verify_receipts` accepted the complete disjoint selection (`:130-186,299-357`). Python booleans also satisfy its numeric type check, while a top-level JSON list reaches `.get` before the controlled `ShardError` handler (`:66-90,406-430`). The pinned baseline contains ordinary finite values (`tests/baselines/python_test_durations.json:1-9`), and direct tests use finite estimates and check stale node IDs, not these malformed shapes (`tests/test_python_test_shards.py:18-45,126-168`). CI separately requires every shard to succeed and verifies exact node coverage (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:974-991`); this fixture does not show a skipped test or a passing failed shard. It shows that reviewed scheduling metadata can become nonstandard JSON or produce an uncontrolled diagnostic. Require finite positive weights and a mapping root at baseline admission, retaining complete/disjoint receipts and the independent shard-success gate.
`verify_receipts` never reads `estimated_seconds`, and its `isinstance(..., int)` checks allow Python booleans as `shard_count` or `shard_index` (`tests/tools/python_test_shards.py:299-357`). An inconsistent estimate or Boolean coordinate can therefore remain in accepted metadata if the exact node selections still match; this does not itself skip a test or weaken complete/disjoint selection. If a receipt is meant to certify a canonical deterministic plan including estimated time, specify and validate those fields. Otherwise state that estimates are advisory and keep the exact node-ID and independent shard-success checks as the actual gate.

### A01-95 — SVG filter-image resource escapes report self-containment check

The rendered-report inspector refuses remote assets only for its enumerated element/attribute pairs. That set covers SVG `image` and `use` but omits `feImage` (`src/emrys/reporting/_run_report/models.py:25-45`; `src/emrys/reporting/_run_report/validation.py:293-315`). Python's `HTMLParser` lowercases the element to `feimage`. A tiny in-memory fixture using the pinned `ReportHTMLInspector` and `validate_rendered_html` code accepted otherwise valid scientific HTML containing an applied SVG filter with `<feImage href="https://example.invalid/x.png"/>`, and also accepted `xlink:href`. The same fixture rejected remote `image`, `img`, and `link` controls. The [W3C filter-effects specification](https://www.w3.org/TR/filter-effects-1/#elementdef-feimage) defines `feImage` as able to reference an external image. This is a validator-level reproduction without installed dependencies, a browser, or a complete report transaction; the current built-in source search found no `feImage` emitter.

An admitted scientific reporter supplies complete HTML bytes (`src/emrys/reporting/_run_report/context.py:241-250,516-517`). Publication runs the rendered-view check on staged and final bytes (`src/emrys/reporting/_run_report/publication.py:154-180`), while the receipt unconditionally records `self_contained: true` and `external_network_assets_used: false` (`src/emrys/reporting/_run_report/receipt.py:52-62,116`). Existing direct tests reject event attributes and `javascript:` links and assert the self-contained receipt field, but do not try this SVG asset (`tests/reporting/test_report.py:465-509`). Exact-byte publication checks and receipt hashes remain useful against later mutation; they do not establish that an already accepted provider view has no external resource. Extend the renderer-boundary asset check and focused negative tests before relying on that receipt claim. No network fetch or supported-Run output was observed.

### A01-96 — Hand-authored Project may reuse one physical FASTQ across roles

The existing `docs/tasks/polish-campaign.md:409-425` records this admission-policy discrepancy as a source prediction. The manifest drafting route rejects any repeated `(device, inode)` across sample/mate roles and has a hard-link refusal test (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/onboarding.py:1441-1457`; `tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_onboarding.py:1779-1788,1805-1807`). The supported hand-authored Project route reaches `_normalize_samples` through `admit_project` and `_admit_sample_inputs` (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/normalization.py:415-434,519-533,728-742`), but its only FASTQ role-uniqueness check compares R1 and R2 paths within each row (`:352-393`). It caches an identical path if repeated across rows during inspection (`:358-375`). A hard-linked R1/R2 pair at distinct paths would also bypass that lexical equality check.

Doctor reuses Project admission without another cross-role physical-file comparison (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/doctor.py:525-539`; `src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/onboarding.py:1686-1698,1701-1758`). Materialization forwards each admitted mate path to STAR, and the task-start duplicate check is scoped to paths within one task (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/materialization.py:513-547`; `src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/task.py:1804-1811`). Sample-manifest syntax, unique sample IDs, per-role snapshots, and existing draft refusal remain meaningful protections (`src/emrys/contracts/scientific_evidence/step08.py:326-354`; `tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_normalization.py:388-444`). This is a supported-path source trace, not a reproduced Run or a claim that equal bytes establish biological identity. Resolve whether one physical FASTQ can fill multiple roles, preserve legitimate reuse of one Dataset by multiple Analyses, and route any parity correction through Project admission rather than adding another registry.

### A01-97 — Quoted tracked filenames can evade the documentation gate

The documentation inventory runs newline-delimited `git ls-files` in text mode, treats each output line as a path, and silently discards it if `is_file()` is false (`scripts/documentation/validate_structure.py:73-95`). With Git's ordinary quoted-path output, a non-ASCII tracked name such as `café.md` is printed as an escaped quoted string instead of the filesystem name. In a disposable tiny Git repository, the pinned `git_paths` function returned no Markdown paths for that tracked file; its actual `validate_local_links` function then returned no problem for the file's broken `[broken](missing.md)` link (`:164-196`). The full documentation CLI could not run locally because `markdown_it` is absent; the probe stubbed that import only to exercise the actual inventory and empty-input link functions. This is an admitted-future-file blind spot, not a broken link observed in the pinned tree: its 170 tracked Markdown names contain no non-ASCII path.

The same inventory function selects Mermaid sources (`scripts/documentation/validate_structure.py:254-260`). Canonical named documents are checked separately (`:199-206`), and ordinary local links and missing anchors remain protected. Existing tests require a clear failure when Git inventory itself errors, and exercise ordinary links, but not quoted filenames (`tests/documentation/test_validate_structure.py:264-287,290-323`). Read NUL-delimited Git paths and retain the current file and link checks; avoid treating the current absence of such filenames as proof of a robust future gate.

### A01-98 — Remote form action passes the report navigation check

The HTML inspector rejects `<base>` and refresh meta tags as navigation-capable, and rejects `javascript:` or `vbscript:` in `action` and `formaction` attributes (`src/emrys/reporting/_run_report/validation.py:223-237,297-305,409-414`). Its embedded-resource list has no `(form, action)` or submit-button `formaction` pair (`src/emrys/reporting/_run_report/models.py:25-45`). The same source-bound `validate_rendered_html` fixture used for A01-95 accepted an otherwise valid scientific view with `<form action="https://example.invalid/collect">`; no browser submission was performed. The [HTML Standard](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/form-control-infrastructure.html#attributes-for-form-submission) treats `action` and `formaction` as submission URLs. This is a user-triggered navigation boundary, separate from A01-95's automatically referenced asset and self-containment claim.

Core reporting says it owns navigation and HTML safety (`src/emrys/reporting/_run_report/README.md:20-27`), while the built-in reporter currently has no form markup in a pinned source search. Existing active-markup tests cover event attributes and an active link, not remote form submission (`tests/reporting/test_report.py:480-509`). Define whether provider-supplied forms and outbound submission are permitted in a local report, then align the validator and focused tests with that policy. Do not infer a network request, data disclosure, or supported-Run output from this source-bound fixture.

### A01-99 — Quoted Python filenames can evade the source-direction gate

The Python source inventory also reads newline-delimited `git ls-files` in text mode and skips output strings that do not resolve to a present `.py` path (`tests/tools/source_dependencies.py:252-282`). In a disposable Git repository, a tracked `src/emrys/libraries/café.py` importing `emrys.stages.alpha.worker` appeared in Git's quoted output and was absent from the actual `python_sources` result. The actual `inspect_repository` then reported no problems; passing the omitted file directly to `collect_edges` exposed the forbidden neutral-library-to-stage import under AC-DEP-002 (`:397-424,475-482`). The 174 Python source paths in the pinned `src/emrys` tree have ASCII names, so no current forbidden import is shown to be hidden. This reproduces the same Git-output mechanism as A01-97 at a different gate and fault boundary.

ASCII-path tests explicitly catch that owner-direction violation, stale transitions, and dynamic literal imports; inventory tests cover ordinary malformed, ignored, and symlink paths (`tests/test_source_dependencies.py:17-42,69-76,175-194,351-425,521-568`). The static CI target runs this gate (`scripts/make_quality.mk:211-219`; `.github/workflows/ci.yml:164-186`). Ruff, Vulture, and compilation scan source directories and can detect different syntax/style faults in a non-ASCII-named file, but they do not enforce this owner-direction rule (`scripts/make_quality.mk:7,215-216,228-236`). ShellCheck's Git path enumeration already uses `-z` (`:231-233`), a repository-local model for an exact filename inventory. Preserve the import and documented-exception checks while making its file enumeration robust. The full hosted CI gate was not run with this fixture.

### A01-100 — Analysis-source table overstates scientific-reporter admission

The evidence-view table is captioned "Admitted analysis artifacts available to the scientific reporter," but it iterates every primary-analysis artifact in the Run summary and displays only availability, path, and hash (`src/emrys/reporting/templates/run_report.html.j2:364-376`). The summary is passed through to that template (`src/emrys/reporting/_run_report/context.py:405-408,474-495`); the table neither filters by completion nor uses the actual admitted-artifact tuple. The reporter admission path requires a declared, required, present, and complete artifact with a source; it skips other rows (`:135-164`). A present-but-failed record can retain a source path and hash (`src/emrys/reporting/_artifact_index/inspection.py:175-213`), so that row would display `present` despite exclusion from the reporter. This specific present-but-failed HTML case is source composition, not a completed report fixture.

An existing full report test publishes a missing Step 09 analysis artifact and confirms candidate rows and result links are withheld (`tests/reporting/test_report.py:1124-1160`); it does not assert this table's caption or rows. The evidence view separately shows completion in its artifact appendix and failed/missing counts in its rollup (`src/emrys/reporting/templates/run_report.html.j2:316-339,398-409`), while the scientific view states unavailability rather than opening excluded rows (`src/emrys/reporting/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking_report/computational.py:104-130`). Preserve those truthful defenses. Decide whether this table represents all declared analysis sources or only admitted reporter inputs, then align its caption, status columns, or selection accordingly. Do not infer that excluded candidate data was read or displayed.

### A01-101 — Report receipt can exceed the CSV field limit

The report receipt writer repeats the entire canonical JSON document in one TSV field for each HTML output (`src/emrys/reporting/_run_report/receipt.py:157-185`). Its reader uses `csv.DictReader` without a field-size policy (`:188-207`). The available Python's `csv.field_size_limit()` is 131,072 characters. An in-memory fixture invoking the pinned writer and reader accepted a short canonical document, but a document with 1,000 ordinary-shaped input descriptors produced a 144,669-character JSON field and raised the controlled `field larger than field limit` read error. The schema has no `maxItems` for `inputs` (`src/emrys/contracts/schemas/artifacts/v5/report_receipt.schema.json:98-107`), and the provider API and context admit a tuple of bound inputs without a count limit (`src/emrys/reporting/__init__.py:57-63`; `src/emrys/reporting/_run_report/context.py:253-282`). Existing report fixtures use small rosters; no installed provider or full Run with 1,000 inputs was exercised.

Fresh publication re-reads its staged receipt before linking final outputs (`src/emrys/reporting/_run_report/publication.py:154-164`; `src/emrys/reporting/_run_report/validation.py:58-79`), so this ceiling predicts a controlled publication refusal rather than false evidence admission. The exact canonical JSON, receipt equality check, input hashes, and receipt-last publication remain useful. Decide a format or bounded-reader policy at this owner without globally changing Python's CSV limit or silently truncating provenance; compare actual provider input sizes before assigning operational priority.

### A01-102 — Build metadata can be paired with another package tree

`admit_installed_package` hashes bytes under the executing `PACKAGE_ROOT` but selects build provenance separately by the distribution name `emrys-rna-workflow`; it checks metadata shape and re-reads it but does not verify that the selected distribution owns that import root (`src/emrys/libraries/source_authority.py:21-23,68-125`). A tiny isolated source-bound fixture ran the actual function with the pinned checkout as the executing package tree and a temporary unrelated distribution metadata directory on `sys.path`. `importlib.metadata.distribution()` selected that directory, and admission returned its invented version, Git commit, and lock hash alongside a real digest of the checkout's package bytes. This was an unusual import/metadata split, not the normal controlled installed-wheel command; no scientific execution bytes were forged.

The distinction matters to attribution: the reporting contract says installed-code hash, version, and build provenance enter the manifest and receipt (`src/emrys/reporting/README.md:51-58`), and report context and receipt carry the selected `git_commit` and package record (`src/emrys/reporting/_run_report/context.py:386-417,458-469`; `src/emrys/reporting/_run_report/receipt.py:120-125`). Existing source-authority tests change a temporary `PACKAGE_ROOT` while retaining ambient distribution metadata and check byte-change sensitivity and malformed metadata, not distribution ownership (`tests/libraries/test_source_authority.py:123-170`). The controlled Python startup and isolated-wheel test establish separate defenses against adjacent source loading (`src/emrys/libraries/source_authority.py:146-173`; `tests/test_package_distribution.py:341-362`); they do not prove the general metadata/root pairing. Preserve the exact content digest, symlink refusal, and repeated admission while defining which distribution file ownership or install-path evidence can support the Git/version attribution.

### A01-103 — Direct R Make commands can load an ambient profile

The operator `r-restore`, `r-check`, and `local-real-r-test` routes select `R_PROFILE_USER` but launch plain `Rscript` or wrappers that do so, without the guarded `--no-environ --no-site-file --no-restore --no-save` startup options (`scripts/make_quality.mk:92-124`; `tests/stages/cohort_candidate_preprocessing/run_step_08_vcf_preprocessing_tests.sh:40-54`; `tests/analyses/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking/run_step_09_cmh_tests.sh:30-40`). A disposable R 4.6.1 fixture set `R_ENVIRON_USER` to a file that changed `R_PROFILE_USER` after the shell-selected value; plain `Rscript -e` ran an alternate profile, while the guarded startup arguments did not. This proves profile-selection bypass for that launch pattern, not a dependency mutation or a passing false qualification. The shell contract test checks Make variables and script selection but not ambient startup suppression (`tests/shell/test_local_r_environment.sh:225-294`). The hosted guarded-R lane invokes these same Make routes (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:255-286`), but no hostile hosted startup file was observed.

Doctor and runtime probes already construct sanitized environments and guarded R arguments (`src/emrys/libraries/process_environment.py:75-130`; `src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/doctor.py:1216-1272`; `src/emrys/evidence/runtime_availability/_probes.py:239-246`). The selected `.Rprofile` and restore script enforce existing-library and lock checks once they run (`src/emrys/.Rprofile:22-124`; `src/emrys/resources/runtime/restore_r_environment.R:77-129`). Retain those checks and explicit operator restoration authority; decide how direct Make routes and their wrappers should enforce the same startup selection before calling their existing scripts. Do not promote this local fixture to Viking behavior.

### A01-104 — Package-tree hashing buffers each complete file

`_read_regular_file` reads every 1 MiB chunk of a package member into a list, joins the bytes, then returns them for hashing (`src/emrys/libraries/installed_package_identity.py:108-154,216-225,325-341`). Peak memory thus scales with the largest member of the installed Python or R package tree, with an additional joined copy. Core installed-code admission and runtime R-package bindings use this identity (`src/emrys/libraries/source_authority.py:68-103`; `src/emrys/evidence/runtime_availability/inspector.py:120-175`). This is a source-derived memory ceiling, distinct from A01-87's reference-artifact reads; no large package or out-of-memory failure was measured.

Direct tiny-file tests protect deterministic digests, ignored Python caches, no-follow/special-file refusal, and changed-entry detection (`tests/libraries/test_installed_package_identity.py:22-35,57-68,244-264,278-345,371-454`).
