Runtime tests protect re-admission of changed package trees (`tests/evidence/runtime_availability/test_runtime_availability.py:1250-1290`). Stream each file's digest and byte count while retaining descriptor-based admission, mode/size framing, stable before/after identity, and deterministic tree semantics; measure real member sizes before assigning urgency.

### A01-105 — Step 02b aggregate QC counts hide a class contradiction

The shared flagstat parser retains separate QC-passed and QC-failed counts for `in total` and `mapped` (`src/emrys/libraries/evidence/qc.py:8-33`). The Step 02b validator immediately sums each pair and tests only aggregate `mapped <= total` (`src/emrys/evidence/canonical_bam_qc/validator.py:54-66`). A tiny direct `build_validation_report` fixture with the exact quickcheck PASS marker, `5 + 5 in total`, and `8 + 0 mapped` returned all five checks as passing. Mapped QC-passed is eight while total QC-passed is five. The owner contract explicitly promises only summed nonnegative counts and aggregate comparison (`src/emrys/evidence/canonical_bam_qc/CONTRACT.md:92-107`); this is a contract-adequacy question rather than a violated current promise. No native samtools output or complete Run produced the contradiction.

The direct negative test uses `11 + 0 mapped` against `10 + 0 in total`, which exercises aggregate overflow but not per-class subset arithmetic (`tests/evidence/canonical_bam_qc/test_validate_step_02b_bam_qc.py:95-106`). Reporting also keeps only aggregate `total_reads` and `mapped_reads` (`src/emrys/reporting/_artifact_index/inspection.py:258-277`). Decide whether `count_consistency` should mean both QC classes are subsets or whether the artifact should explicitly remain aggregate-only. Preserve exact quickcheck, unique-row, nonnegative, aggregate, publication, and Run-bound source checks; do not infer that real samtools emits this impossible combination.

### A01-106 — The Stage Map parity test omits the analysis tail

The Stage Map describes its direct-edge table as the complete default workflow and includes three analysis-tail edges (`src/emrys/contracts/STAGE_MAP.md:58-79`). The only test that reads this edge table compares it with the processing profile after excluding both analysis owners from `EXPECTED_TASKS` and filtering out every edge whose producer or consumer is outside those processing keys (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_profile.py:232-268,271-300`). In a temporary source-bound copy, removing the Step 08-to-09 row changed the parser's full edge count from 15 to 14, while the test's filtered 12-edge comparison stayed byte-equivalent. The documentation structure checker verifies the owner identity roster and mirrored files, but not the edge table (`scripts/documentation/validate_structure.py:211-232`). This is a documented-graph parity gap, not a demonstrated wrong execution graph.

Analysis descriptors separately compose their task inputs into direct edges (`src/emrys/analyses/__init__.py:177-207`), and the workflow consumes the composed edges for analysis dependencies (`src/emrys/workflow/Snakefile:300-314,427-446`). The real Snakemake DAG test has literal expectations for the three analysis-tail edge classes (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_workflow.py:283-339`). Retain that execution oracle and compare the documented tail directly with the composed profile, with a distinct check for the table's claimed completeness. Do not equate a passing processing-profile test with full Stage Map parity.

### A01-107 — CI selection assertions check tokens, not job conditions

The pinned workflow gives `workflow-lint` and eight other ordinary jobs conditions that run automatically on ordinary events and require a selected input on manual dispatch (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:109-115`; `tests/test_ci_workflow.py:25-35`). The workflow contract test checks each condition for three substrings: non-dispatch, non-schedule, and its input name (`tests/test_ci_workflow.py:111-117`). Appending a valid `|| true` to a job condition would retain all three substrings and pass this assertion while also selecting that job on unselected manual dispatches and schedules. This is a regression-test ceiling; the pinned workflow source itself expresses the intended condition, and no workflow was altered or run for this probe.

Actionlint checks syntax and embedded shell, not the intended event truth table (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:155-162`). The same test pins a nine-job selection mapping and manual input roster (`tests/test_ci_workflow.py:25-35,73-108`), which protect different drift but do not establish the truth value of each `if`. Retain those checks and compare conditions by exact semantics or a small event/input truth table before claiming that scheduled and manual lane selection are protected.

Failure propagation is another CI-test ceiling: the static/docs/wheel job puts its checks in one step, and the contract test pins that step's script lines but does not reject a future `continue-on-error: true` on the step (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:164-186`; `tests/test_ci_workflow.py:120-134`). That mutation would preserve the script and Actionlint syntax while making a failing step nonfatal to the job. The synthetic E2E lane demonstrates a stronger local pattern for intentional `continue-on-error`: it inspects selected step outcomes before claiming success (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:1275-1299,1385-1417`; `tests/test_ci_workflow.py:786-829`). Current static-wheel has no bypass, and no CI run or mutation was performed. Preserve the actual failure gate when improving its contract test.

Within that synthetic final gate, the current workflow correctly requires both selected profile uploads, checkout preservation, and the infrastructure upload in addition to profile execution (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:1385-1417`). The focused contract test asserts the two profile-run outcomes appear in the final environment and that the script mentions `UPLOAD_INFRASTRUCTURE`, but does not require `UPLOAD_130`, `UPLOAD_100000`, or `CHECKOUT_OUTCOME` in the failing condition (`tests/test_ci_workflow.py:785-820`). Dropping one of those dependencies could leave this source test and syntax lint green. This is a test-regression ceiling, not a current bypass or hosted result. Extend the final-gate test to the complete selected outcome graph without removing the current upload and checkout gates.

**Long-run concurrency.** The current group selects `github.run_id` for scheduled/manual events and the cancellation policy excludes those events (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:58-60`). Its focused test checks only that the group string contains the schedule condition, dispatch condition, and run ID, while asserting the cancellation expression exactly (`tests/test_ci_workflow.py:137-147`). A valid group expression that selects nonempty `github.ref` first would retain those three tokens but group separate long runs by ref, permitting unintended collision. This is a future-regression test ceiling, not current cancellation. Pin a small event/ref/run-ID truth table or exact intended expression while preserving the existing cancellation assertion.

**Manual guard binding.** The manual-selection test asserts the eight environment *names* and error phrase, but not each environment value or condition operand (`tests/test_ci_workflow.py:73-108`). The pinned workflow maps all eight inputs correctly (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:84-107`). If `RUN_SYNTHETIC_100000` were accidentally bound to `inputs.synthetic_130`, a 100000-only dispatch would fail the at-least-one-lane guard although the synthetic job selects `inputs.synthetic_100000` independently (`:1099-1104`); the current source test and syntax lint could remain green. Add exact input-to-guard mapping and condition-operand assertions alongside the existing roster and job-selection checks. No dispatch or CI run was made.

**Manual refusal exit.** The same source test checks the guard's input names and diagnostic phrase but not its failure status (`tests/test_ci_workflow.py:73-108`). Removing only `exit 2` from the all-false branch would leave the tested text and valid shell intact (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:84-107`); every ordinary and synthetic lane can then skip an unselected manual dispatch (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:109-114,166-169,903-905,1099-1104`), leaving a misleading successful run. Keep the guard and require a nonzero all-false result plus a selected-lane success in a focused contract oracle. This is a hypothetical source-regression path, not a changed workflow or executed dispatch.

### A01-108 — Optional missing STAR member leaves provenance summary passing

Reference inventory admission requires at least one `star_index_file` row, but accepts `required=false` on that row (`src/emrys/evidence/reference_provenance/_reference_inventory.py:62-64,117-121`). A missing optional file becomes `missing_optional` in the artifact observation (`src/emrys/evidence/reference_provenance/_reference_contigs.py:20-27`), while the summary excludes that state from its failure counts and derives STAR agreement from `chrName` and `chrLength` alone (`src/emrys/evidence/reference_provenance/_reference_render.py:78-100`; `src/emrys/evidence/reference_provenance/_reference_contigs.py:76-91,127-135`). In a tiny source-bound fixture with the seven other reference/contig files present and the sole declared `star/Genome` absent, the artifact status was `missing_optional`, `star_agreement=pass`, and `overall_status=pass`. No native STAR index or supported Run used this fixture.

The owner guide describes reconciliation of an explicitly listed reference bundle, and the normal example marks the synthetic `Genome` member required (`src/emrys/evidence/reference_provenance/README.md:1-4`; `configs/reference_provenance.example.tsv:9`; `tests/evidence/reference_provenance/test_reference_provenance.py:38-50,129-139`). The summary's `pass` currently means required members and contig comparisons passed, not that an index member was present or usable. Decide whether at least one index member must be present for an overall pass, or state that narrower claim in the summary and operator guide. Retain explicit optional-missing artifact status, required-file failures, hashes, and ordered contig agreement.

### A01-109 — A competing selector can strand the admitted predecessor

The shared `publish_exclusive(..., replace_expected=...)` path opens and checks the old bytes, renames the old name to a displaced sibling, then links the staged replacement to the original name (`src/emrys/libraries/exclusive_publication.py:64-104`). If another process creates the original name between the rename and the link, the `FileExistsError` branch unlinks the displaced old file before refusing the replacement (`:103-109`). A tiny direct helper fixture inserted a competing `runtime.tsv` at that exact link seam: the call raised its controlled replacement error and retained the competitor, but the previously admitted `old selection` bytes no longer existed at any path in the temporary directory. This did not run a supported Doctor operation or a real concurrent process.

Doctor uses this replacement path to move a dependent Project's selected shared-runtime generation after separately qualifying the candidate (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/doctor.py:1830-1873`). Its owner guide says replacement requires the same source Project, the exact prior selector, fresh probes, and a maintenance claim, while retained Runs keep their old selector (`src/emrys/evidence/runtime_availability/README.md:75-95`). The existing helper test injects a competing change *before* the rename and proves the competitor survives; it does not exercise a competing creation after the rename (`tests/libraries/test_exclusive_publication.py:44-69`). Preserve no-clobber, old-byte admission, staged fsync, and competing-file refusal, but retain the displaced predecessor for inspection or recovery when this later race occurs. The ordinary maintenance claim may prevent conforming EMRYS writers from racing; no supported-path recurrence was shown.

### A01-110 — Duplicate HTML attributes can hide active style and DOM identity

The rendered-report inspector receives an ordered list of attributes from Python's `HTMLParser`, then converts it to `dict`, keeping the last value of any repeated name (`src/emrys/reporting/_run_report/validation.py:210-216`). It stores that map for `<main>` and compares `data-report-view`, `data-run-id`, and other provenance fields with the expected identity (`:243-246,457-475`). It also passes only the mapped `style` value to its embedded-CSS check (`:285-292`). A standard-library probe with first `data-run-id="foreign"` and `style="background:url(https://example.invalid/a)"`, followed by second values `data-run-id="expected"` and `style="color:black"`, retained both pairs but exposed only the second values in the map. The [HTML Standard](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/parsing.html#attribute-name-state) specifies that a browser discards later duplicate attributes, leaving the first values in the DOM. The inspector's separate active-resource loop reads every original pair but does not inspect inline `style` there (`:293-314`; `src/emrys/reporting/_run_report/models.py:25-42`). The same bytes can therefore have a first-value browser style containing a non-embedded URL while the static CSS check sees only the benign second value, as well as a different DOM provenance identity. This is source composition plus a parser probe, not a completed report transaction or observed installed-template output.

The current provider-markup tests mutate active resources and event attributes but do not challenge duplicate provenance or style names (`tests/reporting/test_report.py:480-512`), and the installed template is not shown to generate duplicates. Required banner, one-main, per-pair event and URI checks, exact output-byte, receipt, and post-publication rechecks remain separate defenses (`src/emrys/reporting/_run_report/validation.py:293-314,370-475`; `src/emrys/reporting/_run_report/publication.py:154-180`). Reject duplicate HTML attributes before semantic checks, rather than trusting the last-value map. No browser network request was observed in this audit.

### A01-111 — Duplicate header tags pass shared BAM sort and read-group checks

The shared `parse_header` helper requires one `@HD` line and one `@RG` line, then tests whether the expected `SO:coordinate`, `ID:<scope>`, and `SM:<scope>` tokens occur among each line's tab-separated fields (`src/emrys/libraries/alignments/bam.py:56-65`). It does not require a unique tag name within either line. Direct calls to the pinned helper returned `(True, True, 'HD=1 RG=1')` for a header with both `SO:coordinate` and `SO:queryname`, and for one with both matching and foreign `ID`/`SM` fields. The [SAM format specification](https://github.com/samtools/hts-specs/blob/master/SAMv1.tex) says a noncomment header line may contain each field tag only once. This is an ambiguous malformed header accepted by the helper, not evidence that native samtools emits it.

Steps 02, 04, and 05 use the shared result to mark their coordinate-sort and read-group report checks (`src/emrys/stages/canonical_bam/validator.py:60-69,105-116`; `src/emrys/stages/duplicate_marking/validator.py:60-67,96-100`; `src/emrys/stages/split_n_cigar/validator.py:88-96,127-131`). The existing helper test rejects duplicate `@HD` and `@RG` lines, but does not test duplicate tags inside one line (`tests/libraries/test_bam_validation.py:46-59`). Other report checks still require BAM/BAI magic, samtools quickcheck, and, for Step 02, alignment RG counts. No native BAM/BAI, owner report, or full Run with this header was exercised. Decide exact per-line tag uniqueness once in the shared helper, preserving those independent protections and the three owners' distinct checks.

### A01-112 — Escaped CSS functions evade report resource scans

The core report validator searches CSS text for literal `url(` and `@import` patterns (`src/emrys/reporting/_run_report/models.py:47-50`; `src/emrys/reporting/_run_report/validation.py:126-133`). That search covers the tracked CSS, rendered `<style>` content, and inline `style` attributes (`src/emrys/reporting/_run_report/validation.py:140-150,283-292,340-348`). In a source-bound probe of the exact `ReportHTMLInspector`, `<main style="background-image:u\72l(https://example.invalid/a)">` produced no active-resource error, while literal `url(https://example.invalid/a)` was rejected. The scientific SVG validator similarly searches literal `url(` and `@import` in style text and attributes (`src/emrys/reporting/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking_report/figures.py:187-204`); an isolated extraction of that pinned function accepted the escaped form while refusing the literal form. The [CSS Syntax Level 3 specification](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-syntax-3/#consume-an-ident-like-token) consumes escapes into an identifier before recognizing a case-insensitive `url` function.

External scientific-provider markup reaches the core rendered-report check (`src/emrys/reporting/_run_report/context.py:226-250`; `src/emrys/reporting/_run_report/publication.py:154-180`). Tests reject literal remote CSS and active markup, but do not exercise escaped function names (`tests/reporting/test_report.py:480-509`; `tests/reporting/test_figures.py:691-702`). No tracked built-in CSS or figure is shown to emit this escape, and no completed report, browser fetch, or network request was observed. This is a separate parsing mechanism from A01-95's omitted SVG resource attribute and A01-110's duplicate HTML attributes. Define one CSS grammar and refusal rule across both rendering boundaries; preserve output-byte, receipt, and post-publication identity checks.

### A01-113 — Parent replacement can misbind a stable text snapshot

`stable_text` takes a no-symlink child snapshot, reads through `Path.read_text`, and compares the child snapshot again (`src/emrys/libraries/validation/inputs.py:27-55`). The pathname read is not bound to the inode observed in either snapshot. In a tiny temporary fixture, the original `admitted/source.tsv` contained `safe`, while an alternate directory held a same-name symlink to `false-pass`. Replacing only the parent directory during `Path.read_text` and restoring it before the second snapshot made the actual helper return `false-pass\n` with the unchanged original five-byte snapshot; the path afterward again read `safe\n`. A direct child-file swap was rejected in an earlier probe because its ctime changed. This is a deliberately timed same-user filesystem race, not a reproduced owner report or normal Run.

Several owner validators consume that helper, including STAR index and alignment logs, Step 04 metrics, Step 07 selector and VCF, and RSeQC text (`src/emrys/stages/star_index/validator.py:116-129`; `src/emrys/stages/star_alignment/validator.py:63-76`; `src/emrys/stages/duplicate_marking/validator.py:68-70`; `src/emrys/libraries/validation/mpileup.py:142-160`; `src/emrys/evidence/rseqc_orientation/validator.py:55-64`). The `stable_text` test checks a changed before/after snapshot (`tests/libraries/test_validation_report.py:114-141`). Separate descriptor-bound byte readers have mutation and replacement tests (`src/emrys/libraries/validation/inputs.py:58-76`; `tests/libraries/test_validation_inputs.py:210-312`), so this is a specific text-reader identity gap, not an absence of input protection across the library. Audit each caller's size and UTF-8 policy before reusing a descriptor-bound read; preserve exact errors, snapshot meaning, and report publication rechecks.

### A01-114 — Scientific completion sentence survives unavailable Step 09 sources

The scientific HTML template always says that its view reports the "completed Step 09 analysis" when `scientific` is true (`src/emrys/reporting/templates/run_report.html.j2:174`). A nearby branch instead renders an unavailable-results explanation when `computational_results is none` (`:184-186`). Admission deliberately withholds computational rows if the required Step 09 result set, mutation spectrum, or validation artifact is incomplete (`src/emrys/reporting/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking_report/computational.py:122-147,293-303`). A committed full-report test publishes a view with a missing required Step 09 source and checks that no candidate rows were opened (`tests/reporting/test_report.py:1124-1159`). The run summary can call its own transaction `complete` while retaining a required artifact as missing (`tests/reporting/test_artifact_run_summary.py:174-195`; `src/emrys/reporting/_run_summary/document.py:58-82`).

This is a reader-facing evidence-claim inconsistency, not proof that a particular Step 09 execution did not finish. The unavailable panel, missing-source status, and result-link withholding remain meaningful defenses. Condition the sentence on admitted computational evidence, or explicitly distinguish completed execution from unavailable evidence. The test was characterized from source and was not rerun in this audit.

### A01-115 — Non-UTF-8 reference predecessor escapes controlled reconciliation

When all three reference-provenance outputs already exist, publication reads each prior output and passes it to `validate_output` before replacing anything (`src/emrys/evidence/reference_provenance/reconciler.py:76-89`). That validator decodes bytes without handling `UnicodeDecodeError` (`src/emrys/evidence/reference_provenance/reconciler.py:39-49`). The public `reconcile_from_args` catches `ProvenanceError` and `report.ValidationError` only (`src/emrys/evidence/reference_provenance/reconciler.py:169-174`). A tiny temporary fixture with all three predecessor files and invalid UTF-8 in one of them reached the actual `publish` path and raised raw `UnicodeDecodeError`. The `finally` block still removes its own lock (`src/emrys/evidence/reference_provenance/reconciler.py:114-120`); the observed failure is loss of controlled malformed-predecessor diagnosis, not a false provenance pass or a reproduced Run failure. Existing owner fixtures exercise partial predecessor, foreign lock, and replacement rollback, but not this complete malformed predecessor (`tests/evidence/reference_provenance/test_reference_provenance.py:371-389,430-518`). Preserve the three-output rollback behavior while converting prior-output decoding to the owner error contract.

### A01-116 — Step 10 direct contract can admit changed output bytes

The shared scientific-context transaction first calls `_bound_path` for each receipt-declared file, requiring its current SHA-256 to equal the recorded hash (`src/emrys/contracts/scientific_evidence/scientific_context.py:981-995,1346-1369`). It later reopens the four outputs for semantic admission and computes each returned `ContextTable.sha256`, but compares only row counts with the receipt before returning success (`src/emrys/contracts/scientific_evidence/scientific_context.py:327-328,1377-1408`). A tiny temporary owner fixture changed the candidate-context TSV from LF to CRLF immediately after `_bound_path` returned; the rows retained their meanings. The actual `validate_scientific_context_transaction` returned success while the receipt hash differed from current output bytes and the returned table hash matched the changed bytes. The committed stale-output test mutates before admission, not between those reads (`tests/contracts/scientific_evidence/test_scientific_context.py:155-176`). This is a deterministic direct-contract check/use gap, not proof of a live filesystem race or completed Run false pass.

The Step 10 validation CLI snapshots all declared files and rechecks them before publishing an executed report (`src/emrys/analyses/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking/scientific_context_projection/validator.py:92-110`; `src/emrys/libraries/validation/runtime.py:27-47`). Reporting also compares the returned output-table hash with its own artifact snapshot (`src/emrys/reporting/_artifact_index/reconcile_step10.py:22-43,85-105`). Those surviving boundaries limit this finding; a size-changing LF-to-CRLF mutation would be caught on those paths. Reconcile the semantic reader's exact bytes with the receipt inside the shared contract without removing the CLI/report checks or the independent reference-window oracle.

### A01-117 — Direct Step 08 validator can publish after upstream change

With `--step07-root`, the Step 08 validator reopens each exact Step 07 receipt and VCF, comparing its hash and declared identity with the Step 08 input table (`src/emrys/stages/cohort_candidate_preprocessing/validator.py:45-94`). Its `build_validation_report` returns snapshots only for the sample and partition manifests, annotation GTF, and three Step 08 TSVs (`src/emrys/stages/cohort_candidate_preprocessing/validator.py:269-283`). The common `--execute` path rechecks only those returned snapshots before publishing (`src/emrys/libraries/validation/runtime.py:27-47`). In a tiny temporary owner fixture, appending one byte to the exact Step 07 receipt after `build_validation_report` returned allowed the actual `validate_from_args` path to exit zero and publish all five checks as passing; the receipt's current hash differed from the Step 08 input table's recorded hash. This is a direct-validator transaction continuity gap, not evidence that a supported Run completed with changed upstream input.

The direct mutation test covers each of the six returned snapshots after report construction (`tests/stages/cohort_candidate_preprocessing/test_validate_step_08_preprocessing_outputs.py:307-354`). Another test changes a Step 07 receipt or VCF between separate validator invocations and observes a failed reconciliation on the second call (`:420-459`); neither covers the post-reconciliation interval. Run materialization already declares Step 07 VCFs and receipts as stable Step 08 task inputs, then rechecks them after semantic validation (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/materialization.py:757-815`; `src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/task.py:2788-2800`). Keep that Run boundary and the existing six-input publication checks while extending the direct validator's exact upstream snapshot set when `--step07-root` is supplied.

### A01-118 — Direct Step 07 validator can publish after selector change

For a `regions_file` partition, `selector_ok` resolves and reads the file named by the partition manifest (`src/emrys/libraries/validation/mpileup.py:142-153`). The Step 07 validator snapshots only its six explicit CLI paths and returns that set for the common prepublication recheck; the indirectly read selector is absent (`src/emrys/stages/partitioned_cohort_mpileup/validator.py:212-235`; `src/emrys/libraries/validation/runtime.py:27-47`). A tiny temporary owner fixture began with valid `1\t0\t10` BED content and replaced it with `unknown\t0\t10` immediately after `build_validation_report` returned. The actual `validate_from_args` path exited zero and published five passing checks, although the selector hash changed; rebuilding against the new file produced a failed `selector_reconciliation` check. This is a direct-validator stale-report transaction, separate from A01-01's out-of-bounds selector false pass and A01-41's valid compressed-selector false failure.

The committed post-build mutation test covers each of the six explicit paths, not the indirect selector (`tests/stages/partitioned_cohort_mpileup/test_validate_step_07_mpileup_outputs.py:341-394`). Normal Run lists the selected file among stable Step 07 inputs and rechecks those inputs before verified publication (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/materialization.py:742-755`; `src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/task.py:2788-2800`). No supported-Run bypass or native bcftools output was observed. Preserve those Run checks and the six explicit snapshots while binding the exact selector file at the direct validation boundary.

### A01-119 — Guided Project inspection does not establish paired FASTQ identity

Named Project creation invokes sample admission with `inspect_fastqs=True`, then rechecks input snapshots before publishing the Project (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/onboarding.py:1329-1353,1374-1391`). The streaming inspector parses record framing, sequence/quality lengths, and a maximum read length independently for each file; it does not retain read names for comparison (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/normalization.py:157-226`). `_normalize_samples` inspects each mate and retains its snapshot but never compares their record counts or IDs (`:345-412`). An existing plain/gzip test deliberately admits one R1 record named `@r1` and two R2 records named `@first` and `@later` (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_onboarding.py:1296-1307`). This is a characterized admission ceiling and a source-composed guided-creation path, not an executed full `emrys init` fixture with that pair.

The owner contract promises each FASTQ's record inspection during creation (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/CONTRACT.md:184-198`). Normal Run forwards the admitted mate paths to STAR; its worker checks file presence, compression mode, and native output, while its validator checks BAM and STAR logs rather than pairing (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/materialization.py:513-547`; `src/emrys/stages/star_alignment/step_01_star_align.sh:55-77`; `src/emrys/stages/star_alignment/validator.py:45-118`). The Step 01 contract explicitly limits what byte stability and suffix checks prove (`src/emrys/stages/star_alignment/CONTRACT.md:27-42`). An optional diagnostic compares complete record counts and a requested prefix of normalized read IDs, but is outside the normal Run graph (`src/emrys/ingestion/sample_manifest_admission/README.md:10-13,29-32`; `src/emrys/ingestion/sample_manifest_admission/check_fastq_pairs.sh:114-170`). That prefix is not a whole-file ID proof.

The same guided scanner checks only the first header byte and can count a complete record whose header is just `@` (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/normalization.py:193-222`); direct guided tests cover truncation, length, gzip, and long-line cases but not an empty read identifier (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_onboarding.py:1296-1394`). This is a source-inferred header-grammar question, distinct from mate-to-mate identity comparison; no native STAR behavior is claimed. Decide whether Project readiness requires early count or ID matching and a nonempty identifier, then adjust the accepted input and cost policy across creation and hand-authored admission; otherwise make the current per-file promise explicit. No native STAR outcome, supported-Run completion, or biological pairing was established here.

The optional operator-run pair checker has a narrower but misleading same-file route. It verifies each path is a file and compares counts and normalized prefix IDs without checking that R1 and R2 denote distinct files (`src/emrys/ingestion/sample_manifest_admission/check_fastq_pairs.sh:91-101,114-170`). In a disposable local fixture, one valid one-record FASTQ supplied as both `--r1-fastq` and `--r2-fastq` returned zero and printed `PASS: FASTQ pair check succeeded`; the positive direct tests supply distinct paths (`tests/ingestion/sample_manifest_admission/test_check_fastq_pairs.py:90-137`). Project admission separately rejects identical authored mate paths (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/normalization.py:378-386`), and the helper guide explicitly disclaims complete pairing and provenance (`src/emrys/ingestion/sample_manifest_admission/README.md:10-18`). Decide whether the advisory command's paired-file claim should refuse identical files, including aliases, or state this limit in its output. Preserve its count, prefix, decompression, and no-write checks. The tiny fixture establishes only the optional helper's response, not Project or Run admission.

### A01-120 — Mermaid gate checks declaration but not diagram body

The documentation validator inventories tracked `.mmd` files and calls `validate_diagrams`, which checks only a `flowchart LR/RL/TB/BT/TD` first line and absence of Markdown fences; it does not parse later lines or require a diagram body (`scripts/documentation/validate_structure.py:235-260`). A tiny isolated call to that exact function reported no problem for `flowchart LR` followed by an arbitrary `@@@` body. The committed negative test checks an unsupported `sequenceDiagram` declaration and a fence, not body grammar (`tests/documentation/test_validate_structure.py:290-323`). The script's docstring and the test-owner README call this Mermaid syntax validation (`scripts/documentation/validate_structure.py:235-236`; `tests/documentation/README.md:5-7`). The narrow check still detects an empty, wrong-declaration, or Markdown-wrapped tracked source; the three existing diagrams are explicitly non-authoritative views (`docs/architecture/diagrams/README.md:3-14`). Source review found no second Mermaid parser in Make or CI. This is a documentation-gate claim limit, not evidence that any current diagram is malformed or that the other documentation checks should be retired. State its promise as declaration/fence structure, or use an established parser if rendered syntax is a maintained requirement.

### A01-121 — Task can publish verified marker after a late input change

The Task binds initial stable-input size/hash records, then compares those inputs with fresh snapshots after producer, validator, and semantic checks (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/task.py:2600-2616,2788-2792`). `_publish_attempt` embeds the original input rows in a succeeded terminal record, publishes it, and revalidates logs (`:2378-2439,2865-2882`). Before publishing the verified marker, Task rechecks reused and ordinary outputs, input-directory membership, the validation report, and its terminal record, but does not rehash ordinary stable-input files (`:2889-2924`). Thus an input changing at the terminal-record publication interval could leave a direct Task invocation with a verified marker bound to the old input hash. This is a source-composed interval, not a reproduced Task or complete-Run false pass; the audit Python lacks the owner fixture dependencies, and none were installed.

Existing Task tests mutate an input during producer execution and replace a Step 00c output at terminal-record publication; they protect distinct early-input and late-output failures but not the late-input interval (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_task.py:2178-2191,3066-3099`). Retained `validate_verified_task` does rehash each recorded input/output (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/task.py:2265-2277`), and Run lifecycle re-inspects task evidence after workflow completion and blocks success on integrity failures (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/_inspection_evidence.py:118-150`; `src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/lifecycle.py:2020-2059`). Preserve that later boundary. Decide the direct Task marker's continuity promise and recheck stable input files immediately before its publication if the marker must be truthful on its own.

### A01-122 — Step 10 Fisher rederivation accepts a materially false effect and p-value

The Step 10 contract promises Fisher's conditional odds-ratio estimate, exact two-sided 95% interval, and two-sided p-value for its one inferential row (`src/emrys/analyses/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking/scientific_context_projection/CONTRACT.md:51-59`). R computes them with `stats::fisher.test`; Python independently rederives a conditional distribution from the admitted counts (`src/emrys/analyses/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking/scientific_context_projection/scientific_context_projection.R:670-684,703-721`; `src/emrys/contracts/scientific_evidence/scientific_context.py:481-574,649-719`). Python compares odds and interval bounds on a reciprocal root scale with absolute tolerance about `0.000122`, and compares p-values with a general absolute tolerance `1.5e-8` (`src/emrys/contracts/scientific_evidence/scientific_context.py:124-125,577-589,693-719`; `src/emrys/contracts/scientific_evidence/step08.py:217-220`). These absolute tolerances become broad when a valid odds ratio is large or a valid p-value is very small.

A tiny 400-row output fixture used the repository's synthetic context builders for an informative table with 199 of 200 foreground candidates and 1 of 200 background candidates carrying the motif (`tests/scientific_context_test_support.py:53-103,127-298`). Base R's `stats::fisher.test` returned conditional odds about `15447`, interval about `[2101, 4.5e15]`, and p about `7.77e-115`. The exact `validate_scientific_context_outputs` path admitted those R values. Changing only the reported odds ratio to `1000000` and p-value to `0` still passed the output validator; all other context, hit, logo, and statistics rows stayed fixed. The incorrect odds ratio is about 65 times R's value, and zero is not the computed p-value. This is a small output-owner semantic false pass, not a forged receipt, completed Run, or biological interpretation. Production R and receipt/hash checks remain separate defenses against ordinary output corruption.

The committed output helper hardcodes a positive Fisher oracle only for the 10/20 population with 5/2 motif hits; the guarded real-R fixture and contract mutation tests use that same informative table (`tests/scientific_context_test_support.py:219-263,326-344`; `tests/analyses/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking/scientific_context_projection/test_real_r_projection.py:125-129,181-197,287-338`; `tests/contracts/scientific_evidence/test_scientific_context.py:82-132`). The existing corpus thus protects one ordinary effect, while zero-cell and stronger or alternate informative cases have no independent positive golden. Preserve the conditional recomputation, exact payload/receipt binding, and real-R comparison; add scale-aware numerical acceptance and representative independent count/effect fixtures after the owner defines justified R/Python numerical behavior. This is distinct from A01-22's Step 09 CMH report ceiling.

### A01-138 — Local workflow profile policy is hashed but not enforced

The packaged local profile currently specifies `retries: 0` and `keep-incomplete: true`, and its owner README describes no engine retries and retained incomplete outputs as supported behavior (`src/emrys/workflow/profiles/local/profile.v9+.yaml:1-7`; `src/emrys/workflow/profiles/local/README.md:3-12`). Backend admission requires the exact field names and `executor: local`, then hashes retry/retention values without checking their promised values; it checks only the presence of both diagnostic keys and omits their values from the backend-semantics digest (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/run_implementation.py:278-319`). A retry/retention edit would change that digest, but a newly built Run could still be admitted with policy outside the documented behavior. Switching either diagnostic value to false would leave the backend-semantics digest unchanged; other package/Attempt identity may separately bind changed profile bytes, so this does not establish an in-place Attempt swap. This is a source-level admission and regression-test gap; the pinned profile itself has the stated values.

The real-engine incomplete-output fixture uses a separate temporary Snakefile and supplies `--keep-incomplete` on its command line, so it cannot detect deletion of the packaged profile's setting (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_workflow.py:159-203`). The implementation-identity test mutates Snakefile bytes, not the profile's policy values (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_materialization.py:1661-1700`); the real-engine fixture invokes the profile without asserting command or failed-log visibility (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_workflow.py:68-111`). The same admission code hashes `scheduler` without requiring the pinned `greedy` value; it requires a `cores` key but neither validates nor includes its value in this semantics digest (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/run_implementation.py:278-319`; `src/emrys/workflow/profiles/local/profile.v9+.yaml:1-7`). Production supplies admitted cores explicitly on its Snakemake argv (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/lifecycle.py:605-624`), so the profile's core field may be inert in that route. That does not prove it can be removed from installed or direct-engine use. A narrow semantic profile assertion at admission would protect accepted scheduler, no-retry, retained-incomplete, and visible-diagnostic policy while preserving identity and real-engine recovery tests. Decide whether the profile core value is a real direct-engine default or an unnecessary second setting before any reduction. No failing Task, concurrent Run, or Slurm placement was executed in this pass.

### A01-140 — Site storage receipt does not prove cross-node flock contention

The compute storage probe locks `flock.lock` and starts a child Python process on the same compute node to verify that a competing nonblocking `flock` fails (`src/emrys/evidence/storage_inventory/qualification.py:59-72,445-458`). Head finalization later reopens retained files, compares source and visible hashes, checks hard-link identity, and writes its own fsync probe, but does not attempt to acquire that compute-side lock while it is held (`:595-625,640-685`). The final receipt nevertheless carries the `advisory_flock_contention` check name within its fixed roster (`:26-33,661-680`), and the owner README describes a two-phase receipt binding locking and post-allocation durability (`src/emrys/evidence/storage_inventory/README.md:24-30`). Thus even a real two-node execution of this protocol would establish same-node contention and cross-node read/durability observations, not cross-node lock contention. This scope matters because Run lifecycle uses a shared `flock` acquisition mutex (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/lifecycle.py:698-762`). The local owner tests run both phases on one host using an environment toggle (`tests/evidence/storage_inventory/test_storage_inventory.py:360-382`); no institutional two-node execution was performed here. Preserve the same-node lock, hard-link, rename, retained-hash, UID/GID, and receipt defenses. State the narrower lock evidence or obtain a separate live cross-node contention proof before claiming it.

### A01-141 — Reused compute receipt can receive fresh pass wording

`qualify_compute` reuses an existing compute receipt after `_validate_compute` checks its JSON header, ordered roots, probe-directory names, and hash-field shapes, without rereading the retained probe files (`src/emrys/evidence/storage_inventory/qualification.py:480-515,551-568`). Doctor then always prints `Compute checks passed; storage evidence: ... Head finalization is required` for that return (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/doctor.py:1545-1569`). If a retained compute probe has disappeared or changed since its receipt was written, the message describes current checks as passed although this call only admitted the receipt structure. Head finalization does read the probes and refuses missing or changed evidence (`src/emrys/evidence/storage_inventory/qualification.py:595-625,648-660`); a direct owner test deletes `visible.bin` and observes finalization refusal with no final receipt (`tests/evidence/storage_inventory/test_storage_inventory.py:309-339`). This is a source-composed intermediate diagnostic gap, not a final-site false pass or an observed Doctor invocation. Distinguish reused receipt from newly performed checks, or re-admit retained probes before using fresh-pass wording; preserve immutable compute evidence and head-side final authority.

### A01-142 — Orchestration record profile argument is inert

`validate_record` and `load_record` accept a contextual `profile` argument, serialize it into a separate cache-key component, and pass it to `_validate_record_uncached`; that function never reads the argument while validating the named record's schema and internal semantics (`src/emrys/contracts/orchestration/api.py:695-760,763-773`). The coordinator's `admit_canonical_record` forwards the same optional argument (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/_inspection_admission.py:131-150`). A pinned source search found no supported in-repository call supplying a non-default profile to that admission helper. The parameter therefore adds canonicalization, cache state, and the appearance of contextual proof without supplying cross-record binding. A related `expected_tasks(execution, profile)` signature ignores its profile and projects owner/scope rows solely from the admitted Analysis and Execution Plan (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/_inspection_admission.py:229-266`). Task, inspection, and Snakefile callers thread the profile through that inert projection (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/task.py:1275-1284`; `src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/_inspection_evidence.py:89-103`; `src/emrys/workflow/Snakefile:128`). This is an API clarity and reduction candidate, not evidence of a current Run or Attempt admission bypass.

The actual successor-Run validator compares the profile's functional specification with the Execution Plan and an Attempt's profile digest with the admitted profile (`src/emrys/contracts/orchestration/application_model.py:1062-1112`); inspection and Snakefile admission call that separate validator before task projection, and direct tests mutate those bindings (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/inspection.py:379-389`; `src/emrys/workflow/Snakefile:64-74`; `tests/contracts/orchestration/test_application_model_contracts.py:714-782`). Preserve those cross-record checks and the named-record schema/identity checks. Check external uses of the exported arguments before removing them and the cache-key work; do not describe `validate_record(..., profile=...)` or `expected_tasks(..., profile)` themselves as profile/Attempt agreement checks.

### A01-143 — Scheduler-ID negatives exercise direct-placement null policy

The contract test named for noncanonical scheduler IDs supplies all six values (`"0"`, `"00"`, `"01"`, `"-1"`, integer `1`, and `True`) to a **direct** Workflow Attempt placement (`tests/contracts/orchestration/test_orchestration_contracts.py:625-642`). The Attempt schema requires `scheduler_job_id` to be null for every direct placement, independently of the positive-decimal pattern used by Slurm placement (`src/emrys/contracts/schemas/orchestration/v1/workflow_attempt.schema.json:216-269`). Thus the test's named cases would still reject if the Slurm-only pattern weakened. An adjacent positive fixture already constructs a valid Slurm placement (`tests/contracts/orchestration/test_orchestration_contracts.py:592-612`); changing only its ID would isolate schema admission. The supported placement writer separately refuses noncanonical Slurm IDs, and its direct test exercises that guard (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/execution_profile.py:293-313`; `tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_execution_profile.py:570-585`). Preserve direct-null and writer protection. This is a source-derived independent schema-oracle gap, not evidence that a current runtime Attempt admits invalid IDs; no schema mutation or test was run here.

### A01-144 — Wrong-Run artifact fixture proves field closure only

The test titled `test_run_summary_reconciles_inventory_order_run_identity_and_rollups` inserts `run_id: different_run` into the first artifact entry and asserts an `additional properties` error (`tests/contracts/artifacts/test_artifact_schema_contracts.py:348-361`). ### A01-145 — Selected-candidate HTML lacks a label/value oracle

The template renders control and treatment means both in candidate evidence and the selected-candidate index (`src/emrys/reporting/templates/run_report.html.j2:63-73,224-239`). The scientific view passes the selected-candidate projection through to that template (`src/emrys/reporting/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking_report/view.py:110-144`), while its structural validator checks the candidate roster, rank, and group presence without comparing rendered rate cells with their labels (`src/emrys/reporting/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking_report/validation.py:206-222`). The projection test checks the correct control and treatment Decimal values (`tests/reporting/test_candidate_display.py:303-319`), but the populated rendered-report test merely checks that `control mean`, `11.00%`, `treatment mean`, and `31.00%` each occur somewhere (`tests/reporting/test_report.py:1023-1055`). Swapping the two template value expressions could preserve those assertions while mislabeling a candidate. The independent HTML hash golden calls the renderer without a selected-candidate projection, so it would not detect that swap (`tests/contract_integration/independent_contract_goldens/test_independent_contract_goldens.py:136-150,259-270`). This is a source-inferred regression-oracle gap, not an observed wrong report. Bind literal control and treatment values to their labels inside the same named candidate in **both** the evidence article and the selected-candidate index; one scoped assertion leaves the other render site exposed. Preserve projection values, source admission, structural checks, and published-byte hashes as different defenses; exact output hashes do not establish semantic pairing.

### A01-146 — Failed validation-lane logs have no byte bound

The test policy says failed, interrupted, and peer-cancelled local lanes retain bounded diagnostics (`docs/design/TEST_BASELINE.md:87-93`). The `all-checks` runner copies each complete lane log to a durable temporary file with `shutil.copyfile` and no size cap (`tests/tools/run_validation.py:224-240`). On quiet failure it also reads the complete log as text, then again as bytes to inspect the final newline (`:243-249,445-455`); signal and peer-cancellation paths retain whole logs too (`:395-401,466-485`). Its verbose stream thread calls `reader.read()` without a size, loading all bytes newly available at each iteration before printing them (`:252-263`). The direct tests assert retention and process cleanup using small diagnostic strings, without a maximum byte case (`tests/test_validation_orchestrator.py:376-458,461-510,513-559`). A noisy failure can therefore consume unbounded temporary disk, quiet failure can allocate memory proportional to its complete log, and a verbose lane can allocate memory proportional to a burst of output. This is a source-level resource and policy-wording limit; no large failing lane or full gate was run. Clarify whether `bounded` means per-lane count or byte size, then set an evidence-preserving cap or narrow the claim. Preserve the failure transcript needed for diagnosis and the existing cancellation behavior.

### A01-147 — Blank condition passes the standalone manifest validator

The public CLI registers `manifest` as an owner of `emrys validate` (`src/emrys/__main__.py:44-48,318-333`). That owner's required-column roster includes `condition`, but row validation strips values and adds a nonempty condition to the displayed set without appending an error for a blank one (`src/emrys/ingestion/sample_manifest_admission/validator.py:18-20,112-160`). A row whose other required values are valid can therefore reach `Manifest validation passed` and `Conditions: none` (`:168-190`). The test named for empty required fields supplies `control` for condition and asserts only the sample-ID and FASTQ-path errors (`tests/ingestion/sample_manifest_admission/test_validate_manifest.py:190-202`); a source scan of that owner test found no blank-condition case. The owner README lists condition as required and says empty identities are rejected (`src/emrys/ingestion/sample_manifest_admission/README.md:3-8`). The separate Project/Step 08 manifest parser refuses blank required values (`src/emrys/contracts/scientific_evidence/step08.py:326-354`). This is a source-composed standalone-command false-pass and a contract-alignment question, not a demonstrated Project admission defect or a run test. Decide whether the standalone command promises a nonempty condition, then exercise that value by itself while retaining the distinct Project/Step 08 guard and optional path-existence contract.

### A01-148 — Private synthetic negative cases have no traced public invalid caller

One onboarding test directly calls private synthetic helpers with zero modulus and out-of-range sample, unique-template, and duplicate indexes, and the adjacent test calls the FASTQ generator with mate `3` (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_onboarding.py:2234-2243,2274-2293`). Those branches are live development guards (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/synthetic_fixture.py:176-219,432-440`), but the public fixture command selects one of two frozen profiles, and its member builder supplies only `enumerate(SAMPLES)` and mates `(1, 2)`; neutral loops supply indexes from their declared counts (`:84-110,394-429,654-684,732-746`). No supported invalid-index/modulus caller was traced in the pinned tree. The `modulus == 1` fast path is a narrower source-backed reduction: without it, `seed % 1 or 1` and `gcd(1, 1)` still return 1 (`:176-185`), while one direct assertion pins only that branch (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_onboarding.py:2283`). The same test's default-profile zero-capacity assertion does not exercise generated neutral pairs, because that profile declares none (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/synthetic_fixture.py:84-95,394-429`; `tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_onboarding.py:2279-2283`). Zero modulus, invalid indexes, and invalid mate still have distinct controlled-error behavior for malformed private calls, so they are **provisional test and guard reduction questions**, not safe whole-helper deletions; future internal errors and external private use remain possible. Assess each branch's fault and measured cost before retirement. Keep the public unsupported-profile refusal, generator pair-count fault, generated-mate/geometry checks, and gzip streaming and flush assertions, which protect different behavior (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_onboarding.py:2136-2177,2245-2271,2295-2326`). The shared Step 08/09 support builder explicitly disclaims independent science claims (`tests/scientific_evidence_test_support.py:1-5`); its placeholder VCF/receipt data support Python boundary tests, while guarded real-R and independent Step 09 numerical corpora protect other faults (`tests/scientific_evidence_test_support.py:437-480`; `tests/stages/cohort_candidate_preprocessing/test_step_08_vcf_preprocessing.R:715-783`; `tests/analyses/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking/test_step_09_cmh_oracle.py:108-178`). No code was changed or synthetic fixture executed for this finding.

### A01-149 — Watch retains and reparses full scheduler streams

The interactive Watch path supplies a persistent cache for Scheduler stdout and stderr during snapshot refresh (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/_inspection_presentation.py:664-693,1096-1105,1162-1194`). Each `dashboard.StreamCache` starts without a byte or line tail, reads all initial file bytes and every append into a growing bytearray, then decodes and sanitizes the entire retained buffer for workflow and identity parsing on refresh (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/dashboard.py:249-343,349-442`). The direct test explicitly protects full history with 20,000 repeated lines and an append (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_dashboard.py:528-555`). The operator runbook promises full workflow diagnostic history, while other selected log tails are limited to 64 KiB and 256 lines (`docs/operations/RUNBOOK.md:113-118`; `src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/_inspection_presentation.py:371-393`). Full scheduler-stream cache memory and repeated parse work therefore grow with log size over a long Run even when the visible tail is bounded. This is a source-level resource ceiling and documented choice, not an observed slow or failed Watch or reason to truncate retained log files. Measure real log size and refresh cost before selecting a bounded in-memory projection or incremental parser; preserve user access to full historical diagnostics, generation-change refusal, and the scheduler/log versus Run-authority distinction.

### A01-150 — Bioconductor metadata check selects packages by the fields it validates

The local shell contract gate parses `renv.lock`, then tests canonical Bioconductor `Source`, `RemoteType`, and `Repository` fields only for a package with at least one of those fields already equal to the expected value (`tests/shell/test_local_r_environment.sh:83-121`). At the pinned lock, 28 records have `Source: Bioconductor` and all three fields are canonical; `AnnotationDbi` is one concrete example with a Bioconductor URL and remote ref (`src/emrys/renv.lock:19-27,68-71`). A future edit that changes or drops all three fields for a formerly Bioconductor package removes that package from the checked class, so the gate can pass despite losing the provenance promised by the owner README (`src/emrys/renv/README.md:10-14`). In a disposable lock copy, changing `Biostrings` to `Source=Repository`, `RemoteType=standard`, and `Repository=CRAN` left its name and top-level repositories intact; the exact extracted gate policy exited zero with empty stderr (`src/emrys/renv.lock:407-411,463`). The static package-name roster and `BiocVersion` presence checks still protect declared names, not source classification (`tests/shell/test_local_r_environment.sh:70-75,123-124`); the separate R environment checker assesses release and restored-library agreement, not an independent package-class roster (`scripts/check_r_environment.R:112-125`). This is a reproduced static-gate weakness, not a claim that the current lock is wrong, that package-manager restoration would accept the altered lock, or that a Run/CI lane was executed. Anchor the expected Bioconductor class to an independent supported package roster or lock identity before comparing its fields; preserve repository policy, opt-in activation, guarded computation, and runtime admission.

### A01-151 — R startup opt-in check has no effective default-mode oracle

The packaged profile currently calls `renv/activate.R` only inside the `EMRYS_USE_RENV=1` restoration branch (`src/emrys/.Rprofile:22-35,76-125`), consistent with the owner README's opt-in promise (`src/emrys/renv/README.md:3-6`). A tiny direct `grep -E` probe using that exact extracted pattern returned no match for even `source("renv/activate.R")`; indentation or a differently written unconditional call is also outside the static check. This is a regression-test gap, not a current default-startup failure or a hosted CI result. Add a small dynamic use=0 copied-profile fixture with a stub activation marker and require no activation or mutation, while preserving explicit restore, selected-library, and no-ambient-profile boundaries.

A literal `INPUT=$bam` argument assertion for Stage 04 and an exact expected input/reference line in Stage 05's fake output would cover those worker routes. These are separate trust boundaries. This is a source-inferred wrong-path detection gap, not an observed real-tool misroute or reason to retire either worker test. ### A01-158 — Picard artifact-index reader stops before trailing source content

The Step 04 metrics adapter calls `inspect_picard_metrics`, which breaks after the first same-width data row (`src/emrys/reporting/_artifact_index/registry.py:119`; `src/emrys/reporting/_artifact_index/inspection.py:240-242`; `src/emrys/reporting/_artifact_index/_text_genomic.py:231-258`). The Step 04 owner contract requires exactly one row in the metrics table while allowing later Picard tables, such as a histogram, after the table ends (`src/emrys/stages/duplicate_marking/CONTRACT.md:77-83`). Supported Run reporting first requires successful Results and then compares inspected artifact hashes with verified Task output bindings (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/reporting_operation.py:272-275,292-319,174-240`). Thus this is a direct artifact-index input-integrity and regression-oracle gap, not a demonstrated supported-Run false pass. Add direct-reader cases for a second contiguous metrics row and trailing malformed text; retain valid later Picard tables, the Step 04 validator, and Run source rechecks. A01-56 concerns a separate duplicate-column masking fault.

The focused test pins the input inventory header literally, but reads generated outputs with `DictReader` and asserts selected named fields and repeated bytes (`tests/evidence/reference_provenance/test_reference_provenance.py:17-27,177-184,215-265,298-321`). For example, exchanging only the `provenance_source` and `provenance_release` artifact header labels would leave the row values and the producer's self-check internally consistent, while the cited tests never access those two output fields. The existing fixture deliberately supplies `source` and `release1` as distinct values (`tests/evidence/reference_provenance/test_reference_provenance.py:51-62`), so a literal artifact-row assertion can detect a swapped pairing. This is a source-inferred regression-oracle gap for public evidence labels, not an observed changed file or an assertion that every output field lacks coverage. No other in-repository reader of the three generated TSV names was traced; external readers remain unknown. Pin all three ordered output-header lines literally and the distinct source/release value pair, while keeping the existing dry-run, content, re-observation, and multi-output recovery tests.

### A01-160 — Shared Run-summary scope-state reduction needs an independent mixed case

The shared status owner gives `failed` precedence over `incomplete`, `missing`, and `externally_unavailable`, and considers required artifacts alone when a scope has any (`src/emrys/contracts/artifacts/_artifact_contracts/run_summary_status.py:7-33`). Both the Run-summary producer and its semantic validator call that same reducer (`src/emrys/reporting/_run_summary/projection.py:23-45`; `src/emrys/contracts/artifacts/_artifact_contracts/run_summary_validation.py:100-115`). The direct contract test pins `failed` over missing availability for one artifact, the public valid summary fixture has one required missing artifact, and the failed-scope report test consumes a producer-built summary (`tests/contracts/artifacts/test_artifact_schema_contracts.py:316-335`; `tests/contracts/artifacts/fixtures/artifact_schema_v2/valid/run_summary.json:23-83`; `tests/reporting/test_report.py:597-616`). The existing aggregate count checks and order/identity checks remain useful (`src/emrys/contracts/artifacts/_artifact_contracts/run_summary_validation.py:118-143`), but they do not independently pin one scope containing both failed and missing artifacts or a required/optional conflict. A shared precedence regression could therefore produce a self-consistent scope state and survive those focused tests. Two small literal reducer cases would pin the policy: required failed plus required missing yields `failed`; required missing plus optional failed yields `missing`. Supply explicit expectation, completion, and availability fields rather than deriving expected states from the producer; retain the shared reducer and both producer/admission boundaries. This concerns per-scope `aggregate_state`, not A01-132's separate top-level `summary_state` compatibility decision.

### A01-161 — Selected figures scale with every admitted replicate pair

Candidate projection carries every pair from the admitted sample manifest (`src/emrys/reporting/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking_report/candidate_display.py:397-408`); Step 09 requires at least two matched strata but gives no upper bound (`src/emrys/contracts/scientific_evidence/step09.py:267-310`). The selected-context figure plots every complete pair in each selected candidate panel and repeats all pairs in its alt text (`src/emrys/reporting/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking_report/scientific_context_figures.py:695-715,806-825,879-885`). The paired-profile figure likewise plots each pair and lists all pair identities in a caption (`src/emrys/reporting/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking_report/figures.py:852-868,953-957`). Candidate selection is bounded to eight, but the pair work within each selected candidate is not (`src/emrys/reporting/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking_report/scientific_context_figures.py:953-957`). Its 4 MB SVG refusal is checked only after SVG rendering (`src/emrys/reporting/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking_report/figures.py:38,164-176`). A focused fixture exercises eight pairs and bounded visual notes, not a large admitted pair roster (`tests/reporting/test_figures.py:434-470`). Measure a realistic upper admitted roster and compare bounded visual presentation with retained exact pair evidence before changing selection or output policy. A01-101 concerns a different receipt-field size limit.

### A01-162 — Duplicate unavailable selected-context specification and discarded construction

`unavailable_scientific_context_figures` builds three unavailable specs, including a selected-context track (`src/emrys/reporting/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking_report/scientific_context_figures.py:993-1029`). Its sole traced caller, when context is absent, takes only the first two and discards that third constructed value (`:1041-1046`); the same selected-context specification is written again for an absent candidate projection (`:1054-1070`). The two paths can have different unavailable reasons, so a reduction must keep context absence separate from candidate absence. This is a source-backed code and construction reduction candidate, not an output defect or approved source edit. An owner-complete replacement could build the first two context specs and the selected-candidate spec once each; check external imports of the non-underscored helper and exact unavailable-result fixtures before removing its third return value.

### A01-163 — Reference-provenance re-observation loses declared-path continuity

Inventory admission rejects a symlink at the final lexical component, but an ancestor symlink is allowed when its initial resolution stays inside the reference base (`src/emrys/evidence/reference_provenance/_reference_inventory.py:68-94`). It stores that initially resolved path in the item (`:103-115`); observation reads `item.path` (`src/emrys/evidence/reference_provenance/_reference_contigs.py:20-46`). On `--execute`, the owner rehashes the inventory bytes and reobserves the same stored item paths, without resolving the declared lexical paths again (`src/emrys/evidence/reference_provenance/reconciler.py:129-165`). The owner tests reject a final-component link and an ancestor link that initially escapes the base, and mutate a resolved file after the first observation; they do not retarget an admitted in-base ancestor link (`tests/evidence/reference_provenance/test_reference_provenance.py:344-368,389-427`).

A tiny disposable fixture declared all required members through `current/` pointing to in-base `old/`. After the first actual owner observation, `current/` was retargeted to in-base `new/` with different FASTA bytes. The actual `reconcile_from_args(..., execute=True)` returned zero, published three TSVs, and reported `overall_status=pass`; the published FASTA digest matched `old/genome.fa`, not the file then named by the declared path. This demonstrates an advisory owner-level path-continuity defect under a deterministic link switch, not a natural-race frequency, supported Run, Viking, or scientific result. Re-admit lexical-to-resolved identity before publication, including ancestor components, while preserving initial in-base admission, exact byte re-observation, ordered contig agreement, and the three-output recovery boundary. The output's resolved-path field is evidence of the initially selected file, but does not restore current declared-path meaning.

### A01-164 — Validation-report publisher can change parent after lock creation

The shared validation publisher checks that the immediate output parent exists, is a directory, and is not itself a symlink (`src/emrys/libraries/validation/publication.py:13-29`). It creates a lock, stages and replaces by pathnames, then rereads the final through the same pathname (`:30-70`); it does not bind the parent directory identity through that transaction. A standalone validator accepts an output `Path` and forwards it to this publisher (`src/emrys/libraries/validation/report.py:1-31`; `src/emrys/libraries/validation/runtime.py:27-49,75-95`). A tiny disposable fixture used `alias/reports/` with `alias` linked to `real/`; the immediate `reports/` parent was not a symlink. Retargeting `alias` to `other/` immediately before the publisher opened its staged file let the actual publisher return normally with the final in `other/reports/` and its lock left in `real/reports/`. Its final reread checked the new location, not the locked location. Existing focused tests check a missing immediate parent, existing lock, staged/final semantic validation, and rollback faults (`tests/libraries/test_validation_report.py:250-272,316-352,431-478,507-572`), which do not exercise ancestor retargeting.

This is a directly reproduced standalone owner-path publication and recovery defect, not a demonstrated full Run impact or a live concurrent attacker. Report row/roster validation, input snapshots, Task admission of a retained all-pass report, and artifact-index inspection make separate decisions at other boundaries (`src/emrys/libraries/validation/report.py:64-111`; `src/emrys/libraries/validation/runtime.py:40-49`; `src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/task.py:2765-2807`; `src/emrys/reporting/_artifact_index/inspection.py:151-188`). Preserve those and the distinct staged/predecessor/final semantic checks while binding lock, stage, final, and cleanup to one admitted parent identity. Check the supported output-path policy and add an ancestor-link retarget case before selecting the owner-level repair; a blanket assertion that all symlinked ancestors are invalid would be a policy change.

Reference-provenance publication has the same path-reuse mechanism across its separate three-output transaction: it rejects symlinks at the immediate output root/destination, then uses pathname-based lock, staged, final, backup, and cleanup operations (`src/emrys/evidence/reference_provenance/reconciler.py:52-79,90-126`). An ancestor retarget after lock creation could likewise redirect later names; its tests cover foreign locks and restoration failures but not that ancestor case (`tests/evidence/reference_provenance/test_reference_provenance.py:371-386,430-518`). This is an unprobed owner companion to the reproduced shared-publisher fault, not an additional observed defect class or reason to collapse the distinct three-output recovery tests.

### A01-165 — Step 10 reference-header reader leaks a raw exception

The Step 10 scientific-context contract independently checks that a bound FAI offset follows a matching FASTA header before rederiving candidate windows (`src/emrys/contracts/scientific_evidence/scientific_context.py:1069-1111,1131-1165`). A header containing only spaces passes the nonempty-string branch and then indexes the empty result of `header.split(maxsplit=1)` (`:1093-1099`). An isolated standard-library probe of that exact pinned helper body returned `chr1` for `>chr1\nA\n` and raw `IndexError` for `>   \nA\n`. The direct Step 10 validator wraps contract admission through `step08.attempt`, whose caught errors omit `IndexError` (`src/emrys/analyses/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking/scientific_context_projection/validator.py:88-109`; `src/emrys/contracts/scientific_evidence/step08.py:169-173`). This is a source-composed standalone diagnostic consequence, not a full Step 10 CLI, Run, or cluster reproduction.

Project reference admission uses a different shared FASTA parser that also raises for a whitespace-only header (A01-52; `src/emrys/libraries/references/contigs.py:22-33`; `src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/onboarding.py:1710-1716`). Repairing only that parser would leave this separate Step 10 reader unchanged; valid Project admission is an earlier boundary and limits any supported-Run inference from this malformed input. Add a focused direct contract case that requires a controlled invalid-header result, while retaining receipt digests, FAI bounds, and exact reference-window comparison.

### A01-173 — Benchmark producer can occupy the timing-output path

The optional benchmark requires an absent output root under a real parent (`scripts/benchmark_stage_resources.py:370-377`), then chooses `trial/producer.time.txt` before invoking the manifest's producer (`:443-460`). `_run_timed` reserves stdout and stderr with exclusive `xb`, but after the producer exits writes the usage summary through `Path.write_text` (`:225-261`). A producer or setup command that creates that filename, including a symlink, can therefore have its bytes truncated or its target followed when the script writes timing data. A tiny isolated execution of the exact `_run_timed` function body used a disposable producer to create `producer.time.txt` as a symlink to a same-directory sentinel; the producer exited zero, the symlink remained, and the sentinel's bytes were replaced by the `wall_seconds` record. This did not execute a complete benchmark manifest or write outside disposable scratch. The focused tests cover ordinary recorded timing and initial output/parent refusal, not occupation of the later timing path (`tests/test_benchmark_stage_resources.py:156-220,343-355`). Until `SETUP-02` permits whole-helper retirement (`docs/tasks/backlog_matrix.md:173`), reserve or publish the timing record exclusively and check the producer's behavior. Keep raw resource measurements, failed-repetition refusal, output-parent admission, and trial evidence distinct.

### A01-174 — Stop/cancel decision text predates the bounded public command

The ratified platform direction says there is no public stop/cancel command until a safe terminal interruption contract exists across supported placements (`docs/design/decisions/platform-direction.md:204-206`). The pinned installed CLI registers `stop` for one exact retained submission (`src/emrys/__main__.py:282-285`); the Runbook provides preview and `--execute` instructions and explicitly says transport exit does not prove Run completion (`docs/operations/RUNBOOK.md:139-155`). The coordinator contract limits execution to an admitted v3 request and matching scheduler observation, then separates cancellation transport from independently admitted Attempt/Task/terminal/recovery evidence (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/CONTRACT.md:682-713`). Direct public preview/execute tests characterize that bounded behavior (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_materialization.py:3684-3718,3805-3944`). This is a current source/document contradiction, not proof that all placements have terminal recovery or a safe general cancel API. Update the decision bullet to the narrow delivered policy and leave broader cancellation, terminal interruption, and institutional qualification open.

### A01-175 — Documentation gate does not bind Markdown source locations

`git_paths` admits a tracked or untracked Markdown path when `Path.is_file()` succeeds, which also follows a symlink (`scripts/documentation/validate_structure.py:73-95`). Required canonical documents likewise pass their existence check through `is_file()` and read an H1 through the path (`:119-126,199-206`). Local-link validation constrains each resolved **destination** to the worktree but does not constrain the resolved Markdown **source** (`:165-195`). A canonical path replaced with a symlink to an outside Markdown file with an H1 could therefore satisfy the required-page presence/title check and cause the gate to read content outside the checkout; inventoried source symlinks have the same location ceiling. The pinned tree contains no tracked Markdown symlink, and the negative canonical fixture covers missing/unheaded ordinary files, not this substitution (`tests/documentation/test_validate_structure.py:179-195`). This is source inference, not a reproduced gate pass, disclosure, or current-tree fault.

Derived owner `README.md` and `CONTRACT.md` checks use `is_file()`, and mirrored test-owner presence uses `is_dir()`, also without resolved containment (`scripts/documentation/validate_structure.py:219-232`). A future external symlink could satisfy those presence checks. This extends the source-location policy to derived owner documents and test directories; it does not show a current-tree substitution or diminish the independent Stage Map roster guard. Define whether canonical and inventoried Markdown/Mermaid, derived owner documents, and mirrored test directories must be real in-worktree paths, then enforce that location while keeping existing link, H1, owner, and diagram checks.

The execution/reporting decision says verbose and debug progressively expose console detail (`docs/design/decisions/execution-evidence-and-reporting.md:265-268`). The handler retains `debug` as an internal event-detail category, but projects every non-normal event with verbose rather than a second public debug level (`src/emrys/libraries/application_logging/handler.py:467-475,541-549`). The specialist `emrys debug storage-qualification` route is a separate command, not a console verbosity tier (`src/emrys/__main__.py:303-315`). This is source/document wording drift, not a logging failure. Align the design statement with normal/verbose console projection and durable log detail while preserving the internal event category and specialist command.

### A01-177 — Failed pre-entry Task terminal can claim bound files without a start

The `task-attempt` schema allows each `inputs` and `outputs` array to contain bound-file records (`src/emrys/contracts/schemas/orchestration/v1/task_attempt.schema.json:123-133`). Its `task_start_record: null` branch requires failed status, absent producer/validator/report, and unrechecked stable inputs, but does not require either array to be empty (`:186-220`). Orchestration semantic validation adds no pre-entry array condition (`src/emrys/contracts/orchestration/api.py:655-675,695-717`). The Task terminal reader checks the failed status when the start is absent and skips the start-to-input comparison, then returns after log/tree and abort-closure checks without a pre-entry input/output condition (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/task.py:2060-2080,2083-2137`). The producer initializes both arrays empty, and the focused contract test accepts that positive case but mutates only the `producer` field afterward (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/task.py:2463-2467`; `tests/contracts/orchestration/test_orchestration_contracts.py:794-817`).

This is a source-derived malformed-record admission ceiling, not an executed schema/Task fixture: the audit environment lacks `jsonschema`, and none was installed. A pre-entry terminal could misleadingly list file snapshots that no Task start bound. Inspection can retain that exact diagnostic terminal (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/_inspection_attempts.py:439-457`), but it does not promote an entry without a start to the latest verified Task (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/_inspection_evidence.py:161-199`). Preserve legitimate pre-entry failure diagnostics and positive abort closure; add a focused mutation of the known-valid pre-entry fixture with one nonempty input or output, then choose schema and reader refusal together. This is not a demonstrated false Run success.
