### A01-180 — Package facades include untraced re-export surface

The orchestration package initializer re-exports 12 names from `api.py` (`src/emrys/contracts/orchestration/__init__.py:3-31`). Production callers import the `api` submodule; the focused contract suite imports the package facade and exercises 11 of its re-exports (`tests/contracts/orchestration/test_orchestration_contracts.py:13,438-499`), while `load_record` has no traced facade caller. The shared validation initializer re-exports 29 names, three of which (`require_unchanged`, `row`, `run`) have no traced source/test package-facade caller (`src/emrys/libraries/validation/__init__.py:3-60`). Their underlying modules and other facade names remain live. These are possible export-surface reductions, not evidence that underlying functions, packages, or tests are obsolete; external importers may rely on the facade. A separate interface decision and complete import review must precede any change, with the direct owner behavior, contract tests, and installed wheel preserved.

### A01-181 — Synthetic Slurm journal artifact exceeds its stated diagnostic bound

The workflow owner README says only bounded, redacted setup and terminal diagnostics enter the selected real-Slurm infrastructure artifact (`.github/workflows/README.md:15-21`). On setup exit, the helper captures `sudo journalctl --no-pager -u munge -u slurmctld -u slurmd` into `journal.txt` without a line/byte bound or redaction step (`tests/tools/configure_ci_slurm.sh:25-59`). The workflow's terminal step repeats that unrestricted journal capture into `final-journal.txt`, then uploads the entire `slurm/` evidence directory with a 14-day artifact retention (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:1344-1359,1371-1383`). Focused workflow tests assert selected uploads and forbid database journal/configuration capture, but do not assert a journal-size or redaction policy (`tests/test_ci_workflow.py:785-813,823-896`). These are valuable failure diagnostics, and exclusion of private accounting files is a distinct protection.

This is a source-level artifact-promise discrepancy, not a measured hosted artifact size or observed secret disclosure; no CI run or artifact was inspected. Decide what bounded, redacted service diagnostic is sufficient for setup/terminal failure review, implement the same limit in both capture sites if that promise remains, and keep exact scheduler readiness and selected synthetic-evidence gates. A01-146 concerns the separate local validation-lane log retention policy.

### A01-182 — Guarded R runners repeat executable selection before distinct owner checks

The Step 08, Step 09, and Step 10 real-R shell runners each spend roughly 20–30 lines choosing an owner-specific `Rscript` override, then `RSCRIPT_BIN_OVERRIDE`, then the default executable; an explicit missing/nonexecutable request fails while an unavailable default exits with a named skip (`tests/stages/cohort_candidate_preprocessing/run_step_08_vcf_preprocessing_tests.sh:7-36`; `tests/analyses/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking/run_step_09_cmh_tests.sh:7-28`; `tests/analyses/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking/scientific_context_projection/run_scientific_context_projection_tests.sh:7-27`). Their spelling and diagnostics differ: Step 08 separates missing path from nonexecutable path, Step 09 uses a nested explicit selector, and Step 10 has an additional fake-R contract and package probe after selection (`tests/analyses/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking/scientific_context_projection/run_scientific_context_projection_tests.sh:29-50`). The later owner-specific help, foreign-directory, fake-contract, and guarded scientific checks are distinct; A01-85 separately notes that a direct `make real-r-test` can succeed if every wrapper skips.

This is a source-level test-script consolidation candidate, not a measured test-cost saving or grounds to retire any runner. Compare exact override precedence, error text, default skip exits, and each caller's environment before choosing a shared selector; only adopt it if caller-complete replacement reduces total maintained lines and leaves each owner check and guarded-R CI promise intact. A shared helper that merely adds another routing layer would not satisfy the architecture reduction guardrail.

### A01-183 — Synthetic E2E Step 09 status oracle accepts incoherent tables

The real synthetic E2E driver reads the all-sites and significant-sites TSVs, requires three distinct candidate IDs and one significant row, and checks only that the latter's `call_status` is `significant_up` or `significant_down` (`tests/tools/real_synthetic_e2e.py:872-910`). It does not validate all-sites status vocabulary, compare the selected candidate's status across files, or require the fixture's declared positive direction. The committed positive unit fixture itself labels two all-sites rows `not_significant` (`tests/test_real_synthetic_e2e.py:497-545`), a value outside the production Step 09 `STEP09_CALL_STATUSES` roster (`src/emrys/contracts/scientific_evidence/step09.py:156-171`). In an isolated tiny call to the driver helper, a three-row all-sites table with `b:effect_not_met` and a one-row significant table with `b:significant_up` returned success for candidate `b`; replacing the other two rows with invented `not_significant` also returned success. This is a reproduced **driver-oracle** false pass, not a supported Run false pass or hosted result.

Normal Task execution invokes the Step 09 owner validator and semantic all-pass gate before verified publication (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/task.py:2763-2807`). That validator calls the production row and subset checks, which enforce the status roster and exact significant-row agreement (`src/emrys/analyses/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking/validator.py:215-241,282-300`; `src/emrys/contracts/scientific_evidence/step09.py:359-378,744-770`); the E2E driver also requires a complete real Run inspection before applying its independent small oracle (`tests/tools/real_synthetic_e2e.py:914-947,1455-1466`). Replace the positive unit table with supported statuses and add direct driver cases for invalid all-sites status and a status disagreement, while preserving Run admission, native Step 09 validation, independent numeric oracles, and 130/100000 profile evidence limits. A01-22 separately asks whether this E2E assertion should pin the fixture's expected direction and effect sizes.

### A01-184 — Step 02b/03 worker fixtures miss the canonical BAM index name

The Step 02 worker indexes its canonical BAM as `<bam>.bai` (`src/emrys/stages/canonical_bam/step_02_sort_index_bam.sh:114-131`), and Run materialization declares that BAI as a stable input to both Step 02b and Step 03 (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/materialization.py:549-601`). Both downstream workers accept either `<bam>.bai` or the stripped `<stem>.bai` alternative (`src/emrys/evidence/canonical_bam_qc/step_02b_bam_qc.sh:46-51`; `src/emrys/evidence/rseqc_orientation/step_03_infer_strandedness_and_orientation.sh:43-50`). Their positive direct-worker fixtures create **only** the stripped spelling (`tests/evidence/canonical_bam_qc/test_step_02b_bam_qc.sh:78-95`; `tests/evidence/rseqc_orientation/test_step_03_infer_strandedness_and_orientation.sh:61-77`). A future regression that drops canonical-path acceptance could therefore leave both native fake suites green while preventing their supported Run worker routes from starting.

The alternate spelling is an intentional owner allowance (`src/emrys/evidence/canonical_bam_qc/CONTRACT.md:17-20`; `src/emrys/evidence/rseqc_orientation/CONTRACT.md:33-45`), so this is a source-inferred test-oracle gap rather than obsolete-path evidence or an observed Run failure. Make each primary success fixture canonical-index-only and add one narrow alternate-only case; keep missing-index refusal, input snapshots, native command failure, retained evidence, and later validators as distinct protections. No worker, CI, or Slurm run was made for this finding.

### A01-185 — Early native fakes do not bind the scientific input flags

Step 00a's worker forwards explicit FASTA and GTF paths to STAR, but its fake STAR emits index members from `--genomeDir` alone and the worker test checks resource and index flags without asserting the two reference paths (`src/emrys/stages/star_index/step_00a_build_star_index.sh:84-90`; `tests/stages/star_index/test_step_00a_build_star_index.py:49-75,166-180`). Step 01's fake STAR likewise generates fixed outputs from `--outFileNamePrefix` without reading `--readFilesIn` or `--genomeDir`, while the owner test checks resources, read group, gzip selector, and output presence rather than those path pairings (`src/emrys/stages/star_alignment/step_01_star_align.sh:61-77`; `tests/stages/star_alignment/test_step_01_star_align.sh:21-75`). Step 02's fake sort accepts but does not use its source alignment, and fake addreplacerg requires a nonempty source without checking that it is the selected sorted/input path (`tests/stages/canonical_bam/test_step_02_sort_index_bam.sh:72-140,180-206`; `src/emrys/stages/canonical_bam/step_02_sort_index_bam.sh:114-131`).

The Step 02b fake samtools dispatches on subcommand and returns fixed output regardless of the BAM argument (`src/emrys/evidence/canonical_bam_qc/step_02b_bam_qc.sh:48-63`; `tests/evidence/canonical_bam_qc/test_step_02b_bam_qc.sh:20-95`). Step 03's fake RSeQC ignores BAM/BED contents and its test asserts both path strings occur somewhere in the log, without binding each to `-i` or `-r` (`src/emrys/evidence/rseqc_orientation/step_03_infer_strandedness_and_orientation.sh:43-53`; `tests/evidence/rseqc_orientation/test_step_03_infer_strandedness_and_orientation.sh:21-77`). Exact expected flag/argument assertions would protect worker forwarding. Run materialization chooses declared inputs and Task snapshots them (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/materialization.py:381-471,513-601`; `src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/task.py:2600-2616`), but those boundaries do not prove the native command read the declared bytes. Step 00a's validator separately checks retained STAR parameter paths; the other validators inspect their outputs, not the input/output relationship (`src/emrys/stages/star_index/validator.py:113-143,169-179`; `src/emrys/stages/star_alignment/validator.py:45-118`; `src/emrys/stages/canonical_bam/validator.py:44-123`). This is a source-inferred regression-oracle gap, not an observed native or Run misroute. Keep each worker, validator, and failure/recovery test; A01-152 concerns the analogous Step 04/05 boundary, while A01-184 concerns index-name selection.

### A01-186 — Step 00c worker parses each sidecar twice in one phase

`validate_sidecar_agreement` first calls `validate_fai_file` and `validate_dict_file`; each checks nonempty input and runs its parser while discarding the parsed pairs (`src/emrys/stages/fasta_sidecars/step_00c_prepare_gatk_reference.sh:114-137`). The same function then runs `read_fai_pairs` and `read_dict_pairs` again to capture pairs for an unordered comparison (`:139-150`). No worker mutation occurs between these reads. The second parse is required for the comparison and already rejects malformed content, so the first parse is a bounded same-phase product-code and I/O reduction candidate, without measured runtime benefit. Capture each parser result once while retaining missing/empty diagnostics and the unordered worker comparison. The later Python validator's ordered FASTA/FAI/DICT correspondence protects a different boundary and remains necessary (`src/emrys/stages/fasta_sidecars/validator.py:56-108`; `tests/stages/fasta_sidecars/test_validate_step_00c_reference_sidecars.py:83-150,176-211`). A one-pass design still needs input-identity review; the current double parse is not a reliable external-mutation defense. No source change or worker run was made.

### A01-187 — Expanded Step 10 real-R fixture is not a valid Step 09 BH family

The guarded Step 10 real-R fixture clones source Step 09 rows into 40 successfully tested candidates: ten `significant_up`, twenty `effect_not_met`, and ten `significant_down`, then rewrites summary counts (`tests/analyses/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking/scientific_context_projection/test_real_r_projection.py:110-177`). It leaves each cloned row's original p-value and BH adjustment: `0.0005/0.0015` for up, `0.01/0.01` for effect-not-met, and `0.001/0.0015` for down (`tests/scientific_evidence_test_support.py:158-186,228-241`). Applying the pinned Step 09 global BH rule to those forty p-values gives `0.002` for the twenty up/down rows, not their retained `0.0015` (`src/emrys/contracts/scientific_evidence/step09.py:1318-1335`). A small calculation confirmed those group values; no R or owner test was run.

Step 10's projected-result admission deliberately excludes upstream Step 08 identity and global BH reconciliation (`src/emrys/contracts/scientific_evidence/step09.py:670-677`), so this fixture is still useful for real-R Step 10 motif/Fisher output and determinism (`tests/analyses/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking/scientific_context_projection/test_real_r_projection.py:218-338`). It cannot be promoted to native Step 08→09→10 or full-Run evidence; the full Step 09 owner semantic gate would reject this expanded family. Recompute a coherent forty-row BH family if an integrated upstream fixture is needed, or keep the present test explicitly scoped to Step 10. Preserve the independent Step 09 Python and guarded-R numerical oracles (`tests/analyses/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking/test_step_09_cmh_oracle.py:108-178`; `tests/analyses/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking/test_step_09_cmh_editing_site_calling.R:222-313`). This is a fixture/evidence ceiling, not an observed production calculation failure.

### A01-188 — Reference contigs can be published under a different byte digest

Reference provenance first observes and hashes each declared file, then `render` reopens the same paths to parse FASTA, FAI, DICT, GTF, BED12, and STAR contigs; its later prepublication observation compares only status, digest, and size with the first observation (`src/emrys/evidence/reference_provenance/_reference_contigs.py:20-47,61-124`; `src/emrys/libraries/references/contigs.py:18-19,50-70`; `src/emrys/evidence/reference_provenance/reconciler.py:132-165`). The rendered artifact TSV uses the **first** observation's hash while contig and summary rows use the separately read parse (`src/emrys/evidence/reference_provenance/_reference_render.py:28-105`). In a tiny disposable owner-path probe, I changed a coherent FASTA/FAI/DICT/STAR length bundle from A to B only while the real `render` parsed it, then restored A before the second observation. `reconcile_from_args(..., execute=True)` exited zero and published `overall_status=pass`, an artifact row with A's FASTA SHA-256, and a FASTA contig row with B's length 5. The fixture changed ordinary files at fixed paths, not symlinks; no supported Run or external concurrent process was used.

The existing post-observation mutation test leaves its changed FASTA in place and therefore exercises ordinary A→B drift, which the second observation rejects (`tests/evidence/reference_provenance/test_reference_provenance.py:389-427`). The A→B→A semantic mismatch is a distinct continuity fault; A01-163 concerns ancestor-link path identity and A01-164 output-parent publication. Bind contig parsing to the exact bytes whose digest is reported, or admit a stable file identity across parse and publication, while preserving the second observation, declared hash checks, transactional output validation, and failure recovery. This reproduction does not establish that an institutional Run actually encountered this race.

### A01-189 — One-hit Step 10 fixture hides the nearest-bin counting distinction

The shared scientific-context fixture inserts at most one `TGTACA` motif per hit-bearing candidate and computes each position bin's `candidate_with_motif_count` from the same `hits_in_bin` value as `hit_count` (`tests/scientific_context_test_support.py:99-103,271-289,326-344`). Production's policy is different for a multi-hit candidate: the R producer counts every motif hit in `hit_count` but counts the candidate once in the bin of its nearest hit, breaking equal-distance ties by leftmost start; Python admission reconstructs that same distinction (`src/emrys/analyses/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking/scientific_context_projection/scientific_context_projection.R:659-667,750-772`; `src/emrys/contracts/scientific_evidence/scientific_context.py:788-813,910-933`). With a 201-base context centered at offset 100 and separated motifs beginning at positions 90 and 105, the two hits occupy bins `-10` and `0`; each bin has one hit, but only bin `-10` has one candidate under the tie rule. The current shared builder would assign one candidate to both bins, so it cannot provide that discriminating positive result without revision.

This is a source-inferred fixture/oracle ceiling, not a demonstrated current R or Python miscount. Add one literal two-hit positive case that pins both hit counts, the one nearest-candidate count, and tie behavior, while retaining the existing one-hit corpus, real-R projection, and independent Python admission. A01-187 separately limits the expanded Step 09 statistical family used by that real-R fixture.

### A01-190 — Artifact-index source metrics are not bound to the recorded bytes

`inspect_source` first snapshots and hashes the declared path, then reopens that path for adapter parsing and metrics (`src/emrys/reporting/_artifact_index/inspection.py:67-81,134-150`; `src/emrys/reporting/_artifact_index/_text_common.py:12-25`). For a symlink, `stat_source` saves its link text and the target's stat fields, hashes through the path, and compares target stat/link text before returning (`src/emrys/reporting/_artifact_index/core.py:258-349`). A temporary A→B→A retarget around either the hash open or the later parser open can pair B's hash with A's parsed row count, or A's hash with B's parsed row count, while leaving A's target identity and link text at both checks. The later publication recheck normally compares stat fields without hashing source content (`src/emrys/reporting/_artifact_index/context.py:220-239`). The `step01_star_log_v1` adapter is one simple nonempty-text example (`src/emrys/reporting/_artifact_index/registry.py:111-115`).

The direct fixture retargets a source symlink persistently and expects publication refusal (`tests/reporting/test_artifact_adapters.py:978-1001`), and another test explicitly verifies metadata-only source rechecks (`:1744-1764`). Neither isolates a temporary pivot between the byte and semantic reads. Verified Task output symlinks are refused at Run admission (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/task.py:530-570,2265-2278`), and processing-source bindings have their own retained-hash checks; a supported Run would require a more specific timed mutation and has not been shown to publish false metrics. This is a source-inferred direct artifact-index continuity gap. Bind the adapter parse to the exact bytes recorded as its digest or a stable descriptor identity while retaining persistent retarget, missing/failed artifact, Task, and receipt-last transaction tests.

### A01-191 — Step 07 worker test reads two scientific expectations from production

Supported Run materialization invokes the Step 07 worker without either `--filter-expression` or `--max-depth`, so its parser defaults select those policies (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/materialization.py:697-725`; `src/emrys/stages/partitioned_cohort_mpileup/producer.py:19-23,55-57`). The owner contract states the exact 10,000,000 maximum depth, filter `INFO/AD[1-]>2 & MAX(FORMAT/DP)>20`, and DP/AD/ADF/ADR/SP annotations (`src/emrys/stages/partitioned_cohort_mpileup/CONTRACT.md:42-45`). The fake-bcftools command test pins the maximum depth literally but forms its expected annotation and filter arguments from `producer.ANNOTATIONS` and `producer.DEFAULT_FILTER` (`tests/stages/partitioned_cohort_mpileup/test_partitioned_cohort_mpileup_producer.py:260-280`). A source change to either constant would change the supported Run command and that test's expected command together. No changed policy or native bcftools execution was observed here.

Pin those two contract literals independently in the existing argv assertion. Preserve its exact orientation, sample order, selector, resource, receipt, and staged-output checks, as well as the separate Step 07 VCF validator and Run Task snapshots. A01-01/20 concern report-admission ceilings rather than this producer regression oracle.

### A01-192 — Reporting stage cleanup can remove a replacement directory

The shared `remove_owned_stage` checks that a stage name contains its token and that `lstat` still reports its recorded directory device/inode, then calls `shutil.rmtree(path)` by pathname (`src/emrys/reporting/_files.py:85-100`). Both artifact-index and HTML report publishers call it after a parent-directory check (`src/emrys/reporting/_artifact_index/publication.py:251-261`; `src/emrys/reporting/_run_report/publication.py:223-241`). A same-parent replacement between the child inode check and the path-based remove can redirect deletion to an unowned directory even when the parent remains stable.

A tiny disposable call to the actual shared helper interposed at `rmtree`: it renamed the verified stage aside, installed a foreign same-name directory, and allowed the remove to proceed. The helper returned normally, the foreign directory was gone, and the original owned stage remained. This is a direct helper-path reproduction, not a report transaction or observed concurrent Run. Owner tests inject cleanup errors and parent replacement, but not a same-name stage swap at final deletion (`tests/reporting/test_report.py:1527-1551`; `tests/reporting/test_artifact_adapters.py:1801-1825`). Bind cleanup's final operation to the admitted stage identity and retain the existing stage, parent, lock, recovery, and receipt-last defenses; do not treat the parent check as a child ownership proof.

### A01-193 — Recovery-marker creation is best effort despite a concrete path message

The shared recovery helper suppresses both OS and owner errors from its admission callback and marker write (`src/emrys/reporting/_files.py:103-112`). On an incomplete artifact-index rollback, the publisher calls that helper and then tells the operator to preserve the lock **and recovery paths**, even if marker creation failed (`src/emrys/reporting/_artifact_index/publication.py:239-248`). The HTML report publisher also uses the helper but describes the broader retained recovery state (`src/emrys/reporting/_run_report/publication.py:203-217`). Existing fault tests assert the marker under successful marker writes after rollback or cleanup failure; they do not inject marker-write failure (`tests/reporting/test_artifact_adapters.py:1695-1741`; `tests/reporting/test_report.py:1493-1551`).

This is a source-level operator-diagnostic and evidence ceiling, not proof that the surviving lock or owned outputs are lost. Test the marker-failure branch with exact retained-state assertions and make the error describe the objects that actually remain. Preserve successful recovery-marker creation, lock retention, ambiguous-state refusal, and owner rollback tests.

### A01-194 — Oversized PDF startxref aborts artifact-index preflight

The PDF adapter requires a version header and terminal `startxref`/EOF, but its regular expression accepts an unbounded decimal offset from a tail of up to 65,536 bytes and calls bare `int()` on it (`src/emrys/reporting/_artifact_index/binary_readers.py:221-246`). Under the local Python 3.14.5 default decimal-conversion limit of 4,300 digits, a tiny standard-library check confirmed that a 5,000-digit offset raises `ValueError`. The PDF reader translates `OSError`, but not `ValueError`; `inspect_source` turns only `ArtifactIndexError` into a per-artifact `failed` record (`src/emrys/reporting/_artifact_index/inspection.py:146-213,243-245`). Context preparation has no per-row exception boundary (`src/emrys/reporting/_artifact_index/context.py:172-179`).

The supported Run reporting wrapper does catch `ValueError` as a controlled **whole reporting preflight failure** (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/reporting_operation.py:27-36,315-323`); this is not an unhandled Run crash or a false PDF pass. The existing binary negative fixture checks only truncated/signature PDFs and expects a failed artifact row (`tests/reporting/test_artifact_adapters.py:1562-1588`). Add one well-formed header with a 5,000-digit terminal offset and require the PDF's `adapter_validation_failed` row, preserving other artifact rows and the whole-operation error catch for genuinely unclassifiable failures. The digit-limit behavior depends on the executing Python setting; the package supports Python 3.11+ (`pyproject.toml:12`). No full builder or Run was executed with this input.

### A01-195 — Generic row-count metric repeats the artifact source count

For every inspected artifact with a row count, `build_metrics` emits a generic `source_row_count` metric in addition to `source.row_count` (`src/emrys/reporting/_artifact_index/inspection.py:347-363`). The canonical summary projects that source field directly to `run_summary.tsv`, while its `qc_summary.tsv` iterates all metrics and therefore repeats the count as a QC row (`src/emrys/reporting/_run_summary/projection.py:109-170`). A direct view test explicitly asserts the repeated metric count (`tests/reporting/test_artifact_run_summary.py:123-139`). Step 10 reconciliation consumes the source field rather than this metric (`src/emrys/reporting/_artifact_index/reconcile_step10.py:35-36`), and a tracked-source search found no separate product consumer of the generic metric ID.

This is a maintained-state and QC-row reduction candidate only if the public metric contract permits it. Retiring the generic metric would change the canonical index and QC summary contents, metric ordering, and the direct test; unknown external QC readers may rely on it. Preserve the source row count, exact TSV headers, all native metrics, summary/receipt hashes, and independent report consumers. Resolve public compatibility before any implementation; no output change or cost measurement was made.

### A01-196 — Step 06 direct evidence can admit an empty orientation group

The Step 06 producer counts the input and both output BAMs and refuses zero input or either empty `FWD_like`/`REV_like` group before writing its counts table (`src/emrys/stages/mechanical_orientation/producer.py:140-156`; `src/emrys/stages/mechanical_orientation/CONTRACT.md:42-47`). Its direct negative test covers both zero-group branches (`tests/stages/mechanical_orientation/test_mechanical_orientation_producer.py:198-218`). The standalone validator checks group flag arithmetic, total/assigned arithmetic, and positive input, but has no positive lower bound for either merged group (`src/emrys/stages/mechanical_orientation/validator.py:88-114`). Reporting's Step 06 native reconciliation likewise checks flag and total arithmetic plus the fraction without a nonzero-group predicate (`src/emrys/reporting/_artifact_index/reconcile_native.py:139-183`). A reporting adapter fixture sets `rev_like_records=0` with coherent other counts and expects the artifact to be `complete` (`tests/reporting/test_artifact_adapters.py:1591-1619`).

Thus direct report and index admission can describe a producer-impossible counts family as valid when the other structural sources satisfy their separate checks. This is a source/test trace, not a newly executed validator fixture or a supported Run false pass; normal Step 06 production retains the nonzero guard. A01-27 concerns BAM recounting, A01-76 row width, and A01-77 rounding, which are distinct. Decide whether standalone validation and artifact indexing promise the producer's nonempty-group policy, then pin zero-group cases at both read boundaries while preserving group arithmetic, BAM/BAI structure, Task snapshots, and the producer's refusal.

### A01-197 — Retained summary reinspection ignores recomputed metrics

Retained Run-summary validation re-prepares an artifact context from current sources and rechecks those inputs, then compares each recorded artifact with its recomputed record only for `source`, `availability_status`, and `completion_status` (`src/emrys/reporting/transaction_validation.py:658-708`). The artifact schema permits a well-typed but arbitrary changed metric value, while artifact semantics constrain source path and status/reason/error shape, not metric equality with the source (`src/emrys/contracts/schemas/artifacts/v2/artifact_record.schema.json:81-99`; `src/emrys/contracts/artifacts/_artifact_contracts/artifact.py:11-31`). Later checks compare the two TSV files' bytes with hashes and sizes declared in the manifest, not their metric cells with recomputed records (`src/emrys/reporting/transaction_validation.py:709-757`).

A direct regression can start from a complete fixture, change one numeric metric in a canonical manifest while keeping the source, status, and existing QC TSV unchanged, and pass the revised manifest SHA as the expected receipt identity. The source predicts retained admission with an internally inconsistent metric view; this has not been executed. Existing mutation tests keep the original expected SHA or mutate current source bytes and therefore exercise ledger tamper/source-drift refusal instead (`tests/reporting/test_transaction_validation.py:383-417,479-547`). The verified ledger protects an **already** published manifest from later edits, and prepared-context byte equality protects one in-progress publication (`src/emrys/reporting/transaction_validation.py:717-729,798-829`); neither independently validates an initially wrong, newly ledger-bound metric. Bind retained semantic metrics and other claimed artifact fields to the reinspection at the chosen contract boundary, while retaining byte/receipt/roster checks. A01-131 separately concerns creation-time human TSV cell mapping, and A01-190 concerns hash/parse source continuity. No supported Run with a wrong metric was observed.

### A01-198 — Processing reuse identity omits the installed Python lock

Full Run implementation identity hashes shared source content together with the admitted installed package's Python-lock SHA-256 (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/run_implementation.py:185-210,230-257`). The separate Step 00–06 `processing_implementation_identity` hashes a narrower source closure without that lock (`:212-229`). `processing_compatibility_sha256` includes that narrower digest, selected tool content IDs, backend semantics, Reference, and resources, but has no Python-lock field (`src/emrys/contracts/orchestration/application_model.py:546-635`). The processing-source validator compares this compatibility digest and Reference/sample bindings, not the source and target full implementation digests (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/inspection.py:635-670`).

Consequently two independently admitted Runs can have different lock-attested full identities yet the same processing compatibility if their selected code, backend, and tool identities agree. This is not proof that a changed lock alters Step 00–06 output: Doctor separately requires a pinned Snakemake version and selected native tools (`src/emrys/resources/runtime/runtime_policy.tsv:2-13`), and source Task outputs remain bound by hashes. But Snakemake runs as a Python module through the interpreter, while Run tool projection keeps content IDs rather than a dependency-tree identity (`src/emrys/resources/runtime/runtime_policy.tsv:3-5`; `src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/doctor.py:304-366`; `src/emrys/contracts/orchestration/application_model.py:503-530`). The owner contract says processing semantics may not change across reuse (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/CONTRACT.md:887-895`); current compatibility tests vary source code and selected tool IDs, not lock metadata (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_materialization.py:1772-1837`).

This is an identity-scope and regression-oracle question, not a demonstrated unsafe reuse or a request to require the whole unrelated lock by default. Decide which Python dependency changes can affect Step 00–06, then test a lock-only difference against that policy and bind the minimal required dependency closure. Preserve the distinct full Run identity, Doctor's version checks, source/target receipt admission, exact artifact hashes, and intentional downstream-only reuse.

### A01-199 — Native runtime probe can be weakened by same-count policy edit

The installed `runtime_policy.tsv` currently assigns `bcftools` a `tool_version` check with `--version` and an exact 1.21 expectation (`src/emrys/resources/runtime/runtime_policy.tsv:12`). The loader trusts the check type, probe arguments, and expectation in that installed row; `runtime_profile_checks` replaces its target with the selected binary but preserves the other fields (`src/emrys/evidence/runtime_availability/_profile_contract.py:37-53,327-363`). Probe dispatch follows `check_type`; `path_visibility` with `file_readable` accepts a readable regular file without executing it (`src/emrys/evidence/runtime_availability/_probes.py:351-380,383-410`). Therefore replacing only that policy row's type, arguments, and expectation could retain the 26 IDs while making a site-owned Doctor/Run readiness check accept a wrong-version bcftools file. The current row is correct, and Step 07 still executes the selected tool (`src/emrys/stages/partitioned_cohort_mpileup/producer.py:250-326`); this is a future regression risk, not current false readiness.

The discovery test checks a 26-check count and selected targets/arguments for some rows, but no independent bcftools probe contract (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_onboarding.py:2918-2930`). Runtime owner tests exercise selected GATK/Java policy and synthetic probe dispatch, not a changed bcftools row (`tests/evidence/runtime_availability/test_runtime_availability.py:501-551`). The managed CI lane installs locked bcftools and invokes its version command but does not test the installed policy semantics for a site-provided runtime (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:365-405`; `src/emrys/resources/runtime/pixi.toml:12`). Keep those distinct availability and roster defenses. Pin the supported bcftools probe type, arguments, and version independently of the mutable policy asset, or establish a stronger tool-roster contract for every required native probe.

The positive Doctor fixture derives its fake R namespace versions from the installed policy being tested, then expects the resulting check IDs/status (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_doctor.py:2139-2145,2197-2203,2238-2242`). The onboarding case asserts only the check count and selected Rscript arguments (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_onboarding.py:2919-2929`). A policy-only namespace-version drift could therefore leave these local mocks green. This is an oracle-coupling inference, not evidence that the current namespace versions are wrong; pin the intended namespace requirements outside that same policy-derived fake while retaining real Doctor probes.

### A01-200 — Direct Step 10 contract can admit changed input bytes

The scientific-context receipt binds six input paths and hashes; `_bound_path` checks each hash on one open (`src/emrys/contracts/scientific_evidence/scientific_context.py:981-995,1346-1369`). The transaction then rereads the motif catalog, Step 09 tables, FASTA/FAI, and all-sites candidates for semantic reconciliation and returns without checking those input bytes against the receipt again (`:1370-1408`). A byte substitution after hash admission that preserves parsed values could therefore make the direct contract return a valid semantic result paired with stale recorded input hashes. This is source inference, with no deterministic input-race reproduction or full Run.

A01-116 separately reproduces the analogous output race. The Step 10 validation CLI snapshots discovered receipt paths and rechecks them before report publication (`src/emrys/analyses/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking/scientific_context_projection/validator.py:92-110`; `src/emrys/libraries/validation/runtime.py:27-47`), and supported Run Task input snapshots provide another boundary. The committed direct contract test mutates a bound output before admission rather than an input between reads (`tests/contracts/scientific_evidence/test_scientific_context.py:155-176`). Bind every semantically read input to its admitted receipt bytes in the shared contract while retaining those later checks and independent reference-window comparison.

### A01-201 — Step 09 PDF marker checks buffer the complete artifact

Native Step 09 R opens each PDF and reads its full declared size into a raw vector to check the first five signature bytes and an EOF marker in the final 2,048 bytes (`src/emrys/analyses/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking/step_09_cmh_output.R:184-206`). The Python scientific-evidence contract also calls `path.read_bytes()` for those two bounded observations (`src/emrys/contracts/scientific_evidence/step09.py:251-260`). The producer plots tested candidates in its diagnostic PDFs, so larger admitted populations can enlarge this allocation (`src/emrys/analyses/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking/step_09_cmh_output.R:134-181`). No large PDF, peak memory, or time was measured; this is a bounded-read reduction target, not an observed failed Run.

Reporting's separate binary adapter already uses bounded head/tail reads for PDF admission (`src/emrys/reporting/_artifact_index/binary_readers.py:221-242`). That adapter has a different artifact contract, so it is an approach to compare, not automatic shared policy. Preserve the exact Step 09 marker behavior, producer output hashes, receipt binding, and native plotting when replacing whole-file marker reads.

### A01-202 — Empty Python shard selection invokes default discovery

The shard planner accepts any positive shard count and allocates one list per shard, so a count above collected behavioral node IDs leaves empty selections (`tests/tools/python_test_shards.py:56-63,130-166`). `run_shard` records the empty plan, then passes it to `pytest_command`; that command appends no positional node IDs and therefore invokes Pytest's default test discovery (`:199-214,241-285`). The receipt verifier checks deterministic, complete, disjoint *selected* IDs, not actual executed IDs (`:299-357`). Collection explicitly ignores the wheel and shard self-test files (`:20-23,93-98`), but the no-ID execution command has no such ignore flags. An empty shard could thus run default discovery, potentially including those excluded tests, while its receipt claims zero selected tests. This is source inference about a future overprovisioned configuration, not a current CI omission or observed run. The command wrapper passes these arguments to `pytest.main` in the ordinary in-process path (`tests/tools/python_test_shards.py:217-230`).

Direct shard tests use nonempty 3-node/2-shard and 8-node/2-shard plans (`tests/test_python_test_shards.py:47-94,126-168`), while pinned CI configures four shards for a much larger suite (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:70-74,901-911,1023-1034`). Preserve complete/disjoint receipt verification and per-shard success gates. Reject a count that creates empty selections or handle empty shards as explicit no-ops before invoking Pytest, and test the exact command and receipt together.

### A01-203 — Real-engine DAG oracle loses concrete scopes

The pinned Snakefile connects equal-scope processing predecessors using the current wildcard and fans cross-scope predecessors across admitted scopes (`src/emrys/workflow/Snakefile:400-424`). The real Snakemake DAG test parses each node label only through its first line, retaining the owner rule name and discarding sample/partition detail; it then compares aggregate owner counts and rule-name edge pairs (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_workflow.py:128-147,261-339`). A future wrong-sample or wrong-partition edge with the same rule-label multiplicities could satisfy that graph oracle. The profile/Stage Map test compares owner-level direct edges, not concrete scope routing (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_profile.py:271-303`). This is a source-inferred regression-test ceiling; the pinned graph currently routes scopes correctly.

Production materializes each Task's declared input paths, and Task verifies bound inputs before publication (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/materialization.py:985-1059`; `src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/task.py:2788-2800`). The real-engine fixture also checks completed Task records and no-science success (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_workflow.py:474-520`). A wrong scheduling edge may therefore fail at Task admission rather than silently substitute another sample; no bypass, failed Run, or hosted result was observed. Preserve those independent guards and add one asymmetric sample/partition DAG case asserting concrete edge endpoints and declared-input-to-marker mapping.

The completion fixture gives nearly every processing Task the same placeholder input (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/fixtures/workflow.py:937-945`), while its fake producer/validator writes precomputed outputs without reading predecessor output (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/fixtures/task_double.py:87-96`). Thus the 35-job completion assertion cannot independently detect a same-rule wrong-scope edge if graph parsing drops the concrete scope labels. Keep the completion and recovery test for their actual protections. A stronger real-engine wrong-edge claim needs one asymmetric predecessor marker consumed by the downstream fake, in addition to the concrete DAG endpoint oracle.

The public Run owner-double fixture has the related input-to-output ceiling: it builds all processing and analysis output payloads from a fixture projection before execution and replaces each producer/validator command with a payload publisher (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/fixtures/b5_doubles.py:29-70,83-123`; `tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/fixtures/task_double.py:87-96`). Public failure/resume and successor-Run tests use that substitution (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_materialization.py:6951-6959,7303-7324`). They protect real Run state, receipt, source-binding, and recovery paths, but their green output cannot prove native owner computation consumed the declared predecessor's bytes. Task's independent input snapshots and separate native-owner tests retain different coverage (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/materialization.py:985-1059`). This is a source/fixture evidence limit, not a demonstrated Run wrong-edge pass.

### A01-204 — Guarded-R CI does not pin host system-library versions

The guarded-R CI job labels one step “Install locked R system requirements,” then updates apt metadata and installs eight unversioned Ubuntu development/runtime packages on the hosted runner (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:210-251`). The pinned renv lock constrains R packages; the separate Pixi lock applies to managed userspace rather than this apt step (`src/emrys/renv.lock:1-20`; `src/emrys/resources/runtime/pixi.toml:1-24`; `.github/workflows/ci.yml:288-405`). A successful guarded-R job would exercise the host libraries selected at that run, not an exact repository-locked system-library replay. This is a source-evidence wording limit, without a hosted package inventory or observed drift.

Workflow tests pin guarded-R commands and worker selection but not apt version strings (`tests/test_ci_workflow.py:120-134,259-286`); the R environment checker verifies R/Bioconductor and renv package state, not exact host libraries (`scripts/check_r_environment.R:48-125`). Keep the guarded-R real-computation gate and locked language dependencies. Rename or qualify the step's host-library claim unless exact apt versions and their provenance become an explicit supported requirement.

### A01-205 — Direct R restoration admits ambient mutable paths

The direct `make r-restore` target requires a nonempty existing `RENV_PROJECT`, then invokes the guarded profile without setting `RENV_PATHS_LIBRARY` or `RENV_PATHS_CACHE` (`scripts/make_quality.mk:92-100`). Restore mode verifies that the normalized Project is outside the installed EMRYS package, sets state/staging/lockfile paths, and defaults the cache only when the inherited value is empty (`src/emrys/.Rprofile:83-105`). The packaged renv activation reads nonempty `RENV_PATHS_LIBRARY` as the library root and `RENV_PATHS_CACHE` as its cache selector (`src/emrys/renv/activate.R:556-575,969-983,1424-1434`). The vendored activation can create a selected library and install/bootstrap renv before restore begins (`src/emrys/renv/activate.R:223-258,1424-1434`). The restore script then calls `renv::restore` and may hydrate packages into the selected first library without separately rejecting those paths inside the package (`src/emrys/resources/runtime/restore_r_environment.R:77-109`). Thus an external Project alone does not establish that every mutable renv location stays external. This is a source-inferred direct-operator path risk, not a reproduced package write.

The Runbook instructs operators to set external Project, library, and cache paths together (`docs/operations/RUNBOOK.md:736-751`), CI sets all three beneath its temporary root (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:220-226`), and Doctor builds a managed location (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/doctor.py:1223-1232`). The direct shell fixture checks its Make selector and external Project but not inherited library/cache values; its R profile fixture checks project/lock/state/staging, not those two effective paths (`tests/shell/test_local_r_environment.sh:160-188,225-269`). Preserve those documented and managed guards. Decide the direct restore policy, then test the effective renv library/cache locations with an isolated safe fixture before claiming installed-package protection for arbitrary operator environments.

Both orchestration and artifact registry loaders check each schema against Draft 2020-12 and register it under a local identifier, but do not walk every `$ref` to prove the target ID and JSON Pointer resolve (`src/emrys/contracts/orchestration/api.py:208-249`; `src/emrys/contracts/artifacts/_artifact_contracts/schema.py:68-110`). A syntactically valid dangling reference in a branch no fixture reaches could therefore pass the schema gate and fail only when that branch is validated. This is source inference about a future schema edit; no dangling reference in the pinned tree or supported Run failure was identified.

The registry tests require local-looking `urn:emrys:` or `#` prefixes but do not resolve their targets (`tests/contracts/orchestration/test_orchestration_contracts.py:438-459`; `tests/contracts/artifacts/test_artifact_schema_contracts.py:167-190`). Public artifact fixtures exercise many reachable references (`tests/contracts/artifacts/test_artifact_schema_contracts.py:197-205`), and A01-166 separately protects the literal orchestration roster and draft/prefix assertions. Keep those distinct defenses. Check closure of every allowed local reference, including fragment pointers in unexercised branches, at the registry or dedicated schema gate.

This is a source-level collected-case bound, not a measured time saving or evidence of live storage qualification.

### A01-230 — Local R restore gate checks source strings, not failed restoration behavior

The local R environment shell suite requires literal `renv::hydrate`, unresolved-hydration, `renv::status`, and selected-library substrings in the restore script (`tests/shell/test_local_r_environment.sh:125-142`). Hosted guarded-R CI invokes actual restoration and then `r-check` (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:265-286`; `scripts/check_r_environment.R:48-125`), but its positive path does not independently demonstrate the two negative refusals. Preserve explicit operator mutation, guarded computation, and selected-library/lock checks.

### A01-231 — Evidence report tests do not bind input hashes to their displayed rows

The evidence report projects each additional scientific input into a row with label, path, SHA-256, and `content hash` or `file identity` verification method (`src/emrys/reporting/_run_report/context.py:485-495`; `src/emrys/reporting/templates/run_report.html.j2:378-380`). Parse the input table and require one literal reference-FASTA row with an independently computed digest and `file identity`, plus one rehashed source row with `content hash`. Keep receipt-bound true hashes and recheck flags, publication-byte hashes, and rendered-HTML accessibility/section checks (`src/emrys/reporting/_run_report/receipt.py:91-101`; `src/emrys/reporting/_run_report/validation.py:421-457`); they protect different facts and do not establish truthful row pairing.

### A01-232 — Step 00c accepts a name/length projection as an FAI

The Step 00c worker's FAI reader rejects fewer than two fields and projects the first two; its final sidecar comparison uses those name/length pairs (`src/emrys/stages/fasta_sidecars/step_00c_prepare_gatk_reference.sh:57-80,128-150`). The shared Python parser also accepts two fields, and the owner validator reports structure and ordered FASTA/FAI agreement from the projection (`src/emrys/libraries/references/contigs.py:50-57`; `src/emrys/stages/fasta_sidecars/validator.py:65-108`). The parser test explicitly accepts `chr1\t1`; the owner negative fixture rejects a one-field row or wrong length, while its success fixture uses ordinary five-field rows (`tests/libraries/test_reference_contigs.py:30-70`; `tests/stages/fasta_sidecars/test_validate_step_00c_reference_sidecars.py:30-40,83-107`). The later Step 10 scientific-context reader requires exactly five FAI fields and checks offset and line geometry against FASTA bytes (`src/emrys/contracts/scientific_evidence/scientific_context.py:999-1028,1094-1116`). The direct Step 05 success fixture has five-field FAI rows; its negatives change a length or corrupt all sidecars, rather than isolating missing offset/geometry fields (`tests/stages/split_n_cigar/test_validate_step_05_split_ncigar.py:36-47,137-159`). A tiny local direct-helper fixture with `>chr1`/`ACGT` FASTA, `chr1\t4` FAI, and matching DICT returned `(True, "FASTA=1 FAI=1 DICT=1")` from `_inspect_reference_sidecars`; it did not invoke the full validator CLI or GATK. This establishes the Step 05 helper's narrow pass, not a native-tool result or a completed Run accepting unusable index data. ### A01-233 — Shared publisher rollback test does not prove final-byte reinspection

The shared publisher validates staged bytes, any valid predecessor, then rereads and validates the installed final after replacement (`src/emrys/libraries/validation/publication.py:30-61`). The postpublication failure test raises on validator call three without inspecting its byte argument (`tests/libraries/test_validation_report.py:455-478`). Add a fixture that changes the bytes of the just-installed **owned final inode** immediately after the staged-to-final move, lets the real validator run, and checks invalid-report refusal, restoration of the valid predecessor, and no owned residue. Owner validators publish through this shared route (`src/emrys/libraries/validation/runtime.py:27-49,77-95`); Run separately requires successful validation and a stable report reference (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/task.py:2763-2807`). This is a source-inferred test-oracle gap, not a present publisher defect or a demonstrated Run bypass. Retain the exception-triggered rollback and recovery-characterization tests.

### A01-234 — Shared descriptor-reader faults are multiplied across output modes

Four parametrized negative tests inject open failure, in-place mutation, post-open pathname replacement, and final pathname-metadata change into each of three thin readers, producing 12 collected cases (`tests/libraries/test_validation_inputs.py:25-44,210-312`). This is a source-level case-count proposal, not a measured runtime or CI saving.

### A01-235 — Bogus-only Workflow Attempt definition does not test retained-reference closure

The current schema is closed; this identifies a test-regression gap, not a presently accepted malformed Attempt or Slurm evidence.

### A01-237 — Compound early-stage faults do not prove selective check outcomes

The Step 00a negative fixture changes a STAR contig length and three integer parameters in one invocation, then checks that all four report rows fail (`tests/stages/star_index/test_validate_step_00a_star_index.py:153-169`). The shared flagstat parser accepts only nonnegative ASCII integer pairs and records parsing errors for malformed or duplicate required rows (`src/emrys/libraries/evidence/qc.py:8-33`). The full missing-artifact Run-summary test asserts `summary_state`, a missing-artifact count, a missing record, an incomplete or missing scope, and analysis-policy binding, but never inspects `document["limitations"]` (`tests/reporting/test_artifact_run_summary.py:174-195`). Thus a future assembly change that omits the helper could leave both tests green while a missing required artifact has no published limitation. An empty limitation list may still be valid for a complete artifact set; this is a source-inferred oracle gap, not a present publication defect.

### A01-243 — Attempt task-tree enumeration errors escape structured inspection

`inspect_attempt_task_trees` enumerates the Attempt's `tasks` root and each owner directory outside the per-scope `try` that converts state errors into blockers (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/_inspection_attempts.py:366-467`). An `OSError` at either `iterdir()` escapes the inspection result. `inspect_evidence` and `inspect_run` call this scan (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/_inspection_evidence.py:428-436`; `src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/inspection.py:491-503`). Public inspection catches `OSError` as a controlled command failure, and resume admission wraps it as `ControlError`, so there is no source evidence of unsafe continuation (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/control.py:430-474,2802-2825`). During execution, the same scan occurs after Attempt publication and again after the child (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/lifecycle.py:1885-1909,2020-2044`). If it raises after the Attempt exists, the outer failure path preserves the owned lock rather than publishing a terminal receipt (`:2416-2432`). That conservative state may be correct under uncertain shared storage, but its recovery behavior is not characterized by direct tests.

The nearby fixtures cover missing, symlinked, foreign, and malformed task state, not directory enumeration I/O failure (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_lifecycle.py:3299-3359,3695-3750`; `tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_materialization.py:8036-8123`). Inject a root and owner `iterdir()` fault, assert controlled public refusal and exact lock/receipt preservation after Attempt publication, then test the supported recovery route once enumeration becomes available. Decide separately whether a structured blocker can be returned without misrepresenting incomplete evidence; do not publish success or discard the lock merely to normalize an I/O error. This is a source-inferred recovery-oracle gap, not an observed Slurm or storage failure.

### A01-244 — Step 09 rerun test compares logical lines while claiming exact bytes

The guarded-R Step 09 fixture reruns the same core input to separate output paths, comments that four deterministic TSVs must be `byte-identical`, then compares `readLines(..., warn = FALSE)` vectors for all-sites, significant-sites, summary, and mutation-spectrum outputs (`tests/analyses/paired_cmh_candidate_ranking/test_step_09_cmh_editing_site_calling.R:794-803`). The local probe establishes the test's parsing ceiling, not Step 09 nondeterminism, a native Run failure, or a hosted CI result.

### A01-245 — Motif position-profile test does not distinguish population denominators

The test named `test_position_profiles_use_each_populations_analyzable_denominator` supplies two position bins with denominator 20 in both rows. Add two explicit helper inputs with the same hit count and different denominators, such as 2/10 = 20% and 2/20 = 10%, and bind at least one asymmetric population's value to the outer plot or legend oracle. Preserve Step 10's semantic count reconciliation (`src/emrys/contracts/scientific_evidence/scientific_context.py:722-765`), figure-byte/status tests, and report receipt checks; none of those currently establishes the population-to-y-value mapping. This is a source-inferred regression-oracle gap, not a demonstrated wrong figure, browser rendering result, or biological interpretation.

### A01-246 — Nested samtools indexing allowances are not checked by fake workers

The Step 02, Step 04, and Step 05 owner contracts each specify `threads - 1` additional samtools indexing workers (`src/emrys/stages/canonical_bam/CONTRACT.md:61-65`; `src/emrys/stages/duplicate_marking/CONTRACT.md:43-46`; `src/emrys/stages/split_n_cigar/CONTRACT.md:45-48`). Their workers currently pass `index -@ "$((threads - 1))"` (`src/emrys/stages/canonical_bam/step_02_sort_index_bam.sh:130`; `src/emrys/stages/duplicate_marking/step_04_mark_duplicates.sh:69-72`; `src/emrys/stages/split_n_cigar/step_05_split_n_cigar_reads.sh:125-127`). Step 04/05 tests check the 16-thread Java/GATK allowance, and Step 02 checks sorting memory, but none binds the recorded nested index argv to 15 or one additional worker in the matching success route (`tests/stages/canonical_bam/test_step_02_sort_index_bam.sh:181-201`; `tests/stages/duplicate_marking/test_step_04_mark_duplicates.sh:149-164`; `tests/stages/split_n_cigar/test_step_05_split_n_cigar_reads.sh:223-241`).

A future change to `-@ 16`, `-@ 0`, or no `-@` could leave the fake suites green while changing the declared resource contract. Run materialization supplies the worker `--threads` allowance, and Task records the outer command and outputs, not the nested samtools argv (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/materialization.py:602-647`; `src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/task.py:2600-2616`). Add one literal indexed-command assertion per distinct worker success path, using the existing fake logs, while retaining Java/GATK, sort-memory, native failure, BAI/output, validator, and Task checks. This is a source-inferred oracle gap; no native indexing, measured oversubscription, CI, or Slurm work was performed.

### A01-248 — Direct R restore can mutate before rejecting the wrong R

The explicit `make r-restore` route checks that `RENV_PROJECT` is set and invokes `Rscript` with the installed `.Rprofile` (`scripts/make_quality.mk:92-100`). If renv is absent, activation can create a project library and bootstrap renv (`src/emrys/renv/activate.R:223-258,1232-1264,1421-1434`). Consequently an operator selecting a wrong R may reach external writes, and possibly a package bootstrap, before receiving the version refusal. This is a source-inferred ordering risk; no wrong-version restore or installation was executed.

The shell fixtures cover profile selection with a fake activation under a suitable R, script argument refusals with the profile disabled, and Make environment forwarding through a fake `Rscript` (`tests/shell/test_local_r_environment.sh:160-184,189-220,225-258`). They do not assert that wrong-R startup leaves the external Project unchanged. Put the version check before settings creation and activation, then add a tiny isolated wrong-R no-mutation test. Preserve the external-Project containment, lock/settings identity, managed Doctor route, and post-restore synchronization checks (`src/emrys/.Rprofile:82-121`; `src/emrys/resources/runtime/restore_r_environment.R:77-129`). A01-205 separately concerns ambient mutable path selection even with the correct R.

### A01-250 — A blocked subset fixture does not test successful-receipt completeness

An existing pre-entry failed-Attempt harness can supply pending Tasks (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_lifecycle.py:2619-2631`); a schema-valid forged `succeeded` receipt with unchanged cumulative arrays would isolate the completeness guard. ### A01-255 — Documentation heading and anchor checks use different grammars

The documentation gate uses CommonMark parsing to find a canonical page's first H1 (`scripts/documentation/validate_structure.py:120-125`). A valid Setext heading can satisfy the H1 gate while a link to its anchor is refused; conversely, an ATX-looking line inside a tilde-fenced code block can be treated as an anchor and make a broken link appear valid. The local-link stage still checks target existence and repository-root containment (`scripts/documentation/validate_structure.py:164-196`).

The fixture builder uses ATX headings, and the local-link tests cover missing targets and anchors without Setext or tilde-fence cases (`tests/documentation/test_validate_structure.py:111-142,290-323`). Derive anchors from the parsed Markdown heading tokens and pin both syntax cases, preserving the H1, path, root, and Mermaid defenses. This is source inference about accepted Markdown syntax, not an executed gate failure or proof of a broken current repository link. The architecture diagram files remain linked and non-authoritative (`docs/architecture/diagrams/README.md:6-15`); this finding does not clear them for retirement.
