Three pairs can cover the less-than, equal-to, and greater-than CPU relations while including both memory values.

This is only a source-level case reduction candidate, not a measured CI saving. Retain each distinct CPU relation, both memory scales, the no-declaration source text, and the separate cgroup, full-node, per-node, and per-CPU memory cases (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_capacity.py:162-219,242-275`). A01-262 needs an added leaf-tighter oracle before either case-count reduction is considered.

### A01-264 — Step 04 fake does not bind selected Java and Picard paths

The Step 04 worker checks its supplied Picard jar and Java executable, then calls the selected Java with `-jar "$picard_jar"` (`src/emrys/stages/duplicate_marking/step_04_mark_duplicates.sh:53-68`). Its fake checks the `-jar` position and `MarkDuplicates` position but ignores the intervening jar operand (`tests/stages/duplicate_marking/test_step_04_mark_duplicates.sh:25-40`). The fixture also prepends that same fake directory to `PATH` while supplying the fake by absolute `--java-bin` path (`tests/stages/duplicate_marking/test_step_04_mark_duplicates.sh:8-12,141-164`). Calling ambient `java` instead of the selected executable, or forwarding a different readable jar, could leave the worker test green.

Make ambient `PATH` Java distinguishable from the selected fake and assert the exact jar operand from the successful worker call. Run materialization supplies and snapshots the selected jar and passes both tool arguments (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/materialization.py:602-627`); runtime availability separately probes selected tools (`tests/evidence/runtime_availability/test_runtime_availability.py:666-700`). Preserve those boundary checks and the worker's resource, output, quickcheck, and failure assertions. This is a source-inferred forwarding gap, distinct from A01-152's input BAM binding, without a real Picard run or observed misroute.

### A01-265 — Step 07 absence tests stop at the first orientation pair

The producer constructs every sample's FWD and REV BAM path and checks each BAM and index before the bcftools pipelines (`src/emrys/stages/partitioned_cohort_mpileup/producer.py:229-241`). The direct fixture roster orders two samples and two orientations (`tests/stages/partitioned_cohort_mpileup/test_partitioned_cohort_mpileup_producer.py:141-154`), but its missing-input test removes only indices 4 and 5, the first sample's FWD BAM/index (`tests/stages/partitioned_cohort_mpileup/test_partitioned_cohort_mpileup_producer.py:425-431`). Its fake bcftools infers orientation from argument names without opening BAM bytes (`tests/stages/partitioned_cohort_mpileup/test_partitioned_cohort_mpileup_producer.py:34-47`). A future producer loop or index-check regression limited to a later member could evade these negatives.

Add a sample-A REV BAM and sample-B REV index absence case, retaining the first-pair refusals and existing command/order and publication checks. Normal Run declares and snapshots every orientation input (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/materialization.py:695-754`; `src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/task.py:2600-2616`), and the Step 07 validator separately checks output VCF/receipt semantics. This is a direct worker-suite oracle gap, not a demonstrated Run bypass or native bcftools failure.

### A01-266 — Doctor timing faults are crossed with two profile states

The Doctor repair fixture crosses five timing/probe outcomes, including ordinary success, with retained versus absent runtime inventory, collecting ten cases (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_doctor.py:1887-1897`). Profile publication or exact retained-byte admission occurs before project readiness and timing flush (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/doctor.py:1862-1914`); the test injects timing and probe event-write faults through one callback (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_doctor.py:1997-2024`). The current case product repeats every event fault after both profile decisions.

An eight-case candidate would keep both ordinary profile states, all four event faults under one state, and a retained-profile write and interruption under the other. Compare the chosen cases against each manager invocation, profile byte/inode, lock release, log close, timing boundary, and interruption assertion (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_doctor.py:2027-2119`). If any profile-specific fault interaction is required, retain that case. This is a provisional reduction with no measured runtime saving; it does not weaken Doctor repair authority or establish a live managed-runtime result.

### A01-267 — Changed Run-summary test stops at path identity

The test named `test_prepared_handoff_rejects_a_changed_run_summary` copies a summary beneath a temporary input root and removes `analysis_policy` from the copy (`tests/reporting/test_report.py:144-160,512-527`). The report context snapshots the supplied path and calls `recheck_evidence_context` before processing the prepared document (`src/emrys/reporting/_run_report/context.py:395-404`). That recheck uses a short-circuit `or`: because the copy's path differs from the prepared summary path, it raises `Published Run result differs` without calling the byte reader (`:361-377`). A separate test already supplies an unchanged copy at a wrong path and expects the same refusal (`tests/reporting/test_report.py:1294-1305`).

Replace the changed-copy case with a same-path, post-preparation byte mutation and assert the specific handoff refusal. Retain one wrong-path case and the later publication-time changed-input case (`tests/reporting/test_report.py:1370-1380`); they protect different times. The current production byte comparison remains present. This is a source-inferred oracle gap and possible one-case duplication, not an observed report bypass or a measured saving.

### A01-268 — One malformed workflow result does not exercise every guard

The lifecycle accepts a typed `WorkflowResult` only when exactly one of exit code and termination signal is present and its value has the required integer type and range (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/lifecycle.py:1946-1972`). The named malformed-result test supplies exit 9 and signal 15 together, then checks that a blocked receipt records an invalid observation (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_lifecycle.py:2941-2955`). It reaches the mutual-exclusion arm before the later type and range conditions. No direct case in this pass isolated neither outcome, a Boolean or other wrong type, an exit outside 0–255, or a signal below 1.

Add a small one-fault-per-arm matrix with the same blocked-receipt and lock assertions. Preserve clean failure and signal handling tests (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_lifecycle.py:2511-2540`) and the separate receipt schema and semantic range checks (`src/emrys/contracts/schemas/orchestration/v2/attempt_receipt.schema.json:58-80`; `src/emrys/contracts/orchestration/api.py:621-645`). Those later checks do not prove the lifecycle converts every malformed child observation into controlled blocked evidence. No malformed outcome was executed in this audit.

### A01-269 — Malformed-start cases repeat the same early refusal

The terminal Task observation test crosses `recorded_status` failed/succeeded with `malformed_start` false/true for four fixtures (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_lifecycle.py:2662-2671`). When start is malformed, task-start admission refuses it before the terminal is admitted (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/_inspection_attempts.py:405-444`); both cases assert pending state, no terminal record, and no mutation. The test reads and prints the recorded failed/succeeded status only in the valid-start branch (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_lifecycle.py:2724-2745,2787-2813`).

Compare three cases: both statuses with a valid start and one malformed start. Retain the verbose/plain diagnostic assertions, blocked-results outcome, file snapshot, and separate valid-start terminal references. If a status-dependent effect before start admission is found, retain the fourth case. This is a narrow test-case reduction candidate with no measured time saving or change to inspection policy.

### A01-270 — Shard receipt can be exact while execution includes extra tests

The sharder writes its receipt from `selected.nodeids` before constructing the pytest command; the current command then appends exactly those IDs (`tests/tools/python_test_shards.py:199-214,253-285`). The focused runner test checks only `set(expected).issubset(observed[1])` (`tests/test_python_test_shards.py:47-94`). A future command builder that adds another positional node ID could leave this test green. The receipt verifier checks the declared complete/disjoint plan, not the command that executed (`tests/tools/python_test_shards.py:299-357`); CI uploads that receipt and separately requires each shard to succeed (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:922-946,974-981`).

Pin exact positional test IDs in the captured command against the receipt, with options accounted for separately. Retain deterministic planning, receipt verification, and the shard-success gate. This is distinct from A01-202's empty-selection default-discovery path: here a nonempty shard could run additional or duplicate work while declaring an exact selection. The current builder is exact; no CI omission or extra execution was observed.

### A01-271 — Task-start origin test mutates only the Attempt

`validate_task_start` reloads and compares the execution/profile contract, checks bound early inputs, admits the retained Attempt and its run-lock evidence, and compares the start's references (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/task.py:1948-2013`; `:1804-1833,1861-1910`). Its direct test named `rechecks_every_origin` first admits a valid start, then appends bytes only to the Attempt manifest and expects a SHA-256 refusal (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_task.py:2692-2723`). The traced direct calls do not isolate a post-entry profile change or an active/released run-lock change at this reader. Profile and lock mutations in workflow or live inspection suites occur at different admission boundaries.

Add one-at-a-time profile and run-lock origin changes after Task entry, asserting controlled refusal and unchanged evidence. Keep the Attempt manifest case, whole-Run identity checks, and inspection tests; those layers have different timing and authority. This is a regression-oracle gap in a currently guarded reader, not a demonstrated supported-Run false pass.

### A01-272 — Invalid JSON masks two reference-digest checks

The direct verified-Task test appends `mutated-after-publication` to each of four referenced paths and asserts only `TaskBoundaryError` (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_task.py:2793-2813`). For the Task Attempt and Task start JSON records, that suffix makes the file syntactically invalid. Parser refusal can therefore keep the test green if the later marker-to-Attempt or Attempt-to-start digest comparison is removed. Those links are checked by `_admit_attempt_reference` and `_verify_reference` after record admission (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/task.py:2074-2089,2148-2164,2242-2253`).

For each of the two JSON links, make a separately admitted, schema- and semantics-valid byte change while leaving its parent reference digest unchanged; assert a digest-specific refusal. Retain the malformed-record cases and the validation-report mutation, whose digest is checked before report parsing (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/task.py:2330-2346`). This source inference concerns proof of the reference links, not a current production acceptance of changed records.

### A01-273 — Literal artifact schemas are not bound to the selected registry

The independent artifact golden loads four fixed paths by filename and version (`tests/contract_integration/independent_contract_goldens/test_independent_contract_goldens.py:82-103`), then checks literal JSON Pointers in those documents (`:92-103,178-190`). Production selects current artifact schemas through `SCHEMA_FILES` and `COMMON_SCHEMA_PATH` (`src/emrys/contracts/artifacts/_artifact_contracts/definitions.py:10-17`; `src/emrys/contracts/artifacts/_artifact_contracts/schema.py:68-98`). A future registry redirect could leave the golden green on an unused file. The direct registry test verifies selected schemas are valid and checks the report-receipt ID/version, but does not bind every selected file to the independently reviewed golden (`tests/contracts/artifacts/test_artifact_schema_contracts.py:167-205`).

The pinned registry currently names the golden's files; no current schema mismatch was found. Assert the selected path map or apply the literal pointer expectations to the selected documents, while keeping registry validation, direct fixture admission, independent literals, and versioned schema retention. This narrows A01-16's description of what the golden proves; it does not make a whole schema obsolete.

### A01-274 — QC human-table header has no independent ordered oracle

The Run-summary producer writes the human QC TSV with `QC_SUMMARY_HEADER` (`src/emrys/reporting/_run_summary/models.py:36-51`; `src/emrys/reporting/_run_summary/document.py:94-108`). Its direct publication test compares the output header with that same imported production constant (`tests/reporting/test_artifact_run_summary.py:87-105`). The independent literal header map pins `RUN_SUMMARY_HEADER` but omits `QC_SUMMARY_HEADER` (`tests/contract_integration/independent_contract_goldens/headers.json:13-35`; `tests/contract_integration/independent_contract_goldens/test_independent_contract_goldens.py:106-115,194-215`). A coordinated writer/constant change could therefore pass the direct header assertion and golden.

Add the current ordered QC header to the literal map, preserving the direct publication, canonical bytes and digest checks, and metric-value cases. A01-131 concerns cell mapping rather than column identity. This is a future-regression oracle gap, not an observed malformed QC table.

### A01-275 — R ignore gate tests text rather than effective Git rules

The local R environment shell test searches `.gitignore` for four substrings (`tests/shell/test_local_r_environment.sh:77-80`). Current root rules actively ignore the matching cache, library, and staging trees (`.gitignore:35-43`), and the packaged renv directory has its own ignore rules (`src/emrys/renv/.gitignore:1-8`). Yet a commented matching line or later negation could satisfy the text search while making representative generated files stageable. This is an inferred false pass of a static protection gate, not a current staging leak.

Check representative generated paths with Git's effective ignore matcher, including the intended nested owner path. Retain the shell suite's distinct lock, startup, and explicit-restore checks; an ignore gate does not replace review of forced additions. A01-83 addresses missing scientific derivative suffixes at a different boundary.

### A01-276 — Doctor's manager failure fixture stops before R restoration

Doctor's managed runtime repair loops over a Pixi native install and a Pixi-managed R restoration (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/doctor.py:1198-1254,1750-1781`). The positive test observes two manager invocations (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_doctor.py:2034-2040`). The negative failure/interruption fixture makes its only manager call fail or interrupt and asserts exactly one call (`:2309-2352`). It therefore does not isolate failure of the second action after native state has changed. The same production failure handler is used by the loop, so this is a partial-state test gap, not evidence of a current repair defect.

Add a second-action failure and interruption case with the first action succeeding, then assert no profile publication, retained maintenance claim and logs, and the required recovery boundary. Keep the first-action, successful two-action, and final requalification cases; never run an actual package manager as part of this audit.

### A01-277 — Wheel dependency map hides duplicate metadata entries

`declared_requirements` turns a `Requires-Dist` list into a dict keyed by normalized package name (`tests/test_package_distribution.py:180-189`). Wheel inspection checks the key set and exact name/specifier map after that collapse (`:199-222`); the installed probe also compares a collapsed map (`:341-358`). Two identical metadata entries for one dependency would satisfy both checks. A01-216's proposed removal of the redundant set assertion remains sound, but the surviving map is not an exact *entry* roster.

If the intended wheel contract is one requirement entry per dependency, assert the raw metadata count or explicit uniqueness before normalizing, then keep exact specifiers and isolated install/resource checks. No duplicate is observed in the pinned wheel source or a built wheel in this pass; this is a future false-pass concern, not evidence that installed dependencies are wrong.

### A01-278 — Step 00a's extra-member worker guard has no direct fault case

The Step 00a contract allows additional native STAR index files but requires every generated member to be regular (`src/emrys/stages/star_index/CONTRACT.md:69-73`). After verifying the 15 required members, the worker rejects any extra top-level symlink or directory (`src/emrys/stages/star_index/step_00a_build_star_index.sh:84-96`). Its fake STAR emits exactly the required regular files or an incomplete index, and the direct tests assert those outcomes (`tests/stages/star_index/test_step_00a_build_star_index.py:59-75,151-196`). Removing the worker's additional-member loop could leave those tests green.

Add a small fake-output case that admits one extra regular file and refuses one extra symlink or directory. Keep the required-member and native command assertions. The Run coordinator independently enumerates and snapshots staged extras before publication (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/task.py:1584-1624`); its separate boundary does not turn the missing direct worker oracle into a demonstrated full-Run bypass. No STAR or full Run was executed for this finding.

### A01-279 — Engineering guide overstates the no-install rule

The engineering guide says computation, validators, renderers, and tests never install packages or change locks (`docs/operations/ENGINEERING_CONVENTIONS.md:44-54`). The wheel smoke instead builds a temporary installer Project, runs `uv lock` there, and runs `uv sync --offline` to install the wheel and locked dependencies (`tests/test_package_distribution.py:250-308,341-346`). Ordinary CI selects that test through the static/wheel job (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:164-186`). The test is an intentional installed-distribution proof in disposable state; this observation says nothing about product computation installing dependencies.

Narrow the guide's statement to runtime compute, validation, rendering, and scheduler code, and name the isolated wheel-test exception. Preserve the wheel smoke, its installed-resource/command checks, and Doctor's separate explicit repair authority. This is a documentation accuracy finding, not an instruction to remove the test or change the product boundary.

## Protections to preserve during the next pass

The initial review found no basis for blanket retirement of independent numerical oracles, literal headers and check rosters, transaction and recovery fault tests, coverage baselines, installed-wheel smoke, guarded R and native-runtime checks, or disposable Slurm evidence. Similar test names across owners do not establish equivalent inputs or faults. Coverage measures Python execution, not scientific correctness or independent expectations.

The documentation gate's 19 fixed filenames preserve assigned reader and authority routes (`scripts/documentation/validate_structure.py:14-34`; `docs/README.md:8-29`). Link validation catches a missing destination only while an inbound link remains; deleting a page and its links together would evade that check (`scripts/documentation/validate_structure.py:164-196`). The fixed-name check therefore has a distinct maintenance fault, and the H1 check catches a present but headingless canonical page (`:199-206`). Retain both. The parser accepts any nonempty H1 anywhere, regardless of title or position (`:120-126`; `tests/documentation/test_validate_structure.py:158-176`); the guide's phrase "first headings" could imply a required opening heading, while the gate checks for an H1 anywhere (`scripts/documentation/README.md:3-6`). No fixed-title or first-line gate has a demonstrated need. A01-125/126/175 concern test exits, snapshot scope, and source-location admission rather than reasons to remove the named-page or H1 defenses.

An apparently duplicated Step 08 API check needs caution: its SHA-256 fingerprint includes the `Table` fields and `ContractError` ancestry, but the following explicit literal assertions protect those two invariants if someone deliberately rebaselines the opaque digest (`tests/contracts/scientific_evidence/test_step08.py:195-240`). The separate `__all__` roster protects export membership (`:242-270`); neither is a whole-test retirement. Similarly, missing `genome_chr_bin_nbits` remains an admitted current Project form (`src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/CONTRACT.md:129-134`; `tests/contracts/orchestration/test_orchestration_contracts.py:527-539`), and old-looking submission tests protect retained-request diagnostics (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_submission_inspection.py:544-563`; `tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_slurm_submission.py:1173-1204`). Historical names alone are not grounds for deletion.

The ingestion owner pass found no whole-test retirement: standalone manifest and FASTQ commands remain supported advisory routes, and their malformed inputs, optional file checks, prefix ceiling, decompression, and no-write assertions protect different decisions (`src/emrys/ingestion/sample_manifest_admission/validator.py:37-60,64-190`; `src/emrys/ingestion/sample_manifest_admission/check_fastq_pairs.sh:39-57,91-170`; `tests/ingestion/sample_manifest_admission/test_validate_manifest.py:48-263`; `tests/ingestion/sample_manifest_admission/test_check_fastq_pairs.py:85-346`). The ten packaged R namespace probes protect selected-library and version admission (`src/emrys/resources/runtime/runtime_policy.tsv:18-27`; `src/emrys/orchestration/run_coordinator/doctor.py:586-613`); this source pass found no basis to retire them. These are source and test dispositions, not local R or Viking qualification.

The workflow's literal processing-owner/rule/scope fence (`src/emrys/workflow/Snakefile:157-184`) and the profile-to-Stage Map/artifact-template tests (`tests/orchestration/run_coordinator/test_profile.py:271-306`) protect different graph representations. Real Snakemake fixtures use test-owned no-science commands, so their scheduling proof remains distinct from scientific results. R restoration runs only in explicit operator mode (`src/emrys/.Rprofile:76-125`; `src/emrys/resources/runtime/restore_r_environment.R:5-68`); guarded computation selects an existing library (`src/emrys/.Rprofile:27-75`). Preserve both mode boundaries and do not promote local restoration checks to Viking qualification.
